## 1. System Overview

The `architect-team` Claude Code plugin (current: v4.1.0 — quiet-holds-and-the-users-release, on the v4.0.0 claude-5-modernization MAJOR) is a spec-to-production multi-agent coding pipeline. It accepts EITHER a requirements folder (OpenSpec, Superpowers, or plain markdown) OR a plain-language requirement typed directly as prose (v0.9.17), and drives it end-to-end through a Phase −2 → 8 sequence: **Phase −2** (Triage & Routing, v0.9.22 — the `bug-classifier` agent dispatches the requirement as `bug` / `feature` / `data-eng` / `mixed` / `unclear` (the fifth `data-eng` kind added in v3.50.0 routes to the `data-eng-pipeline` sibling lane); pure-bug routes to the sibling `bug-fix-pipeline` skill, pure-feature continues to the existing flow, `mixed` spawns BOTH in parallel with a `triage_done` recursion-prevention flag, `unclear` emits a structured question), intake & mapping (Phase −1, with the v0.9.21 sub-section D producing per-frontend `INTERACTION_INTUITION_MAP.md` and firing a bulk-verify gate), detection & normalization (Phase 0), the 100%-coverage planning-validation gate (Phase 1), parallel team decomposition & spawn (Phase 2), hook-enforced per-team review gates (Phase 3), continuous solution-requirement intake (Phase 3b), reconciliation (Phase 4), cross-layer integration (Phase 5), the outer task-group loop (Phase 6), master review (Phase 7), and the final report + auto-commit (Phase 8). The v0.9.20 `## Default mode of operation` rule (gates are opt-in for *process* gates) and the v0.9.21 *domain-gate* carve-out coexist in the same section. **v0.9.23 promoted the Phase 8 documentation-currency update step from "orchestrator performs the updates" to a dedicated `doc-updater` agent** (`sonnet` under the v4.0.0 role split — `fable` before it; bounded `Write` only to the inventory paths, NO `Edit`) — wired into BOTH the main pipeline Phase 8 AND the bug-fix-pipeline Phase B8, so doc currency is structurally automatic for both feature work and bug fixes.

**v0.9.22 ships a sibling `bug-fix-pipeline` skill** with phases B−1 → B8 — replicate-first (Playwright for frontend / backend script for backend / ambiguity-escalation when unclear); reproduction-is-the-regression-test (frontend bugs also author a backend diagnostic); generalized-fix (the `system-architect` Bug-Fix Generalization Audit mode rejects symptom patches unless explicitly authorized); QA-replay-against-live-dev (the `qa-replayer` agent re-runs the reproduction artifacts against the deployed dev fix; pass criterion is symptom-gone-end-to-end); live-dev-environment-by-default (production is an opt-in escalation). Reached via `/architect-team:bug-fix` (explicit) OR the main pipeline's Phase −2 triage. **At Phase B8 the bug-fix pipeline runs the same documentation-currency gate as the main pipeline** (v0.9.23 dispatch parity).

**v4.0.0 (claude-5-modernization) changed where the rules live, not what they are.** The constitution is `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/SKILL.md` (41,510 bytes at the v4.1.2 cut, 64,873 at v4.1.0, from 258,527): the ranked Read-this-first rules (v3.66.0), the compiled operating principles and one STOP-pointer stub per discipline (heading + pointer + first paragraph) stay inline; every discipline's full text is a chapter under `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/` (50 chapters) and `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/INDEX.md` maps each phase to the chapters it must Read first — depth capped at constitution → index → chapter, and a chapter never names another chapter as a prerequisite. The heavy lane / driver skills (`architect-team-pipeline` 153 KB → 46 KB with per-phase chapters, `bug-fix-pipeline`, `mini-architect-team-pipeline`, `team-spawning-and-review-gates`, `ux-test-builder`, `data-eng-pipeline`, `playwright-user-flows`, `verified-agent-output`, `visual-to-api-design`) carry the same shape with their own INDEX under `references/`. Every agent preloads `skills/ct6-agent-core` — the five canonical boilerplate blocks (operating context, forbidden git operations, checkpoint discipline, operating principles, reporting and escalation) rendered by `scripts/setup/compile_skills.py` from `scripts/setup/agent_boilerplate_blocks.py` — through its `skills:` frontmatter, so no agent body carries a compiled block (`scripts/setup/sync_agent_boilerplate.py --check` guards the inverse). Models follow roles (`scripts/setup/set_default_model.py --split role`); reviewer counts follow `skills/team-spawning-and-review-gates/references/review-tiers.md` (three reviewers + synthesis ONLY for architecture approval, the Phase 7 master review, map convergence, the research panels and the Phase 7 editability / interaction sweeps; the producer + one paired reviewer for every Phase 3 / 5 slice gate — one editability / interaction reviewer, one `flow-explorer` / `flow-executor` / `visual-analyzer` / `reference-tracer` per slice by default, three only when the gate is tier 3; a tool or a `prompt` hook for classification and shape checks); and every "N reviewers loop until all ok" gate runs as code in `workflows/ct6-converge.js` (parallel reviewers with typed verdicts, one fixer per round, a human-only `maxRounds`, silence never read as ok) — `ralph-loop` is retired from every surface. The deterministic half of that loop is `scripts/converge/record.py` (v4.1.0): `declare` appends a `converge:<kind>` gate to `.architect-team/declared-gates.json` BEFORE the loop, the workflow's result carries a `ct6-converge/v1` `record` block, and `record` writes it under `.architect-team/converge/` and satisfies the gate ONLY when every reviewer returned `ok` in the final round — the Stop arm `_audit_declared_gates` holds the run until then (the hold ralph-loop provided, enforced from disk rather than from a typed phrase). A held Stop is hook JSON on stdout with exit 0 — `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` carries the directive, shown only as transcript `Stop hook feedback` (v4.1.0; `CT6_STOP_HOLD_MODE=block` restores `decision: block`, `CT6_STOP_USER_NOTICE=1` adds a one-line `systemMessage`) — and releasing a hold is the USER's verb: `/architect-team:unlock <why>` runs `hooks/completion_unlock.py release` under one durable record in `.architect-team/releases/`, sanctioned only inside the user's own command turn (Arm 5 of `hooks/pretool_skill_gate.py`); the agent finishes or reports a held turn, it never releases it. `superpowers` and `cartographer` are CONDITIONAL plugins (`CONDITIONAL_PLUGINS` beside an EMPTY `REQUIRED_PLUGINS`: preferred when installed, reported and remediated when absent, never gating; the lanes record `methodology: native` and follow `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/native-methodology.md`). Because a Claude 5 session ships without `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate` unless `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` is set, `setup.py` writes that flag with the same consent flow as the Agent Teams flag, the SessionStart hook warns when it is absent, and the completion lock reads both harness task-dir layouts (`~/.claude/tasks/<full session id>/` and the older `session-<first 8>/`) and degrades honestly — a missing DEFAULT task root is an advisory `unavailable` source the lock names, a missing REDIRECTED root still blocks. Two new hooks join the seven: `hooks/deletion_guard.py` (PreToolUse[Bash] — recoverability is the classifier, `trash` is the soft-delete default) and `hooks/timings_log.py` (agent / task / phase / run intervals to `run-history/timings.jsonl`; observability, never a gate). New engines: `scripts/principles/` (codified project principles, `/architect-team:principles`, Layer-3 `verify-principles-respected`, the optional `principles_review` evidence field), `scripts/test_index/` (the test index + impact selector — the selected tests while building, the full suite ONCE at Phase 8, reversing the v3.65.0 whole-dev-suite-at-Phase-5 rule on purpose), `scripts/data_dictionary/sampling.py` (sample-first data iteration; `--full-data` opts out), `scripts/publish/` (private-URL page publishing behind the human-owned `.architect-team-publish.json`, `/architect-team:publish`; default mode `claude`), `scripts/homepage/` (the per-project homepage + timing statistics, `/architect-team:home`), `hooks/decisions_ledger.py` (the append-only decisions ledger), and `scripts/measure/instruction_budget.py` (the instruction weight, RECORDED under `docs/measurements/` — lane start ~45K tokens from ~141K, not yet at the 25K target). `user-invocable: false` hides the 57 internal skills from the `/` menu (their descriptions are still loaded); `disable-model-invocation` is deliberately NOT set on the lane skills; the hooks stay global in `hooks/hooks.json`. The CHANGELOG now carries the ten most recent releases, the rest moved verbatim to `docs/CHANGELOG_ARCHIVE.md`.

As of **v4.1.0** the plugin ships **60 skills + the v2.3.0 phenotype subsystem (now FOUR seeded phenotypes: `user-management`, `config-management`, `ai-management`, `code-wiki`), 39 named agent definitions (each preloading `skills/ct6-agent-core` through its `skills:` frontmatter), 30 slash commands (v4.1.0 adds `unlock`), 9 enforcement hook scripts (across 10 wired hook events — v4.0.0 adds `PreToolUse[Bash]` → `deletion_guard.py` and `SubagentStart` / `TaskCreated` → `timings_log.py`, which also rides `SubagentStop` / `TaskCompleted` / `Stop`; v3.30.0 added the `SessionStart` + `PostToolUse(Skill)` wirings) + the `hooks/vao_tools.py` Layer-3 verification facade (26 CLI tools over 19 `hooks/vao/` modules; v3.47.0 adds `verify-check-can-fail`, v3.55.0 adds `verify-frontend-e2e-loop-exit`, v3.59.0 adds `verify-claim-instrument-binding`, v3.66.0 adds `verify-diagnosis-falsified`, v4.0.0 adds `verify-principles-respected` + `verify-required-reads`) + the `hooks/skill_invocation_audit.py` Layer-6 Stop-hook auditor + the v3.8.0 `hooks/lineage_graph.py` CDLG core + `hooks/run_metrics.py` instrumentation** (plus a shared schema module + the v1.0.0 `hooks/locks.py` cross-session lock layer — extended in v3.8.0 with `cdlg_overlap` — + the `hooks/override_markers.py` helper, + the v3.30.0 `hooks/run_continuity.py` substrate + the v3.56.0 `hooks/open_work.py` open-work substrate behind the turn-boundary completion lock), **15 setup/support scripts** (`setup.py` (v3.32.0 first-install hardening) + `install_mempalace.py` + `install_librarian.py` (v3.29.0) + `install_gateway.py` (v3.36.0 — the external-LLM gateway installer) + `install_knowledge_server.py` (v3.49.0 — the knowledge-server installer + CLI behind `/architect-team:knowledge-install`: provisions `~/.architect-team/knowledge/`, PRINTS the portable `server.py --repo-root <repo>` MCP registration, and confirms serving only after a real `tools/call` round trip) installers, `scripts/notify/notify.py` email notifier from v0.9.18, v1.0.0's `scripts/setup/teams_mode.py` mode-detection helper extended in v1.5.0 with `format_dispatch_banner()`, v1.1.0's `scripts/setup/worktree_paths.py` worktree-aware state-resolution helper, **v1.2.0+v1.3.0's `scripts/setup/worktree_lifecycle.py` worktree-lifecycle helper**, **v1.8.0's `scripts/setup/agent_resume.py` resume + checkpoint helper**, **v3.1.0's `scripts/setup/agent_boilerplate_blocks.py` + `sync_agent_boilerplate.py` canonical-boilerplate pair**, **v3.42.0's `scripts/setup/compile_skills.py` + `guidance_blocks.py`**, and **v3.32.0's `scripts/setup/set_default_model.py` agent-model lever** — the deterministic `--model fable|opus|sonnet|haiku` uniform frontmatter rewrite that is also the implemented Opus fallback for a harness predating the `fable` alias, plus the v3.35.0 availability-gated Codex 5.6 role split: `AGENT_ROLES` classifies all 39 agents, `--split codex` / the tri-state `--auto` on `CT6_CODEX_56_AVAILABLE` put the 21 development/code-checking/testing agents on the secondary alias while the 18 architecture/control/design agents stay on `fable`; v3.40.0 — the split writes the provider-neutral `ct6-secondary` under policy `secondary-split` (legacy `codex-5.6-sol` / `codex-split` still read), `--split secondary` canonical, backed by the `SECONDARY_PROVIDERS` registry — OpenAI Codex `gpt-5.6-sol` or Z.ai GLM 5.2 `glm-5.2`; v3.41.0 — the registry entries carry the REQUIRED `route_dialect` (the gateway route is derived `<route_dialect>/<model>`; `route_model` removed) and the split's written frontmatter id becomes the harness-spawnable spawn alias `claude-haiku-4-5`, gateway-impersonated to the chosen secondary, disclosed), the agent model policy at the v4.0.0 ROLE split (`--split role`, policy `role-split`: `fable` on the 15 judgment seats, `opus` on the 9 delivery / execution seats, `sonnet` on the 14 mechanical verification / extraction / observation / documentation seats, `haiku` on `bug-classifier`; the v3.43.0 delivery-adversarial split stays reproducible via `--split delivery`), and **8932 pytest self-tests + 2 skipped, 0 failed** (the v4.1.0 CHANGELOG entry's count across 277 top-level test files, bracketed under `docs/measurements/` as the release's closing act; the v4.0.0 bracket was 8874 + 2 across 274 files at `docs/measurements/2026-11-09-v4.0.0-suite.json`; see the Tests-section environment note) that validate every structural artifact and guard against cross-component drift. Earlier inventory (when this map was first synthesized at v1.2.0): 26 skills, 27 agents, 12 commands, 1744 tests. **v1.3.0 makes auto-cleanup of merged worktrees the default for every `/architect-team` family invocation** — `/architect-team`, `/architect-team:bug-fix`, and `/architect-team:mini` each fire `cleanup_merged_worktrees()` as their FIRST action (before argument parsing, before refinement, before the v1.2.0 auto-worktree creation). Trigger 1 sweeps merged-and-forgotten worktrees from prior runs at the start of each new run; trigger 2 fires at the end of mini Phase M7 after the auto-merge to main and removes the mini's own worktree. The `exclude_current=True` safeguard means the cwd is never auto-removed (even on re-entry from a merged-branch worktree). The new `/architect-team:cleanup-worktrees` command (12th) exposes the same helper for on-demand invocation with `--dry-run` and `--against <ref>` flags. Merged-branch detection uses `git merge-base --is-ancestor`; squash-merged branches are NOT auto-detected. Best-effort: cleanup failures NEVER block the new run. **v1.2.0 makes auto-worktree creation the default for every `/architect-team` family invocation** — `/architect-team`, `/architect-team:bug-fix`, and `/architect-team:mini` auto-create a fresh worktree at `<parent-of-repo>/<repo-name>-<slug>/` on branch `architect-team/<slug>` before invoking the pipeline skill; the user's main checkout stays on whatever branch they were on; each run is filesystem-isolated; concurrent runs work without any manual `git worktree add` setup. Re-entry detection (`current_worktree_is_run()`) prevents nested worktrees; the `--no-worktree` flag reverts to v1.1.0 single-tree behavior verbatim; cleanup is NOW automatic in v1.3.0 (the v1.2.0 manual recommendation at Phase 8 / B8 / M7 success still appears, but the next run sweeps merged worktrees without user action). **v1.1.0 closes the cross-session coordination gap for git-worktree-based parallel runs** via a 3-layer model: filesystem isolation via git worktrees, architectural coordination via the v1.0.0 lock layer resolved to the main worktree, context sharing via MemPalace resolved to the main worktree. The `scripts/setup/worktree_paths.py` helper exposes `shared_state_dir()` / `run_state_dir()` / `is_worktree()` — used by the lock layer's default resolution and (per skill docs) by the MemPalace integration's palace-path resolution. Single-session users (no worktrees, OR with `--no-worktree`) see ZERO behavior change because both resolvers degenerate to `cwd / '.architect-team'` in non-worktree clones. **v1.0.0 makes Claude Code's experimental Agent Teams primitive the default dispatch mode** — long-lived named teammates with their own 1M context windows, a shared task list, direct `SendMessage`, and a Lead that owns coordination. Teams mode engages when `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1` (env or `~/.claude/settings.json`) AND Claude Code ≥ 2.1.32 AND `--no-teams` was not passed; otherwise the pipeline falls back to subagents mode (the v0.10.0 behavior, unchanged). The `hooks/locks.py` primitive lets two concurrent `/architect-team` invocations in separate Claude Code sessions claim disjoint file scopes and run truly parallel — overlapping scopes queue based on a path-glob intersection check (4h TTL stale detection by default). The two evidence hooks now handle both `PostToolUse(TaskUpdate)` / `SubagentStop` AND the teams-mode `TaskCompleted` / `TeammateIdle` triggers, dispatching internally to the same enforcement code path. Its enforcement is layered: Python hooks gate teammate task-completion, teammate idle, and the orchestrator's terminal state; independent verifier agents and teams re-check test-completeness, editability, interactive-element-and-page genuineness, visual fidelity, and (v0.9.23) documentation currency against reality; and the discipline skills are pressure-written to resist rationalization. A run optionally emits per-project email notifications (v0.9.18) when the target project supplies a `.architect-team-notify.json`.

## 3. Directory Structure

```
claude_skill_lib/
├── .claude/                 # OpenSpec-installed workspace commands + skills (opsx/* commands + openspec-* skills; tracked)
├── .claude-plugin/          # Plugin identity: plugin.json + marketplace.json (version 4.1.0)
├── agents/                  # 39 named subagent definitions (.md with frontmatter: name / description / tools / model / color + skills: [ct6-agent-core]; NO compiled boilerplate blocks since v4.0.0; model by role — 15 fable / 9 opus / 14 sonnet / 1 haiku)
├── commands/                # 30 slash-command bodies (.md with frontmatter; v4.0.0 adds home / principles / publish; v4.1.0 adds unlock — the USER's hold-release verb, disable-model-invocation: true)
├── workflows/               # (v4.0.0) Workflow-tool scripts
│   └── ct6-converge.js      #   N reviewers in parallel -> one fixer per round -> loop until every reviewer returns ok; human-only maxRounds; replaces the ralph-loop plugin
├── hooks/                   # hooks.json wiring + 9 enforcement scripts + shared substrates + the vao/ package
│   ├── hooks.json           #   wires 10 events -> 9 scripts: PreToolUse[*] (skill gate) / PreToolUse[Edit|Write|NotebookEdit] (override guard) / PreToolUse[Bash] (deletion guard, v4.0.0) / PostToolUse[TaskUpdate] + TaskCompleted (review gate) / PostToolUse[Skill] (engagement recorder) / SubagentStop + TeammateIdle (idle check) / Stop (completion audit) / PreCompact (closeout) / SessionStart (run continuity) / SubagentStart + SubagentStop + TaskCreated + TaskCompleted + Stop (timings_log, v4.0.0) — detect-once command strings (v3.9.3)
│   ├── pretool_skill_gate.py        # PreToolUse[*] + PostToolUse[Skill]: the skill-invocation hard-gate (v3.15.0) + Arm 2 sticky run (v3.30.0) + Arm 3 task-deletion refusal (v3.61.0) + Arm 4 commit identity (v3.67.0) + Arm 5 unlock-release only inside the user's /architect-team:unlock turn (v4.1.0)
│   ├── pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py # PreToolUse(Edit/Write/NotebookEdit): the unilateral-override guard + the human-owned immutables (deploy config v3.44.0, completion-lock ground truth v3.56.0, docs/measurements/ v3.60.0, publish config + the rendered PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md v4.0.0, .architect-team/releases/ v4.1.0)
│   ├── deletion_guard.py    #   (v4.0.0) PreToolUse(Bash): recoverability-classified deletion guard (allow / ask / deny via hookSpecificOutput; ALWAYS exit 0) + the trash / prune / classify / check verbs
│   ├── completion_unlock.py #   (v4.1.0) NOT a wired hook — the engine behind /architect-team:unlock: inspect (read-only) / release (retire tasks, resolve asks, waive gates, stand the marker down — under ONE release record in .architect-team/releases/) / status; the release verb is Arm-5-gated to the user's own command turn
│   ├── review-gate-task.py         # PostToolUse(TaskUpdate) OR TaskCompleted gate (v1.0.0 trigger split; v3.47.0 active-run unmanifested-task gate; v4.0.0 resolves the task dir via open_work.session_task_dir)
│   ├── teammate-idle-check.py      # SubagentStop OR TeammateIdle gate (v1.0.0 trigger split)
│   ├── pipeline-completion-audit.py # Stop gate + standalone --check + the completion lock (v3.56.0) + the continuation guard (v3.30.0) + the audit arms (evidence integrity v3.47.0, _audit_frontend_e2e v3.55.0, _audit_release_backing v3.61.0, _audit_diagnosis_falsified v3.66.0, _audit_required_reads v4.0.0; _audit_declared_gates also holds the converge:<kind> gates and READS a cited record, v4.1.0); a hold is hook JSON on stdout with exit 0 — transcript Stop hook feedback, never a hook error on the user's screen (v4.1.0; CT6_STOP_HOLD_MODE=block restores decision: block)
│   ├── timings_log.py       #   (v4.0.0) SubagentStart/Stop + TaskCreated/Completed + Stop: agent / task / phase / run intervals -> .architect-team/run-history/timings.jsonl (observability, never a gate; also a library for notify.py + scripts/homepage/)
│   ├── precompact-closeout.py       # PreCompact closeout doc-currency reminder (v3.18.0); runs closeout_check.py
│   ├── sessionstart-run-continuity.py # SessionStart resume directive (v3.30.0) + the model-split / activation self-heals (v3.39.0–v3.41.1) + the credit-failover path (v3.58.0) + the task-tools warning (v4.0.0)
│   ├── review_evidence_schema.py   # shared single-source-of-truth evidence schema v7: 17 required + 6 optional fields (v3.59.0 claim_instrument_binding_review, v4.0.0 principles_review) + _detect_trigger_mode (v1.0.0)
│   ├── open_work.py         #   (v3.56.0) the completion-lock substrate: the harness task list (BOTH layouts — <root>/<full session id>/ and session-<first 8>/, v4.0.0) + the transcript-DERIVED ask-ledger + classify_turn_output -> evaluate_completion_lock; v3.64.0 abandonment; v4.0.0 advisory `unavailable` DEFAULT root + task_tools_opted_in; operator list / resolve / retire CLI; NOT a wired hook
│   ├── run_continuity.py    #   run-continuity substrate (v3.30.0): active-run.json lifecycle marker + progress fingerprint + engagement / teammate transcript analysis + CLI
│   ├── git_identity.py      #   (v3.67.0) NOT a hook — per-checkout commit identity: resolve / set / status / args / the `git --` wrapper over <main-worktree>/.architect-team/git-identity.json; read by pretool_skill_gate.py Arm 4 and worktree_lifecycle's auto-merge
```

Runtime state is written under `<workspace>/.architect-team/` (gitignored) and `<workspace>/.mempalace/` (gitignored) — see §6.

## 4. Module Guide

> **Currency note (2026-08-09):** the inventory tables below were authored at v2.8.0 (29 skills / 30 agents / 14 commands / 2635 tests — per this map's intro blockquote). Subsequent v2.9.0 → v3.31.0 disciplines have shipped additional skills, commands, agents, `hooks/` modules, the `services/` server tier, and the v3.31.0 instruction-compliance lint engine (`scripts/compliance/instruction_compliance.py` + `docs/INSTRUCTION_COMPLIANCE_RUBRIC.md`). **As of v4.1.0 (fa28aa0) the live inventory is 60 skills / 39 agents / 30 commands / 9 enforcement hook scripts + 10 wired events / 26 Layer-3 verification tools / 8932 tests + 2 skipped, 0 failed across 277 top-level test files (disk-anchored via `git ls-files 'tests/test_*.py'`; linux / py3.13; the v4.0.0 bracket was 8874 + 2 across 274 — see the Tests-section environment note); the agent model policy is the v4.0.0 role split (15 `model: fable` / 9 `model: opus` / 14 `model: sonnet` / 1 `model: haiku`; `scripts/setup/set_default_model.py --split role`, policy `role-split`); every agent preloads `skills/ct6-agent-core`; every heavy skill's disciplines live in `references/` chapters behind STOP pointers with a per-skill INDEX of required reads. The per-section tables below were rewritten to this tree in the v4.0.0 refresh.** **Post-v3.49.0 the data-engineering lane shipped end-to-end (Runs A–F, v3.49.0–v3.54.0) and v3.55.0 added the frontend-E2E loop-exit gate: the `data-eng-pipeline` skill + the 25th command `data-eng` (v3.50.0, Run B), the `warehouse-sql-mining` skill + the `scripts/sql_mining/sql_mining.py` engine (v3.51.0, Run C), the `data-annotations` skill + `scripts/data_dictionary/annotations.py` (v3.52.0, Run D), the usage/review round-trip inside `scripts/data_dictionary/data_dictionary.py` (v3.53.0, Run E), the JSON-LD emitter `scripts/data_dictionary/jsonld_export.py` (v3.54.0, Run F), and the 22nd Layer-3 tool `verify-frontend-e2e-loop-exit` (`hooks/vao/frontend_e2e.py`) + the `_audit_frontend_e2e` completion-audit arm (v3.55.0) — the three post-v3.49.0 skills are rowed below.** **v3.49.0 adds the `services/knowledge_server/` server-tier member (6 modules — the stdlib-only MCP knowledge server over the run-produced sidecars) + `scripts/setup/install_knowledge_server.py` + the 24th command `knowledge-install` + 8 new test files — NO new skill / agent / hook / Layer-3 tool.** **v3.36.0 adds `scripts/setup/install_gateway.py` (the external-LLM gateway installer — LiteLLM proxy, the codex-5.6-sol → OpenAI route, api-key vs subscription auth modes) + the setup.py `--external-llm` wiring + `tests/test_install_gateway.py` (35 offline tests) — NO new skill / agent / command / hook / Layer-3 tool.** **v3.33.0 adds the `claude-design-import` skill (the 48th) + the `scripts/claude_design/claude_design_import.py` engine + `tests/test_claude_design_import.py` (47 offline tests); a Claude Design link is materialized and fed into the EXISTING interactive-mockup oracle path — NO new agent / command / hook / Layer-3 tool.** The v3.11.0 additions explicitly rowed below: skill `structure-optimization`; agents `structure-analyst` / `reference-tracer` / `structure-adversary`; command `optimize-structure`; the `system-architect` eighth audit mode (ninth mode overall, counting the default architectural-recommendation mode) (Restructure Plan Audit). The cascading `note:` field in the frontmatter is the authoritative running ledger for every shipped release after v2.8.0. **Affordance inventory (v2.13.0 discipline): none detected** — this is a no-UI plugin repo; the only `_AFFORDANCE_SIGNATURES` occurrences on disk are the detector's own pattern definitions (`hooks/vao/persona.py`) and the canonical test fixtures, which are signature SOURCES, not affordances of this codebase. The v3.8.0 additions explicitly documented in the tables below: skills `endpoint-trace-mapping` + `data-lineage-mapping`; agent `endpoint-tracer`; hook modules `lineage_graph.py` + `run_metrics.py`; the `cdlg_overlap` function added to `hooks/locks.py`; and the iteration-limit removal in `hooks/pipeline-completion-audit.py`. (Other post-v2.8.0 additions are now each individually rowed / bulleted in their subsections below — the `visual-to-api-design`, `test-prod-safety-classifier`, `test-run-monitor`, `cartographer-team`, `domain-research-team`, `api-design-from-frontend`, `data-engineering-exploration` skills; the `visual-to-api`, `classify-test-prod-safety`, `discipline-status`, `inject`, `monitor-tests` commands; and the `discipline_registry.py`, `inflight_inbox.py`, `shared_rule_constants.py` hook modules — while the `domain-researcher`, `test-run-watcher`, `monitor-synthesizer` agents are disclosed in the Agents note below; all of them also remain tracked in the frontmatter ledger.)

### Skills (60)

> Every one of the **60** skills is rowed below — no skill is disclosed-only. The table is the v2.8.0 snapshot (its original rows) + the v3.8.0 lineage skills (`endpoint-trace-mapping` + `data-lineage-mapping`) + the 10 skills that were previously disclosed only in the ledger / footnotes, now promoted to first-class rows: `common-pipeline-conventions`, `verified-agent-output`, `interactive-mockup-discovery`, `visual-to-api-design`, `test-prod-safety-classifier`, `test-run-monitor`, `cartographer-team`, `domain-research-team`, `api-design-from-frontend`, `data-engineering-exploration` — plus the v3.46.0 `delivery-manifest`, promoted to its first-class row in the v3.47.0 refresh, the v3.52.0 `data-annotations` (rowed at its release), and the v3.48.0 `contract-first-parallelism` + v3.50.0 `data-eng-pipeline` + v3.51.0 `warehouse-sql-mining`, promoted to first-class rows in the v3.55.1 doc-currency sweep (they had been ledger-disclosed only) — plus the v3.65.0 `intuition-map-lifecycle` + `playwright-suite-builder`, rowed at their release, the v3.66.0 `verification-planning` (rowed in the v4.0.0 refresh — it had been ledger-disclosed only), and the four v4.0.0 skills `ct6-agent-core` / `project-principles` / `page-publishing` / `project-homepage`. **Arithmetic: 60 rows + 0 disclosed-not-rowed = 60.** Where a skill carries `references/` chapters (14 do), its row names the INDEX under `references/` that says what to Read before each phase (v4.0.0) and the chapter count.

**v2.1.0 — `interactive-mockup-discovery` joins**: the canonical home of the two-pass mechanism for interactive HTML mockup oracles (Pass 1 = observation via `interaction-observer`; Pass 2 = intent inference via `interaction-intuiter`'s INTENT-INFERENCE mode; Layer 3 = `verify-interactions-honored`). Documents the 7-value `action_kind` vocabulary and the 10-pattern semantic-vs-observed mismatch matrix. v2.0.0 added `verified-agent-output`.

| Skill | Role |
|---|---|
| `phenotypes` | (v2.3.0) Discover/match/emit pre-made generalized deployable architectures (blueprint + scaffold + metadata under `phenotypes/`); proposed reuse-first or via `--phenotype`, never silent. Engine: `scripts/phenotypes/phenotypes.py`. Seeded with `user-management` + `config-management` + `ai-management` + `code-wiki` (v3.13.0; a self-hosted Next.js documentation wiki absorbed from deepwiki-open, LLM-stripped, ingesting `*_MAP.md`). |
| `phenotype-absorption` | (v2.3.0) The absorb playbook — ingest any arbitrary codebase into a new labeled phenotype (analyze → generalize → scaffold → validate → index). Reached via `/architect-team:absorb-phenotype <path> --label <name>`. |
| `architect-team-pipeline` | The orchestrator playbook — Phase −2 → 8. **v4.0.0:** `SKILL.md` is the spine (153 KB before v4.0.0, 46 KB at v4.0.0, 18 KB at v4.1.2 with 26 chapters); each phase's full procedure is a chapter under `skills/architect-team-pipeline/references/` (`phase--2.md` … `phase-8.md`, `phase-8-commit.md`, `phase-boundary-inbox-check.md`, the data-eng front-door precedence note — 18 chapters) and `skills/architect-team-pipeline/references/INDEX.md` says which to Read before each phase. Run-state rules: unbounded solving (loop until success — no iteration ceiling as of v3.8.0; oscillation continues from a different angle rather than aborting), the shared-state concurrency model, the required-input escalation marker. Phase 3 runs the SELECTED tests per slice and Phase 8 the full suite once (v4.0.0 test selection); Phase 8 commits as the checkout's recorded identity (v3.67.0); the −1B / −1C convergence loops run as `workflows/ct6-converge.js`. |
| `intake-and-mapping` | Phase −1 codebase discovery + the per-codebase / integration convergence loops — since v4.0.0 each runs as `workflows/ct6-converge.js` (three reviewers in parallel, one fixer per round, a human-only `maxRounds`; `ralph-loop` retired), declared before the loop and recorded after it through `scripts/converge/record.py` (the `converge:<kind>` gate the Stop audit holds until every reviewer returned `ok`), with `cartographer` CONDITIONAL and the native mapping form in `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/native-methodology.md` when it is absent. Map-invalidation flag forces re-validation of a wrong-but-fresh map. |
| `reuse-first-design` | The extend > compose > reuse > build-new ladder; the Reuse Decision Log. |
| `frontend-route-mapping` | ROUTE_MAP.md schema + completeness rubric. |
| `design-fidelity-mapping` | Conditional DESIGN_MAP.md (design tokens, asset registry, per-screen specs). `design_baseline` frontmatter; a baseline migration forces a full re-derive. |
| `visual-fidelity-reconciliation` | Strict QA vs DESIGN_MAP. Phase 0 live-app precondition; zero-tolerance; the design-migration "unchanged inverts" rule; verify-against-the-Oracle-not-a-classification. |
| `visual-verification-team` | Independent live-app verification — `visual-capture` → `visual-analyzer` → `system-architect` synthesis; a tier-2 shape since v4.0.0 (capture is the producer, ONE `visual-analyzer` per capture set its paired independent checker; three only when the gate is tier 3). The verdict is measured data, not eyeballed images. |
| `playwright-user-flows` | White-box Playwright methodology; real-backend-by-default for `both`-layer features. **v4.0.0:** Phase A (examination before any test code) / B (authoring from the inventory) / C (coverage verification) and the anti-pattern rationalizations are 4 chapters under `skills/playwright-user-flows/references/` with `skills/playwright-user-flows/references/INDEX.md`; Phase 5 enumerates a suite THROUGH the test-index selector (impacted flows + pins), not the entire dev project. |
| `dev-api-integration-testing` | Live-dev-API testing — real DB / queue / cache, side-effect verification. |
| `coverage-mapping` | `coverage-map.json` schema + lifecycle (Phase 1 / 3 / 7 / 8). |
| `team-spawning-and-review-gates` | Teammate manifests; the v7 review-gate evidence schema (17 required fields = 12 base self-review incl. `ui_interaction_review` + the 5 VAO fields `oracle_match_review` / `baseline_clean_review` / `no_fake_data_review` / `adversarial_review` / `skill_invocation_audit`, plus the 6 optional `interactions_honored_review` / `live_verification_review` / `appearance_scope_review` / `check_integrity_review` (v3.47.0) / `claim_instrument_binding_review` (v3.59.0) / `principles_review` (v4.0.0), and the independent `task-reviewer` verdict); the independent-review dispatch; the SR schema; **v3.47.0** adds the `spec_fingerprint` manifest field, the `spec-drift` SR origin (direct-dispatch), red-first's three sources, and the reading-teammate-state chapter (the three knowable states). **v4.0.0:** 13 chapters under `skills/team-spawning-and-review-gates/references/` with `skills/team-spawning-and-review-gates/references/INDEX.md`; `skills/team-spawning-and-review-gates/references/review-tiers.md` makes the reviewer count a per-gate decision — tier 3 (three independent reviewers + synthesis, the loop owned by a Workflow script) for architecture approval, the Phase 7 master review and map convergence; tier 2 (the producer + ONE paired adversary, a third verdict only on disagreement) for every Phase 3 task gate, slice-level editability / interaction completeness and bug-fix sensibility; tier 1 (a Layer-3 tool or a `prompt` hook) for classification and shape checks — so three reviewers is no longer the universal default; the tier and the models used are written into the evidence file. Source of truth for the schema: `hooks/review_evidence_schema.py`. |
| `root-cause-test-failures` | Predict → 3-pass RCA (forward / backward / falsify) → evidence-backed verdict; multiple-simultaneous-causes. |
| `diagnostic-research-team` | A tier-3 panel of three `diagnostic-researcher` agents (a judgment gate by design — declare → `ct6-converge` → record) + `system-architect` robustness review before a test-failure fix team spawns; since v3.66.0 every falsification test is EXECUTED and ranking is by executed evidence. |
| `expensive-verification-debugging` | When a verify cycle is expensive (deploy / rebuild / slow CI), audit the whole failure pathway and batch the fixes. |
| `editability-completeness` | `editability-reviewer` agents at the gate's review tier (v4.0.0 — ONE paired reviewer for a slice at Phase 3 / 5, tier 2, a second independent reviewer only on disagreement; three in parallel via `ct6-converge` for the Phase 7 sweep and `/architect-team:editability-audit`, tier 3) enumerate every attribute, classify editability, trace UI→DB; architect robustness review; multi-pass until the panel agrees on an empty `gaps[]` (no fixed pass cap). |
| `interaction-completeness` | The judgment-heavy VERIFICATION gate that `playwright-user-flows` was followed (v0.9.19) — `interaction-reviewer` agents at the gate's review tier (v4.0.0 — ONE paired reviewer for a slice at Phase 3 / 5, tier 2, a tie-breaking third only when the two disagree; three in parallel via `ct6-converge` for the Phase 7 sweep, tier 3) independently re-enumerate every interactive element AND every page/screen/route, classify element wiring + page `live`/`placeholder`/`confirmed-stub`, audit Playwright test authenticity, trace element→endpoint, flag hardcoded-should-be-dynamic values; architect Round-3; multi-pass to convergence (no self-imposed pass cap since v3.66.0); gaps → SRs. The sibling of `editability-completeness` at the granularity of controls and pages. |
| `dynamic-value-discovery` | A cross-role discipline (v0.9.19) for telling a genuine static literal from sample data standing in for a dynamic, data-bound value — classify every displayed value `static`/`dynamic` FROM CONTEXT, bind every dynamic one to a named data source, escalate genuine ambiguity. Modeled on `reuse-first-design`; consulted by the architect, the developers, and the evaluator. |
| `mempalace-integration` | Per-workspace MemPalace store — `--wing` mining (rooms are `init`-detected from directory structure, not a `mine` flag), auto-mine on artifact write, search before output. |
| `readme-styling` | The bitmap house style for READMEs — canvas/centering, pipe-table + ASCII-graph alignment, banner / dividers / panels / flowcharts / logic maps, the GitHub-safe + ANSI color model, and the theming engine (6 preset themes + an interactive picker + the `readme-theme` marker). |
| `documentation-currency` | The Phase 8 docs-reflect-the-code gate — the doc inventory (maps + README + CHANGELOG + CLAUDE.md), what "current" means, the orchestrator-updates-then-system-architect-audits flow. |
| `bug-fix-pipeline` | The sibling `Phase B−1 → B8` pipeline — replicate-first, reproduction-is-the-regression-test, generalized-fix, QA-replay-against-live-dev. Reached via `/architect-team:bug-fix` or the main pipeline's Phase −2 triage. (v0.9.22) **v4.0.0:** 21 chapters at v4.1.2 (`phase-b-1.md`, `phase-b1.md` … `phase-b8.md`, the five non-negotiable disciplines, structured bug isolation, the anti-patterns to reject, augment-never-replace precedence, …) under `skills/bug-fix-pipeline/references/` with `skills/bug-fix-pipeline/references/INDEX.md`; B3 labels an unfalsified cause a hypothesis (v3.66.0); `--full-data` opts out of sample-first iteration on a data change. |
| `interaction-intuition` | Per-frontend codebase intuition map — enumerates every interactive element, intuits each element's action and candidate endpoints, assigns confidence, authors ambiguity questions for low-confidence items. Produces `INTERACTION_INTUITION_MAP.md`. (v0.9.21) |
| `ux-test-builder` | UX test builder pipeline `Phase U0 → U9` — persona + objectives + target site → literal flow → `flow-explorer` agents per the review tier (one by default, three only at tier 3) propose 10-15 adjacent flows each → distilled → one `.spec.ts` per flow → `flow-executor` agents per the review tier (one runs every flow against the live target by default, a second re-runs disputed flows, three only at tier 3) → consensus → bug routing. Reached via `/architect-team:ux-test`. (v0.9.29) **v4.0.0:** 16 chapters at v4.1.2 (`phase-u0.md` … `phase-u9.md`, the five disciplines, notifications) under `skills/ux-test-builder/references/` with `skills/ux-test-builder/references/INDEX.md`. |
| `proposal-refiner` | Conversational pre-pipeline prompt refinement — grades a free-text prompt on 6 axes (clarity, scope, acceptance, grounding, conflict, scope-fidelity), asks clarifying questions, and iterates until the grade clears — no default iteration ceiling since v3.66.0 (a cap only when the user sets one). Runs BEFORE Phase −2 when input is prose. Reached standalone via `/architect-team:refine-prompt`. (v0.9.33) |
| `email-testing` | Cross-cutting email-testing discipline — 4 phases (E1 detect, E2 Mailpit provision, E3 capture+analyze, E4 link-follow+flow-complete). Consumed by `bug-replicator`, `flow-executor`, `integration` agents. Mailpit by default; template-first analysis; every link tested; mandatory teardown. (v0.9.34) |
| `mini-architect-team-pipeline` | Mini variant orchestrator (Phase M0 → M8) — single architect, single `mini-qa` agent, working branch `mini/<slug>`, auto-merge to main on green; M8 cycle 4 (three red verdicts on the same proposal) writes `.architect-team/mini/<slug>/escalation/` and re-spawns `/architect-team` with that folder as REQ_DIR. Heavyweight review is batched via the `Mini-Run:` trailer + the `/architect-team:mini-review-sweep` command. Reached via `/architect-team:mini`. (v0.10.0) **v4.0.0:** 15 chapters at v4.1.2 (`phase-m0.md`, `phase-m1.md`, `phase-m2.md`, `phase-m3.md` … `phase-m8.md`, `qa-guidance.md`, …) under `skills/mini-architect-team-pipeline/references/` with `skills/mini-architect-team-pipeline/references/INDEX.md`; M7 commits as the recorded identity (v3.67.0 — the hardcoded maintainer override REMOVED). |
| `endpoint-trace-mapping` | **(v3.8.0 — lineage P1)** Per-endpoint internal call-trace mapping that produces the Code & Data Lineage Graph (CDLG). The agent does the live, polyglot, LLM-and-LSP-driven extraction at runtime; the deterministic core (schema + validator, `func://`/`asset://` IDs, runtime-witness reconciliation, freshness, cost ceiling) lives in `hooks/lineage_graph.py`. Two-layer extraction contract + witness-verification (every asserted edge is reconciled against the existing `code-path-witness.json` ground truth via `witness_gate`). Dispatches the `endpoint-tracer` agent. |
| `structure-optimization` | **(v3.11.0)** Codebase-restructure PLANNING pipeline (stages S0-S8): maps via `cartographer-team` (freshness-checked; produced when missing), `structure-analyst` ×3 drafts (S2 is a tier-3 judgment panel by design — the converged proposal is the map the plan rests on) converge under a deterministic every-file partition check (git ls-files = movement table ∪ stays; zero orphans/duplicates), sharded `reference-tracer` closure (one tracer per shard by default; file:line evidence + mandatory search_log) assembled into `movements.json` v1.0, `structure-adversary` rounds — tier 2 per attack round (ONE adversary paired against the closure, a second verdict on disagreement) and a tier-3 panel of three on the two confirming clean rounds — until two consecutive all-clean, `system-architect` Restructure Plan Audit, then `RESTRUCTURE_PLAN.md` + an `openspec-propose`-authored strict-validated change. Plan-only — execution is `/architect-team` driving the produced change. |
| `data-lineage-mapping` | **(v3.8.0 — lineage P3)** Asset-lineage mapping over the CDLG `asset://` nodes (`reads` / `writes` / `modifies` / `originates` edges). Carries an explicit Reuse Decision vs `data-engineering-exploration` (lineage-mapping is the per-asset trace; data-engineering-exploration is the 7-stage data-plane design pipeline). |
| `data-dictionary` | **(v3.17.0 — CT6-6 component 1, DD-1…DD-18)** Self-contextualizing data-dictionary builder: derive the data model from code/docs/direct input, define every field, record provenance (`direct-user-input`/`direct-code-comment`/`inference`/`live-data`) + value-level corroboration against live data. Engine: `scripts/data_dictionary/data_dictionary.py` (SQLite introspect + ~100-row sample + grain/field inference + reference/relational maps + `DATA_DICTIONARY_MAP.md` serializer). Complementary to `data-lineage-mapping` (this defines the FIELDS + provenance; that traces who reads/writes the assets). Sequenced after codebase+integration mapping; honest no-DB path (never fabricates `live-data`). |
| `data-annotations` | **(v3.52.0 — Run D, the data-eng lane's team memory)** A per-user, git-shared annotation store on data objects: `scripts/data_dictionary/annotations.py` manages `docs/data-annotations/<user>.json` (note/quality_flag/deprecation vocab anchored to table/table.field; validate-on-write + atomic; injective username validation). Corroborate-on-ingest PER-FIELD — factual claims route through `data_dictionary.py::corroborate_definition` and a field absent from every source is served `uncorroborated`, never truth (opinions stored as-authored). Gate-integrity: annotation memory INFORMS a per-run gate, never skips/auto-satisfies it. The knowledge server merges annotations at query time (ADDITIVE in `services/knowledge_server/dictionary_source.py`; a no-annotations response is byte-unchanged). Complementary to `data-dictionary` (that defines the fields; this is the team's durable notes on them). |
| `warehouse-sql-mining` | **(v3.51.0 — Run C, the data-eng lane's evidence miner)** T-SQL shape mining over stored-procedure / view TEXT — joins / filters / aggregate-ratio metrics + table read/write relationships — via the vendored stdlib extractor `scripts/sql_mining/sql_mining.py` (NO sqlglot, decision D6). Parse-coverage-honest (`{parsed, skipped, failed}` with reasons; undecodable / malformed land in failed, never dropped, never crash); corroboration-gated (candidates enter at provenance `inference` and pass `corroborate_definition`; relationships emitted as lineage `data_asset`/`reads`/`writes`, existing kinds only). Mines SQL TEXT, not a live warehouse. Feeds the dictionary / lineage maps / `data-engineering-exploration`. |
| `data-eng-pipeline` | **(v3.50.0 — Run B, the data-engineering lane)** The lane orchestrator (phases D−1…D8 on the bug-fix-pipeline structure), reached via `/architect-team:data-eng` / the `--data-eng` flag / the Phase −2 classifier's fifth kind `data-eng`. D0 dispatches `data-engineering-exploration` VERBATIM (the lane is its third caller — compose-not-fork); D1/D2–D6/D8 reuse Phases 1/2–6/8; two knowledge-server-wired disciplines — D−1 warm-catalog-first (query `get_dictionary_status` BEFORE a rebuild; the server informs, the per-run gate decides) + D7 catalog-refresh (rebuild the dictionary → re-index the knowledge server → mine to MemPalace). **v4.0.0:** 12 chapters at v4.1.2 under `skills/data-eng-pipeline/references/` with `skills/data-eng-pipeline/references/INDEX.md`, incl. `skills/data-eng-pipeline/references/sample-first.md` — before a change that targets a subset of a big table runs against full data, the lane profiles the subset from the dictionary, stratifies on the dimension the change depends on, sizes the smallest covering sample, iterates with a per-stratum before/after diff, then scales (engine `scripts/data_dictionary/sampling.py`; `--full-data` opts out). |
| `closeout` | **(v3.18.0 — CT6-6 component 2, CO-1…CO-3)** Session-end documentation-currency double-check: fires before compaction (the `PreCompact` hook), reviews the changes against the requirement, and updates any stale doc itself rather than flagging it. Engine: `hooks/closeout_check.py` (deterministic staleness signals over the working-tree diff); worker: `agents/closeout-agent`; manual trigger: `/architect-team:closeout`. Reuses `documentation-currency` + the `doc-updater` whole-file pattern; works OUTSIDE a pipeline run (no coverage-map needed). |
| `claude-md-efficiency` | **(v3.19.0 — CT6-6 component 3, CMD-1…CMD-4)** When (and only when) MemPalace is installed, `CLAUDE.md` becomes a thin POINTER (tells the agent where to find things, loaded on demand) not a container, kept very small, with standards→reference-MemPalace + toggleable customizations. Engine: `scripts/claude_md/claude_md_efficiency.py` (pointer-shape + byte-budget assessor + minimal-pointer generator). Reuses `mempalace-integration` (the availability check + the mine/wake-up flow it points INTO). |
| `mcp-output-contract-design` | **(v3.20.0 — CT6-6 component 4, MCP-1…MCP-3)** Best-in-class output standardization for an LLM agent embedded in an application: a closed output contract (JSON Schema) + a forced structured-output mechanism + validation + retry-on-mismatch, so the agent's output is guaranteed consistent to parse. Engine: `scripts/mcp_design/output_contract.py` (`build_output_contract` + `validate_against_contract` + `assess_contract`); worker: `agents/mcp-design-agent`. Outward-facing counterpart to `verified-agent-output` (which verifies CT6's own agents). |
| `helpdesk` | **(v3.21.0 — CT6-6 component 5, HD-1…HD-3)** Logit — the user-run MANUAL counterpart to the automatic issue logging: after a bad session, file a triage report with consent + a privacy level (`full` / `summary` allow-list / `off`); the submission carries the version + `source: manual-helpdesk` so the same triage process consumes it. Engine: `scripts/helpdesk/logit.py` (consent-gated builder + privacy redaction + validator); command: `/architect-team:logit`. Honest boundary — the actual send to the triage server is the server-tier (SEC + EVAL), not in-repo. |
| `token-compression` | **(v3.22.0 — CT6-6 component 6, TC-1…TC-3)** "Caveman" compression of agents' INTERNAL communication to lower token cost, WITHOUT touching external output (hard internal-only boundary). Engine: `scripts/token_compression/caveman.py` (`compress` drops filler + wordy phrases, preserves content/identifiers/numbers/line-structure/code verbatim; `compression_stats` measures the saving). A lossy-of-filler stdlib floor; a heavier ML package (LLMLingua-style) is a documented 3rd-party app-layer option. |
| `claude-design-import` | **(v3.33.0)** Native ingestion of a Claude Design project offered via a LINK (`claude.ai/design/p/<id>` and/or the `claude_design` MCP) instead of a downloaded zip: `detect_claude_design_offer` (both trigger forms + the `?file=` / `Implement:` focus) then `materialize_project` (path-safe; `_safe_relpath` rejects absolute paths + `..`) writes the WHOLE project to `<workspace>/.architect-team/claude-design/<project-id>/`, handed to the EXISTING `oracle-deriver` `interactive-mockup` oracle path (then `interactive-mockup-discovery` then `visual-to-api-design`) — NO new downstream consumer. Engine: `scripts/claude_design/claude_design_import.py` (stdlib, offline; the real MCP fetch is an injected `ClaudeDesignSource` runtime adapter, no tokens persisted; `plan_when_unavailable` = instruct-then-fallback to the zip/local design path). |
| `delivery-manifest` | **(v3.46.0)** The end-of-run bill of sale — at every pipeline close-out (Phase 8 / B8 / M7 / U9, and on demand) produce a stakeholder-readable DELIVERY MANIFEST: the problem in plain speak, validation steps a non-builder can execute EACH with an expected result, and for a feature the location / name / functionality of every new element; a user-provided example document drives vocabulary + layout (`template_source` recorded). Engine: `scripts/delivery/delivery_manifest.py` (`validate_manifest` zero-error gate + placeholder rejection; **v3.47.0** adds the ERROR-severity citation findings `uncited-verified-claim` / `uncited-element-claim` — evidence must NAME an artifact). Embedded into every `run_complete` email via `--plan-file`. |
| `contract-first-parallelism` | **(v3.48.0, hardened v3.48.1)** The architect's mock-first full-stack parallelism protocol (CFP-1…6): settle the inbound-surface CONTRACT first (architect-approved, binding), the backend provisions the endpoint at its REAL path with a contract-conforming provisional payload, and the frontend builds its complete integration against that live surface while the backend builds the internals underneath. Every mock is ledger-tracked (`proposed → approved → mock-serving → live`) and the run is non-closable until `live` — retirement registers `cfp-retirement-<contract-id>` in the v3.47.0 declared-gates registry so the EXISTING Stop-hook `_audit_declared_gates` arm enforces the claim; the ledger reader is FAIL-CLOSED (v3.48.1). Engine: `scripts/contract/interface_contract.py`. |
| `common-pipeline-conventions` | The CONSTITUTION — the canonical HOME of the cross-pipeline disciplines every lane references (not a phase — a shared rulebook). **v4.0.0 shape (v4.1.0 size):** `SKILL.md` is 64,873 bytes at fa28aa0 (the v4.1.0 code commit) (from 258,527) and carries ONLY what every agent must always hold — the ranked Read-this-first rules (v3.66.0), the compiled operating principles, the no-self-imposed-limits rule, and one STOP-pointer stub (heading + pointer + first paragraph) per discipline; every discipline's full text is a chapter under `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/` (50 chapters — scope / appearance-change policy / unbounded solving / run continuity / in-flight clarification + its injection mechanism / prod-safe test classification / the codebase discipline registry / the Layer-3 gate-invocation table / declared gates / deploy mandate / git commit identity / contract-first parallelism / local-dev-test / verified-live / live-data wiring / no standing red / …, plus the v4.0.0 `deletion-discipline.md`, `converge-workflows.md`, `native-methodology.md`, `test-selection.md` and the v4.1.0 `completion-unlock.md` — a held turn is finished or reported, never released by the agent; the user's unlock command is the release) and `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/INDEX.md` maps each phase to the chapters it must Read first (`always` → skill-invocation, unbounded solving, run continuity, no-pipeline-bypass, in-flight clarification, deletion, converge workflows, completion-unlock). Depth is capped at constitution → index → chapter; the Layer-3 tool `verify-required-reads` reports a phase that started without its reads. |
| `verified-agent-output` | (v2.0.0) The Verified Agent Output (VAO) framework contract — Layer 1 `oracle-deriver` (the frozen oracle spec) + Layer 2 `adversarial-reviewer` (paired producer≠checker) + the 26 deterministic Layer-3 `verify-*` tools (v3.47.0 adds `verify-check-can-fail` — falsifiability: zero-work signatures, red-run-first, region-aware scanning; v3.55.0 `verify-frontend-e2e-loop-exit`; v3.59.0 `verify-claim-instrument-binding`; v3.66.0 `verify-diagnosis-falsified`; v4.0.0 `verify-principles-respected` + `verify-required-reads`) + the 5 VAO evidence fields in schema v7 (+ the 6 optional incl. `check_integrity_review` / `claim_instrument_binding_review` / `principles_review`). **v4.0.0:** the six-layer walkthrough, the schema-v7 breaking change, the failure-mode mapping and the composition notes are 5 chapters under `skills/verified-agent-output/references/` with `skills/verified-agent-output/references/INDEX.md`. The inward-facing counterpart to `mcp-output-contract-design`. |
| `interactive-mockup-discovery` | (v2.1.0) The two-pass mechanism for interactive HTML mockup oracles — Pass 1 observe (via `interaction-observer` in headless Chrome) → Pass 2 intent-infer (via `interaction-intuiter`'s INTENT-INFERENCE mode); the 7-value `action_kind` vocabulary + the 10-pattern semantic-vs-observed mismatch matrix; Layer 3 `verify-interactions-honored`. |
| `visual-to-api-design` | The visual-to-API design pipeline for a visual codebase (UI present, requirements absent/partial) — 4 stages (context discovery → per-persona research → page catalog → backend design) with tier-3 3-reviewer convergence per stage (each declared, run as a `ct6-converge` workflow, recorded), each stage frozen to a structured JSON artifact. Reached via `/architect-team:visual-to-api`. **v4.0.0:** 6 chapters (when it runs, the 4 stages, the 7-stage exploration pipeline, the failure shape, cross-references) under `skills/visual-to-api-design/references/` with `skills/visual-to-api-design/references/INDEX.md`. |
| `test-prod-safety-classifier` | (v2.17.0) Mass-classifies every Playwright/QA test `@prod-safe` (read-only) or `@not-prod-safe` (contains a mutation) — Mode 1 codebase-wide (`--write-annotations`), Mode 2 the Phase-3 gate that SR-escalates ambiguous cases. Command: `/architect-team:classify-test-prod-safety`. |
| `test-run-monitor` | (v3.3.0) The passive test-run monitor team (`test-run-watcher` → `monitor-synthesizer`) over LocalAdapter / CIAdapter / ProductionQAAdapter — classifies each finding flake/regression/environmental/new into a per-run report. Strictly log-only — no interrupts, no SR filing, no gating. Command: `/architect-team:monitor-tests`. |
| `cartographer-team` | The 3-analyst codebase-mapping team that produces / refreshes `CODEBASE_MAP.md` (freshness-checked), wrapping the external `cartographer` plugin when it is installed — since v4.0.0 `cartographer` is a CONDITIONAL plugin, so an absent install is reported and the native mapping form in `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/native-methodology.md` runs instead (never an abort); the three-reviewer `codebase-map-reviewer` convergence is a tier-3 gate run by `workflows/ct6-converge.js`. Consumed by `intake-and-mapping` and by `structure-optimization` (stage S1). |
| `domain-research-team` | (v3.4.0) Persona + objectives research (Phases R2-R4) — a tier-3 panel of three `domain-researcher` agents (a research panel producing a map is a judgment gate; declared before and recorded after the `ct6-converge` loop) parse the provided inputs AND perform mandatory outside industry/market/competitor research, then converge + master-synthesize a personas map; rounds until agreement (v3.66.0). |
| `api-design-from-frontend` | Backend API-surface design derived from an existing frontend codebase — enumerates the frontend's data needs + call sites and designs the matching endpoint / contract surface. Sibling of `visual-to-api-design`. |
| `data-engineering-exploration` | The 7-stage data-plane design / exploration pipeline (the data-engineering counterpart to the app pipeline). Carries the explicit Reuse Decision vs `data-lineage-mapping` (this designs the data plane; that traces per-asset lineage). |
| `intuition-map-lifecycle` | **(v3.65.0)** Lane-callable lifecycle for a frontend codebase's `INTERACTION_INTUITION_MAP.md` — three verbs: `ensure` (absent → produce; presence alone does not satisfy — the engine reports `action_required` ∈ none / produce-map / repair-map / populate, and a zero-element scaffold returns `populate`, never `none`), `grow` (user-specified flows upsert as `user_verdict: confirmed` / `source: user-flow`, deliberately bypassing the −1D bulk-verify — the user specifying the flow IS the confirmation; a contradiction with a confirmed entry leaves it byte-identical and emits a targeted question QUOTING both readings; a caller-marked `ambiguous: true` element is never upserted), `bug-path-upsert` (`source: bug-path` + `regression_refs[]` {test, ref}, the same shared contradiction guard). Additive schema (absent fields ⇒ intuiter-produced, no migration). Engine: `scripts/intuition_lifecycle/` (CRLF-preserving byte-fidelity rewrites; casefold + whitespace-collapsed matching per the v3.62.0 lesson). Mirrors `interaction-intuition` — deliberately NOT compiled with the principles block (pinned). |
| `playwright-suite-builder` | **(v3.65.0 — the 7th pipeline-driving skill; compiled principles block; registered in `_PIPELINE_SKILLS` / `_PIPELINE_COMMAND_SKILLS` + the frozen fallback / `RUN_DRIVING_SKILLS`)** The P0–P9 durable-suite orchestrator behind `/architect-team:playwright-suite` (v4.1.2: SKILL.md + `skills/playwright-suite-builder/references/INDEX.md` + 10 chapters — P0, P2, P3, P4, P5, P6, P8, P9, running-the-suite and suite-manifest): e2e-dir resolution per the target repo's own convention; inventory reconciled against the committed `<e2e-dir>/suite-manifest.json`; map freshness via `hooks/frontend_impact.py`; existing-test review DISPATCHES `test-completeness-verifier` + `interaction-completeness` (never duplicates them); suite-wide gap analysis per `playwright-user-flows` Phase C (unknown personas escalate, never guessed); gap-fill per the full authoring discipline; debug-flow registration from resolved bug artifacts; persona grouping into Playwright `dev`/`prod` projects; dev/prod classification via `test-prod-safety-classifier` (P8 default-deny — a mutating prod test needs a fully-authorized exception). Engine: `scripts/playwright_suite/manifest.py` (`verify-suite-manifest` — the P9 ship check). |
| `verification-planning` | **(v3.66.0)** Use BEFORE verifying any claim — a fix, a feature, a deploy, a diagnosis — to choose HOW: enumerate the venues (local run / dev environment / replay / seeded fixture / a stratified sample (v4.0.0) / production), prove which one each claim depends on, run the cheapest faithful one first, and record a `verification_plan` (`claim`, `venues_considered[{venue, unique_provides, faithful, why_not}]`, `chosen_venue`, `why_cheaper_venues_cannot_exhibit`, `recorded_at`); the `cheaper-faithful-method-found` counterpart verdict; PROVIDING the venue is the run's job. Enforced by `verify-target-element-measured`'s `stuck-without-verification-plan` gap (a `cannot-verify-without-deploy` verdict with no plan naming ≥ 2 venues and a `chosen_venue`). Pointed at from `integration` / `qa-replayer` / `expensive-verification-debugging` / `dev-api-integration-testing` / `playwright-user-flows` / bug-fix B5. |
| `ct6-agent-core` | **(v4.0.0)** The rules every CT6 agent carries, preloaded into each of the 39 agents through its `skills:` frontmatter (never invoked by hand; `user-invocable: false`): the five canonical boilerplate blocks — operating context, forbidden git operations, checkpoint discipline, operating principles (the eight ETHOS principles), reporting and escalation — rendered between `<!-- ct6:block:<id>:begin/end -->` fences by `scripts/setup/compile_skills.py` from `scripts/setup/agent_boilerplate_blocks.py`, plus the deletion-discipline and project-principles pointers. No agent body carries a compiled block any more (192 removed across 39 files); `scripts/setup/sync_agent_boilerplate.py --check` guards the inverse contract. Tests read the agent SURFACE (agent file + preloaded skills) via `tests/helpers/surfaces.py`. |
| `project-principles` | **(v4.0.0)** Lane-callable discipline for a project's codified principles — the rules that outlive any one run ("every service owns its own tables", "no new ORM"). The register `<workspace>/.architect-team/principles.json` (schema `project-principles/v1`; `proposed` / `confirmed` / `retired`; a document or extracted source needs its file AND verbatim quote; soft cap 15; retirement is a verb) is rendered deterministically to `<workspace>/docs/PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md` (never hand-edited — the override guard refuses agent edits). Every option presented to a user is tagged `fits` / `violates P-xx (why)`; an autonomous run picks a fitting option and stops (writes `escalation-pending.md`) only when every option violates; precedence user instruction > confirmed principles > CT6 defaults. Engine `scripts/principles/` (`show` / `propose` / `confirm` / `add` / `retire` / `default-set` / `render` / `brief` / `extract-candidates` / `check`); command `/architect-team:principles`; Layer-3 `verify-principles-respected` over a `principles-verdicts/v1` sidecar; optional evidence field `principles_review`. Schema chapter: `skills/project-principles/references/principles-schema.md`. |
| `page-publishing` | **(v4.0.0)** Use whenever a run or a user wants a PAGE rather than terminal text — a report, dashboard, explainer, the project homepage. Two modes read from the human-owned config (`hooks/publish_config.py`; template `.architect-team-publish.example.json`): `claude` (default — the page is copied to `.architect-team/publish/outbox/` and handed to the Claude Artifact tool; no URL, no port, no upload) and `private-url` (a `secrets.token_urlsafe(24)` token; the configured adapter places the page at `<base_url>/<slug>/p/<token>/<name>`; the link + expiry recorded in `~/.architect-team/publish/registry.json`; every link printed WITH its honest boundary — no comments routed to Claude, no shared database, and over plain HTTP the token travels in clear text). Adapters under `scripts/publish/adapters/`: `local-port` (a stdlib token-checking detached server; TLS when a cert is given), `gcp-signed-url` (`gcloud storage cp` + a time-limited signed URL), `gcp-cloud-run` (GENERATES a Dockerfile + `deploy.sh`; deploys nothing — a human runs it), `nominated-host` (scp into the user's own web root). The page contract (`skills/page-publishing/references/page-contract.md`: one self-contained HTML file under 16 MB, pinned cdnjs / jsdelivr / unpkg libraries only, no secret-shaped strings) is machine-checked by `check_page` before any publish. Engine `scripts/publish/publish.py`; the one setup question in `scripts/publish/setup_question.py` (`setup.py --project-root`); command `/architect-team:publish`. |
| `project-homepage` | **(v4.0.0)** Lane-callable discipline for the per-project CT6 homepage — one self-contained static HTML page at `.architect-team/homepage/index.html` built from the files that already exist: structure (intake state, codebases, lanes), settings (every `.architect-team-*.json` at the root — KEYS and set / unset only; a key whose name contains `token` / `secret` / `key` / `password` / `api` / `auth` is never rendered), decisions (`hooks/decisions_ledger.py`), maps with freshness from `services/knowledge_server/freshness.py` (`unknowable` with the reason when the import fails, never a fabricated `current`), functions (the lineage graph), tests (the test index, the last selection, every `suite-manifest.json`) and performance (run metrics + the timing log via `scripts/homepage/timings.py` — "history, not a promise"). Records `built_at` + source hashes and reports "stale since" per source. Engine `scripts/homepage/build.py`; command `/architect-team:home` (`--rebuild` / `--stale` / `--publish` via `page-publishing`); `setup.py --project-root` builds the first page. |

### Agents (39)

**v2.1.0 — `interaction-observer` joins** (`sonnet` under the v4.0.0 role split — `fable` at the v3.32.0 uniform default; color: green): dispatched by oracle-deriver when `spec_shape: interactive-mockup` triggers; runs the mockup in headless Chrome (or reads a pre-captured snapshot for stdlib-only contexts); enumerates every interactive element; simulates each interaction; records observed_effect into the oracle spec's `interactions[]`. Bounded Write to `.architect-team/oracle-spec/<change>/interaction-evidence/`. v2.0.0 added `oracle-deriver` (Layer 1 — spec_shape walks; v2.1.0 extends with the 6th `interactive-mockup` shape and the dispatch contract for the observer) and `adversarial-reviewer` (Layer 2 — 5 role-paired shapes). The `interaction-intuiter` agent body (existing) gains the new INTENT-INFERENCE mode + the canonical 10-pattern mismatch matrix in v2.1.0.

| Agent | Model | Color | One-line purpose |
|---|---|---|---|
| system-architect | fable | blue | On-demand architecture; + 8 audit modes (Diagnostic Plan — criterion 8 executed falsification, no cycle cap (v3.66.0), Editability Map, Interaction Map, Visual Gap Synthesis, Master Review Audit, Documentation Currency Audit, Bug-Fix Generalization, Restructure Plan Audit — v3.11.0) + Interface Contract Approval (v3.48.0, a tier-3 gate). Analysis-only. |
| frontend | opus | cyan | Phase 2 frontend implementer; Playwright + visual-fidelity workflow; runs the SELECTED tests per slice (v4.0.0). |
| backend | opus | green | Phase 2 backend implementer; live dev-API integration tests; runs the SELECTED tests per slice (v4.0.0). |
| reconciler | opus | orange | Phase 4 conflict resolution; no feature code. |
| integration | opus | pink | Phase 5 cross-layer; live dev API + Playwright + the visual-fidelity sweep; enumerates a suite THROUGH the test-index selector — impacted flows + pins, not the entire dev project (v4.0.0); `blocked` only for a genuine external blocker WITH a verification plan (v3.66.0). (v3.10.0 R4d: magenta→pink.) |
| scaffold-agent | fable | purple | **On-demand utility (not pipeline-dispatched).** Generates new role-specialized agent `.md` files into `agents/` (e.g. `ml-engineer`, `mobile-ios`, `data-pipeline`) — reads existing agents as templates, validates the generated frontmatter (valid tools per the R4a vocabulary: no retired `LS`/`NotebookRead`/`Task`, `TaskList, TaskUpdate` never `TodoWrite`; valid colours; valid model incl. `inherit`; a `skills:` preload of `ct6-agent-core`). Invoke it when you need a new domain agent; it is never spawned automatically by a pipeline phase. |
| codebase-map-reviewer | fable | red | Spawned ×3 per codebase in Phase −1B — a tier-3 gate, the loop run by `workflows/ct6-converge.js`; read-only verdict over every claim in its partition (the full `git ls-files`, v3.66.0). |
| integration-explorer | fable | blue | Spawned ×3 in Phase −1C (tier 3); round-robin convergence. |
| master-synthesizer | fable | purple | Phase −1C final; merges the 3 integration drafts. |
| route-mapper | sonnet | cyan | Per frontend codebase in Phase −1B; ROUTE_MAP.md always, DESIGN_MAP.md conditionally. |
| test-completeness-verifier | sonnet | red | Phase 3 + 5; confirms unit/integration/Playwright kinds ran + the real-backend audit (the tier-2 mechanical verifier beside the adversary). |
| task-reviewer | fable | red | Phase 3; independent per-task review of a teammate's diff vs the acceptance criteria; writes the `independent_review` block — the ONE paired independent verdict of a tier-2 task gate (a third verdict only on disagreement). Read-only on source. |
| adversarial-reviewer | fable | orange | (v2.0.0) VAO Layer 2 — the paired producer≠checker adversary in 5 role-paired shapes; attacks the claim, never confirms it; writes `adversarial_review`. |
| oracle-deriver | fable | cyan | (v2.0.0) VAO Layer 1 — derives the frozen oracle spec by `spec_shape` walk (6 shapes incl. the v2.1.0 `interactive-mockup`, dispatching `interaction-observer`; v3.33.0 consumes a materialized Claude Design project); retries until accepted, no fixed cycle cap (v3.66.0). |
| interaction-observer | sonnet | green | (v2.1.0) Pass 1 of interactive-mockup discovery — runs the mockup in headless Chrome (or reads a pre-captured snapshot), enumerates every interactive element, simulates each, records `observed_effect` into the oracle spec's `interactions[]`; bounded Write to the oracle-spec interaction-evidence dir. |
| diagnostic-researcher | fable | red | Dispatched as a tier-3 panel of three via `ct6-converge` for a test-failure SR (a judgment gate by design); full-pathway trace + ranked hypotheses — since v3.66.0 EXECUTES every falsification test it authors (`falsification_executed` / `observed_output` / `status` survived, refuted or not-executable; probes under `.architect-team/diagnostic-research/<test-id>/probes/`); read-only on SOURCE only. |
| domain-researcher | fable | yellow | (v3.4.0) Dispatched by `domain-research-team` at Phase R2 — the skill runs its research panel at tier 3 (three researchers; declare → `ct6-converge` → record); parses the provided inputs AND performs mandatory outside industry / market / competitor research (`WebFetch` + `WebSearch`); rounds until agreement, owner-only escalation (v3.66.0). |
| editability-reviewer | sonnet | yellow | Dispatched per the review tier (v4.0.0): ONE paired reviewer for a slice at Phase 3 / 5 (tier 2; a second independent reviewer only on disagreement), three in parallel via `ct6-converge` for the Phase 7 sweep (tier 3). Enumerate + classify + trace every attribute UI→DB. |
| visual-capture | sonnet | cyan | Dispatched per screen-group slice under `visual-verification-team` (one by default, three only at tier 3); starts the live app, captures screenshots + computed-style data. Mechanical; no verdicts. |
| visual-analyzer | opus | red | Dispatched per capture set — the visual-verification team is a tier-2 shape (capture is the producer, ONE analyzer its paired independent checker; three only at tier 3); the objective data diff + pixel diff + code cross-check. |
| interaction-reviewer | sonnet | yellow | Dispatched per the review tier (v4.0.0; v0.9.19 origin): ONE paired reviewer for a slice at Phase 3 / 5 (tier 2; a tie-breaking third only when the two disagree), three in parallel via `ct6-converge` for the Phase 7 sweep (tier 3). Independently enumerates every interactive element AND page, classifies element wiring + page genuineness, traces element→endpoint, audits Playwright test authenticity, flags hardcoded-dynamic values. Analysis-only — read-only on source, no `Edit` of feature code. |
| interaction-intuiter | sonnet | cyan | Spawned per frontend codebase at Phase −1B (v0.9.21). Reads route + design + integration maps, enumerates interactive elements, intuits actions + candidate endpoints, assigns confidence, authors ambiguity questions. Produces `INTERACTION_INTUITION_MAP.md`; the v2.1.0 INTENT-INFERENCE mode (the 10-pattern mismatch matrix) is Pass 2 of interactive-mockup discovery. |
| bug-classifier | haiku | blue | Spawned at Phase −2 (v0.9.22). Triages the incoming requirement as `bug` / `feature` / `data-eng` (v3.50.0) / `mixed` / `unclear`. Lightweight analysis-only — the tier-1 classification seat and the one `haiku` agent. |
| bug-replicator | opus | red | Spawned per affected codebase at Phase B1 (v0.9.22). Writes + runs Playwright or backend scripts that reproduce the bug against live dev. The artifact IS the regression test. |
| qa-replayer | opus | green | Spawned at Phase B6 (v0.9.22). Re-runs reproduction artifacts against live dev after the fix is deployed. Returns `bug-resolved` / `bug-still-present` / `test-did-not-exercise-fix` (v0.9.31) / `env-failure`; a `cannot-verify-without-deploy` verdict needs a recorded verification plan (v3.66.0). |
| doc-updater | sonnet | orange | Spawned at Phase 8 / Phase B8 (v0.9.23). Reads git diff + coverage map + doc inventory, updates all stale docs. Bounded Write to inventory paths only. |
| closeout-agent | sonnet | cyan | **(v3.18.0 — CT6-6 closeout)** Session-end doc-currency worker. Reviews the working-tree diff against the requirement, runs `hooks/closeout_check.py`, and updates any stale doc itself (whole-file rewrites; bounded Write to the inventory; no Edit). Works OUTSIDE a pipeline run (no coverage-map/run-ledger), unlike doc-updater. Spawned by the `closeout` skill / PreCompact reminder / `/architect-team:closeout`. |
| mcp-design-agent | fable | purple | **(v3.20.0 — CT6-6 MCP design)** Designs a best-in-class output contract for an LLM agent embedded in an application (MCP-1…3). Enumerates the app's producer points; builds a closed contract per point via `scripts/mcp_design/output_contract.py`; binds a forced structured-output mechanism + validate-and-retry. Bounded Write to `.architect-team/mcp-design/`; never writes the app's code. |
| fix-sensibility-checker | fable | orange | Spawned at Phase B6b (v0.9.29). Computes impact set from fix's git diff, authors + runs minimal Playwright sensibility flows, routes nonsensical items as fresh SRs with `origin.kind: fix-regression`; repeated fix-regressions are an observability signal answered from a different angle, never a count (v3.66.0). |
| flow-explorer | sonnet | cyan | Dispatched at Phase U3 (v0.9.29) per the review tier — one by default, three only when the gate is tier 3. Reads persona + site maps + literal flow, proposes 10-15 additional Playwright user-flow specs exercising adjacent capabilities. |
| flow-executor | opus | green | Dispatched at Phase U6 (v0.9.29) per the review tier — one runs every distilled Playwright flow against the live target by default (the authored flow's expected effect is the producer side, the executor its paired independent check), a second re-runs disputed flows, three only when the gate is tier 3; documents per-flow outcome. |
| prompt-refiner | fable | orange | Spawned at Phase R2 (v0.9.33). Grades a free-text prompt on 6 axes (incl. `scope-fidelity`, v1.4.0), generates codebase-grounded clarifying questions as a batch — never a cap (v3.66.0). |
| mini-qa | opus | cyan | Single QA agent for the mini variant (v0.10.0). Spawned at Phase M5; reads the architect's `## QA Guidance` contract; runs unit + integration + ≤3 Playwright on the live dev URL; emits a `qa-verdict.json` with green/red and a per-criterion table. Read-only on source. |
| test-run-watcher | sonnet | cyan | (v3.3.0) The passive test-run watcher over a Local / CI / production-QA adapter (`test-run-monitor`); log-only — no interrupts, no SR filing, no gating; no default budget (v3.66.0). |
| monitor-synthesizer | sonnet | cyan | (v3.3.0) Synthesizes the watchers' findings into the per-run monitor report (flake / regression / environmental / new); never files SRs, never gates. |
| structure-analyst | fable | blue | **(v3.11.0)** Dispatched at structure-optimization S2 — a tier-3 judgment panel of three by design (the converged proposal is the map the whole plan rests on); independent restructure drafts carrying a FULL file partition (movement table + stays, self-checked vs git ls-files). Bounded Write to the run drafts dir. |
| reference-tracer | sonnet | orange | **(v3.11.0)** Dispatched at S4 per the review tier — one tracer per non-overlapping shard by default, three only at tier 3; mechanically closes each movement's reference set (imports/config/build/ci/docs/string-path/test + outbound relative imports + forced refactors) with file:line evidence + a mandatory search_log. |
| structure-adversary | fable | red | **(v3.11.0)** Dispatched per S5 round — tier 2 on each attack round (ONE adversary paired against the closure, a second independent verdict on disagreement) and a tier-3 panel of three via `ct6-converge` on the two confirming clean rounds that end S5; refutation-only (modalities the tracers did not log, partition re-run from scratch, migration-order/tooling/runtime-reference attacks, delete-dead alive-checks). |
| endpoint-tracer | sonnet | cyan | **(v3.8.0 — lineage P1)** Spawned by `endpoint-trace-mapping`. Produces the per-endpoint internal call-trace + the CDLG via a two-layer extraction contract; reconciles every asserted edge against the existing `code-path-witness.json` runtime witness (`witness_gate`). Emits the graph that `hooks/lineage_graph.py` validates. The live polyglot extraction is the runtime job (kill-gated, NOT claimed proven); the deterministic schema/ID/witness/freshness/cost machinery it targets is unit-tested; the depth cap is a RENDERING cap only (v3.66.0). |

> **Model (v4.0.0 — the ROLE split; every Model cell above is read from the agent's frontmatter):** the shipped state is `scripts/setup/set_default_model.py --split role` (policy `role-split`, the `ROLE_SPLIT_MODELS` table; an unclassified stem fails safe to `fable`): **`fable` on the 15 judgment seats** (`system-architect`, `adversarial-reviewer`, `task-reviewer`, `oracle-deriver`, `master-synthesizer`, `codebase-map-reviewer`, `integration-explorer`, `diagnostic-researcher`, `domain-researcher`, `fix-sensibility-checker`, `structure-analyst`, `structure-adversary`, `prompt-refiner`, `scaffold-agent`, `mcp-design-agent`), **`opus` on the 9 delivery / execution seats** (`backend`, `frontend`, `integration`, `reconciler`, `mini-qa`, `bug-replicator`, `qa-replayer`, `flow-executor`, `visual-analyzer`), **`sonnet` on the 14 mechanical verification / extraction / observation / documentation seats** (`test-completeness-verifier`, `editability-reviewer`, `interaction-reviewer`, `interaction-observer`, `interaction-intuiter`, `visual-capture`, `test-run-watcher`, `monitor-synthesizer`, `reference-tracer`, `route-mapper`, `endpoint-tracer`, `flow-explorer`, `doc-updater`, `closeout-agent`), and **`haiku` on `bug-classifier`**.
