Every agent additionally preloads `skills/ct6-agent-core` through its `skills:` frontmatter (the five canonical boilerplate blocks — no agent body carries a compiled block; `scripts/setup/sync_agent_boilerplate.py --check` guards it) and its `tools:` list names `TaskList, TaskUpdate` (never `TodoWrite`, which Claude 5 ships disabled). How many of a reviewer run per gate is decided by `skills/team-spawning-and-review-gates/references/review-tiers.md`, not by the agent: the ×3 / panel-of-three in the rows above marks the tier-3 gates — map convergence, architecture approval, the master review, the research panels, the structure-optimization S2 drafts and confirming S5 rounds, and the Phase 7 editability / interaction sweeps — while every Phase 3 / 5 slice gate is tier 2 (the producer + ONE paired reviewer; a third verdict only on disagreement) and the UX explorers / executors, visual capture / analysis and reference tracers run one per slice by default. History: v3.32.0 pinned all 39 to uniform `fable`; v3.43.0 put the 12 delivery + adversarial agents on `opus` (`DELIVERY_ADVERSARIAL_AGENTS`, still reproducible via `--split delivery`, policy `deliver-opus-split`); the v4.0.0 role split then moved 18 agents — 14 fable → sonnet, `bug-classifier` fable → haiku, and `adversarial-reviewer` / `fix-sensibility-checker` / `structure-adversary` opus → fable. The uniform Opus fallback for a harness that predates the `fable` alias remains `python3 scripts/setup/set_default_model.py --model opus`; the service tier resolves fable→opus via `service_config.resolve_model`. **Gateway secondary role split (v3.35.0 — a DIFFERENT axis, independent of the above):** when the external-LLM gateway has a secondary provider available, the same lever's `--split secondary` (or `setup.py --codex` / `CT6_CODEX_56_AVAILABLE=1`) moves the 21 development/code-checking/testing agents (`AGENT_ROLES`) to the gateway-served secondary while the 18 architecture/control/design agents stay on `fable`; **v3.40.0** writes the spawn-compatible id `claude-haiku-4-5` (gateway-impersonated to the chosen secondary — `openai` → `gpt-5.6-sol`; `zai` → `glm-5.2` via `https://api.z.ai/api/paas/v4`) under policy `secondary-split`. That split is install-time policy applied to the INSTALLED plugin copy and never rewrites the committed tree.

> **Note (v4.0.0):** all 39 agents are rowed above — the six that earlier editions disclosed only in prose (`adversarial-reviewer`, `oracle-deriver`, `interaction-observer`, `domain-researcher`, `test-run-watcher`, `monitor-synthesizer`) gained rows in this refresh, and every Model cell was re-read from the agent's frontmatter after the role split. The live agent count is **39** (v3.11.0 added `structure-analyst` / `reference-tracer` / `structure-adversary`; v3.8.0 added `endpoint-tracer`; v3.4.0 `domain-researcher`; v3.3.0 `test-run-watcher` + `monitor-synthesizer`).

### Commands (30)

- `architect-team` — runs the Phase −2 → 8 pipeline against EITHER a requirements folder OR a plain-language requirement typed directly as prose (v0.9.17). Flags: `--no-commit` / `--no-push` / `--no-compact` / `--allow-push-to-default` / `--proposal-first` / `--bug-fix` / `--feature-only` / `--no-refine`.
- `bug-fix` — faster bug-focused variant (v0.9.22). Drives through replicate → reproduce-test → propose → fix → QA-replay loop. Equivalent to `--bug-fix` flag on the main command. **(v4.0.0)** `--full-data` opts out of sample-first iteration when the fix targets a subset of a big table (default: profile → stratify → sample → iterate → scale, per `skills/data-eng-pipeline/references/sample-first.md`).
- `data-eng` — **(v3.50.0)** entry point for the data-engineering lane (Run B). On the bug-fix.md template (dispatch banner / merged-worktree cleanup / auto-worktree), invokes the `data-eng-pipeline` skill (phases D−1…D8). Equivalent to the `--data-eng` flag on the main command; the Phase −2 classifier's fifth kind `data-eng` routes here from the front door. **(v4.0.0)** `--full-data` opts out of sample-first iteration (a D-phase change to a subset of a big table otherwise runs against a stratified sample first; engine `scripts/data_dictionary/sampling.py`).
- `ux-test` — UX test builder (v0.9.29). Takes persona + objectives + target site + credentials env-var. Drives through site mapping → literal flow → flow-explorer agents per the review tier (one by default, three only at tier 3) → distillation → flow-executor agents per the tier → consensus → bug routing.
- `refine-prompt` — standalone prompt refinement (v0.9.33). Grades and iteratively refines a free-text prompt without running the pipeline.
- `architect-team-setup` — installs openspec CLI, pytest+httpx, Playwright+chromium; checks Claude Code ≥ 2.1.32 + the Agent Teams flag. **(v4.0.0)** `REQUIRED_PLUGINS` is EMPTY — `superpowers` / `cartographer` / `bauplan` are CONDITIONAL (reported + remediated, never gating; `ralph-loop` retired); `check_task_tools` writes `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` into `~/.claude/settings.json` with the same consent flow as the Teams flag (a Claude 5 session ships without `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate` otherwise); `--project-root <path>` runs the per-project steps — `check_publish_config` asks the one page-publishing question (`scripts/publish/setup_question.py`; writes `.architect-team-publish.json` once, never overwriting an existing one) and `build_first_homepage` renders `.architect-team/homepage/index.html`. **(v3.38.0)** carries the `Ask for missing keys — never punt` wrapper section — on an absent-key state from the external-LLM path the executing agent asks in-session via AskUserQuestion (capture-and-apply, with a prior `--yes` carrying over as `--activate` consent) or records an explicit decline via the installer's `decline` subcommand; never the bare script remediation as the only path.
- `visual-qa` — on-demand visual-fidelity audit → the visual-verification-team gate.
- `mempalace-install` — installs the MemPalace CLI + prints the MCP wire-up.
- `memory` — ad-hoc MemPalace `search` / `mine` / `status` / `wake-up` / `sweep`.
- `editability-audit` — on-demand editability-completeness audit.
- `mini` — entry point for the mini variant (v0.10.0). Runs `mini-architect-team-pipeline` (Phase M0 → M8) on the live dev environment with a single architect, a single `mini-qa` agent, and auto-merge to main on green.
- `mini-review-sweep` — batched heavyweight review (v0.10.0). Consumes commit messages tagged with the `Mini-Run:` trailer and dispatches the full architect-team review surface (visual / editability / interaction / docs-currency) across the batch, decoupled from the inner mini loop.
- `optimize-structure` — **(v3.11.0)** produce an adversarially-verified codebase-restructure PLAN via the `structure-optimization` skill (`[codebase-path | --all] [--objective] [--execute] [--no-commit] [--no-push] [--no-compact]`); plan-only (never moves a source file); `--execute` hands the strict-validated change to the implementing pipeline.
- `cleanup-worktrees` — explicit on-demand cleanup of merged `architect-team/*` worktrees (v1.3.0). Walks `git worktree list`, identifies any whose branch is merged into `origin/main`, and removes them (worktree + branch). Excludes the current worktree by default (safety). Flags: `--dry-run` (preview without filesystem changes), `--against <ref>` (override the default `origin/main` comparison). Invokes the same `cleanup_merged_worktrees()` helper that the six lane commands (`architect-team` / `bug-fix` / `mini` / `data-eng` / `ux-test` / `playwright-suite`) auto-fire as their first action.
- `absorb-phenotype` — (v2.3.0) absorb any arbitrary codebase into a new labeled phenotype via the `phenotype-absorption` skill (`<path> --label <name>`); analyze read-only → generalize a blueprint + parameterized scaffold → validate the phenotype.json → index it.
- `visual-to-api` — force the visual-to-API design pipeline against a visual codebase (UI present, requirements absent/partial); drives context discovery → per-persona research → page catalog → backend design with 3-reviewer convergence per stage.
- `classify-test-prod-safety` — (v2.17.0) mass-classify every Playwright/QA test file as `@prod-safe` or `@not-prod-safe`; default `--dry-run`, `--write-annotations` injects the annotations; ambiguous cases escalate.
- `discipline-status` — (v2.18.0) report which CT6 codebase disciplines are applied/stale for the current codebase (the discipline registry); read-only, `--apply` auto-runs auto-apply-safe disciplines. The same logic Phase 0.1 fires at run start.
- `inject` — (v2.19.0) inject an in-flight clarification / scope amendment / separable parallel problem into the running pipeline; lands in the per-run inbox and is picked up on the orchestrator's next wake (v3.16.0 adds the `parallel-problem` concurrent-lane classification).
- `monitor-tests` — (v3.3.0) run the passive test-run monitor team (local / CI / production-APM adapters); log-only, no mid-run interrupts, no auto-SR filing, no gating.
- `closeout` — **(v3.18.0)** run the end-of-session closeout double-check (CO-1…CO-3) — review what changed vs the requirement, confirm the documentation-currency inventory is current, and update any stale doc; `--check` is read-only. The same check the PreCompact hook fires automatically.
- `logit` — **(v3.21.0)** the manual Logit / Helpdesk triage report after a bad session (HD-1…3); consent + privacy level (full/summary/off), captures the issues agents couldn't solve, and builds a privacy-applied triage submission via `scripts/helpdesk/logit.py` (the actual send is the server tier).
- `librarian-install` — **(v3.29.0)** full-lifecycle installer/CLI for the CT6 Librarian topic-research curation daemon (install / status / add-topic / list-topics / remove-topic / run-once / uninstall); provisions state under `~/.architect-team/librarian/`, wires the real Anthropic LLM when a key resolves (else provisioned-but-disabled + `--enable`), and PRINTS the per-OS boot descriptor. **(v3.38.0)** gains the `Ask for missing keys — never punt` section (capture-and-apply via the env-scoped `--enable` run, or an explicit recorded decline) + the `decline [--clear]` subcommand and the `--interactive-prompts` / `--re-ask-keys` flags.
- `knowledge-install` — **(v3.49.0)** installer/CLI for the standing repo-knowledge MCP server (`services/knowledge_server/`) via `scripts/setup/install_knowledge_server.py` on the librarian-install template: provisions `~/.architect-team/knowledge/`, GENERATES + PRINTS the portable `python <repo>/services/knowledge_server/server.py --repo-root <repo>` MCP registration (never auto-edits the user's MCP config), `--check-only` / `--json` / `status` / `print-registration` / `uninstall --purge` parity, and a confirm-serving step in the `confirm_gateway_serving` mold — "serving on this machine" ONLY after a real `tools/call` round trip against the actually-started server, never "deployed". A repo without generated sidecars yields a DORMANT (not false) registration, reported honestly.
- `playwright-suite` — **(v3.65.0)** build or refresh a frontend codebase's durable, persona-grouped Playwright suite via the `playwright-suite-builder` skill (P0–P9): inventory vs the committed `suite-manifest.json`, existing-test review through the standing verification gates, suite-wide gap analysis + gap-fill, debug-flow registration, `dev`/`prod` project grouping under default-deny prod safety, and an engine-validated manifest write. Flags: `--no-commit` / `--no-push` / `--no-compact` / `--allow-push-to-default` / `--proposal-first` / `--no-worktree`. Auto-commits + pushes on a clean P9 pass.
- `status` — read-only "where am I": current dispatch mode (Agent Teams vs subagents fallback), active `architect-team` worktrees, open solution requirements, last completed run.
- `principles` — **(v4.0.0)** show / add / retire / extract the project's codified principles through `scripts/principles/cli.py` (`show` | `add "<statement>" --scope <scope>` | `retire P-xx --reason "..."` | `extract <file>`; `--workspace <path>`): the register `.architect-team/principles.json` and its rendered twin `<workspace>/docs/PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md` are written together, never by hand; read-only by default; `extract` proposes candidates from a document with file + verbatim quote for the user to confirm.
- `publish` — **(v4.0.0)** publish a self-contained HTML page where the project's human-owned publish config says pages live (`<file.html> [--name <page-name>] [--slug <project-slug>] [--force] | --revoke <token> | --list | --status | --check <file.html>`): `claude` mode (default) hands the file to the Claude Artifact tool; `private-url` mode mints a tokened link through the configured adapter (`local-port` / `gcp-signed-url` / `gcp-cloud-run` / `nominated-host`) and prints it WITH its honest boundary; `--check` runs the page contract alone; `--force` overrides contract findings but never the secret finding. Engine `scripts/publish/publish.py`.
- `home` — **(v4.0.0)** build, inspect or publish the per-project CT6 homepage (`.architect-team/homepage/index.html` — structure, settings, decisions, map freshness, functions, tests and performance in one static page): `--stale` reports staleness per source (`built_at` + source hashes) without rebuilding, `--rebuild` forces a rebuild, `--publish` hands the page to `page-publishing`. Engine `scripts/homepage/build.py`; the timing panel's numbers come from `scripts/homepage/timings.py` ("history, not a promise").
- `unlock` — **(v4.1.0)** the USER's verb for releasing a completion hold (`/architect-team:unlock <why, in your own words>`; `disable-model-invocation: true`, so the model cannot invoke it). Inspects what holds the turn (open harness tasks, unresolved asks, declared and `converge:<kind>` gates, the active run marker), then — under ONE durable record `.architect-team/releases/unlock-<ts>.json` carrying the user's words, the session, what held and what was done — retires the session's open tasks through `open_work.py retire --force` (every task carrying the reason), resolves the open asks with the record as evidence, WAIVES every unsatisfied declared gate (`waived_at` / `waived_by: user` / `evidence_path` → the record; never faked as satisfied), stands the run marker down (`pipeline-stand-down.md` with the user's words) and appends one decisions-ledger line. Engine `hooks/completion_unlock.py` (`inspect` / `release` / `status`); the release verb is sanctioned only inside this command's own turn (Arm 5 of `hooks/pretool_skill_gate.py`), so an agent held by the lock can never reach for it.

### Hooks (9 enforcement scripts + 10 wired events) + the shared substrates (evidence schema / lock layer / run continuity / open work / completion unlock / git identity / deploy + publish config / timings + decisions ledgers) + the `hooks/vao/` package

- **`hooks/hooks.json`** — wires **10 hook events across 9 enforcement scripts**: `PreToolUse[*]` → `pretool_skill_gate.py` (v3.15.0), `PreToolUse[Edit|Write|NotebookEdit]` → `pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py`, `PreToolUse[Bash]` → `deletion_guard.py` (**v4.0.0**), `PostToolUse[TaskUpdate]` → `review-gate-task.py`, `PostToolUse[Skill]` → `pretool_skill_gate.py` (v3.30.0 — the engagement recorder that arms the sticky run marker when a run-driving Skill fires), `TaskCompleted[*]` → `review-gate-task.py` + `timings_log.py`, `SubagentStop[*]` → `teammate-idle-check.py` + `timings_log.py`, `TeammateIdle[*]` → `teammate-idle-check.py`, `Stop[*]` → `pipeline-completion-audit.py` + `timings_log.py`, `PreCompact[*]` → `precompact-closeout.py` (v3.18.0 — the first `PreCompact` wiring), `SessionStart[*]` → `sessionstart-run-continuity.py` (v3.30.0 — the first `SessionStart` wiring), `SubagentStart[*]` → `timings_log.py` (**v4.0.0**), `TaskCreated[*]` → `timings_log.py` (**v4.0.0**). (**v1.0.0** added `TaskCompleted` / `TeammateIdle` for teams-mode dispatch.) All `async: false`. Every command string uses the v2.16.0 detect-once form `$(command -v python3 || command -v python) "…"` (v3.9.3 — A1: the interpreter is selected once so a BLOCKing hook runs exactly once and a `python3`-only host never drops the block). Both evidence hooks branch internally on payload shape. The hooks stay GLOBAL here rather than in skill frontmatter (a v4.0.0 decision): the completion lock must survive a session boundary where no skill has been invoked yet; the per-call cost measured 50–75 ms and is not the problem the proposal thought it might be.
- **`hooks/review_evidence_schema.py`** — NOT a hook; the shared single source of truth for the evidence contract (`SCHEMA_VERSION` = 7, `REQUIRED_EVIDENCE_FIELDS` = the 17 teammate self-review fields incl. `ui_interaction_review` (v0.9.19) + the 5 v2.0.0 Verified Agent Output fields, `OPTIONAL_VAO_FIELDS` (6 — `interactions_honored_review` (v2.1.0) + `live_verification_review` (v2.2.0) + `appearance_scope_review` (v3.14.0) + `check_integrity_review` (v3.47.0) + `claim_instrument_binding_review` (v3.59.0) + `principles_review` (v4.0.0; like `check_integrity_review`, a passing value MUST cite the Layer-3 verdict it derived from)), `REQUIRED_INDEPENDENT_REVIEW_FIELDS` for the v5 `independent_review` block, the `VALID_*` value sets incl. `VALID_UI_INTERACTION_VALUES`, `safe_id()`, `validate_evidence()`, plus **v1.0.0's `_detect_trigger_mode(payload)`** that classifies an incoming hook payload as `"subagents"` (PostToolUse(TaskUpdate) / SubagentStop) or `"teams"` (TaskCompleted / TeammateIdle) by event-type / tool-name inspection). `validate_evidence()` rejects evidence missing the `independent_review` block or whose `independent_review.reviewer == teammate`, blocks a `*_review` field set to `fail`, and requires the `_note` on an `n/a`. Both evidence hooks import it (added v0.9.9 — before that the two hooks carried drifted copies). **v3.47.0** adds `normalize_task_id` + isinstance guards, and the gate hook wraps validation fail-closed — a validator crash blocks rather than tracebacks-and-allows. **v3.44.0** adds the conditionally-required `frontend_impact_e2e_review` (a frontend-impact diff can't be marked done on a unit test alone; `hooks/frontend_impact.py` supplies the detector); **v3.55.0** scopes its note-escape to no-runnable-UI-only — the run-level `_audit_frontend_e2e` Stop-hook arm is the backstop no per-task note can escape.
- **`hooks/locks.py`** — NOT a hook; the **v1.0.0 cross-session lock layer**. Four stdlib-only functions: `acquire_lock(scope_glob, ttl_seconds, run_id)` writes a `{holder, scope_glob, acquired_at, ttl_seconds, run_id}` JSON lock at `.architect-team/locks/<scope-hash>.json` (returns the lock id, or `blocked: <existing-lock-id>` when an existing non-stale lock's scope intersects the requested one); `release_lock(lock_id)` removes the lock file (idempotent); `detect_stale()` returns the ids of locks whose `acquired_at + ttl_seconds` is in the past or whose JSON is malformed / missing required fields (auto-released on the next intersecting acquire); `globs_intersect(a, b)` returns True when two path-globs could match a common path. Used by every pipeline Lead to claim its file scope before dispatching teammates — two concurrent `/architect-team` invocations in separate Claude Code sessions queue (overlapping scope) or proceed truly parallel (disjoint scope). **v3.8.0 (lineage P4)** adds `cdlg_overlap(...)` — a call-graph-closure-intersection check that flags two slices touching a shared HOT CALLEE (a function both call-closures reach), not just a shared file; the parallel-dispatch decision can now serialize work that collides at the function level even when the edited file sets are disjoint.
- **`hooks/review-gate-task.py`** — `PostToolUse(TaskUpdate)` (subagents mode) OR `TaskCompleted` (teams mode, **v1.0.0**). Blocks a teammate task flipping to `completed` without valid review-gate evidence. Reads the same `.architect-team/reviews/<task-id>.json` path in both modes; same v7 validation; same exit 0 = allow / 2 = block semantics. **v3.9.3 (A8/A9):** decodes stdin via `sys.stdin.buffer.read().decode("utf-8","replace")` (UTF-8-safe on cp1252) and treats an `OSError` on the evidence read as a blocking gap (fails closed, never tracebacks-and-skips). **v3.47.0 (the active-run unmanifested-task completion gate):** during an ACTIVE run, the run's own session flipping a task to `completed` when NO teammate manifest registers it (`expected_review_evidence` / `shared_task_id` / `task_ids`) is BLOCKED with remediation — run-session scoped via the session id `run_continuity.py` records at engagement (teams mode shares ONE session id); kill-switch `CT6_TASK_GATE_DISABLED`; fail-open outside active runs and for teammate sessions; a non-dict manifest skips-with-warn; a path-shaped evidence id is flagged as an unusable entry rather than silently matched. **v4.0.0:** `_completing_task_subject` resolves the harness task dir through `open_work.session_task_dir` — `<tasks-root>/<full session id>/` (the layout Claude Code writes today) else the legacy `session-<sid8>/` — so the active-run task gate reads the directory that actually exists; every infrastructure failure returns None and falls back to legacy behaviour (fail-open on infrastructure, never a new block).
- **`hooks/teammate-idle-check.py`** — `SubagentStop` (subagents mode) OR `TeammateIdle` (teams mode, **v1.0.0**). On a teammate going idle, validates every `expected_review_evidence` task. Same enforcement in both modes. Blocks on a corrupt matched manifest (v0.9.9 — was fail-open).
- **`hooks/pipeline-completion-audit.py`** — `Stop` hook + standalone `--check`. Trigger is unchanged in v1.0.0 — same Stop event in both modes. Gates the orchestrator's terminal state: blocks a stop while `.architect-team/` shows an incomplete run (open SRs, a test-failure SR with no diagnostic plan, an unsatisfied editability loop, a test-completeness debt, an unverified visual reconciliation, a failing Phase 7 master-review audit verdict, a failing Phase 8 documentation-currency audit verdict). Escalation-marker- and `stop_hook_active`-aware; fails open on any error. **v3.8.0 (unbounded solving):** the global iteration ceiling (`ITERATION_CEILING` / `_audit_iteration_ceiling`) was REMOVED — the audit no longer halts a run for exceeding a loop count. Its remaining checks now act as a **non-halting worklist**: they still define "not yet success" (so the run keeps iterating until everything is green), but they never abort. The ONLY terminal pause is the `escalation-pending.md` marker, written exclusively for required owner-input. **v3.9.2 (deterministic openspec gate):** NEW `_audit_openspec_validation(root, at)` — once a Phase-7 `master-review/audit-*.json` verdict exists, the hook **independently re-runs** `openspec validate --all --strict --json` from the repo root and BLOCKS the commit on any invalid active change (the gate no longer trusts the orchestrator's self-reported `openspec_validate` field); best-effort no-op when there is no `openspec/` workspace, no `openspec` CLI on `PATH`, or on subprocess error. Wired into `audit()`. **v3.9.3 (A8):** decodes stdin via `sys.stdin.buffer.read().decode("utf-8","replace")` (UTF-8-safe on cp1252); **A7:** every text-mode subprocess call carries `encoding="utf-8", errors="replace"`. **v3.30.0 (continuation guard):** an ACTIVE `active-run.json` marker (via `run_continuity.py`) now blocks a Stop even when the worklist is momentarily clean — an ENGAGED session stays blocked while the run makes progress (unbounded) and auto-escalates (writes `escalation-pending.md` + allows) after `CT6_MAX_NO_PROGRESS_STOPS` (default 3) consecutive no-progress blocks; a non-engaged session keeps the legacy one-nudge semantics + a resume-via-Skill directive. Kill-switch `CT6_RUN_CONTINUITY_DISABLED`; fails open. `--check` unchanged. **v3.47.0 (three evidence-integrity arms):** `_audit_check_integrity` — when the run's diff adds test files, EVERY `verify-check-can-fail` verdict in the verdict directory must pass (latest-wins removed; notes surface informationally); `_audit_declared_gates` — every entry in `.architect-team/declared-gates.json` must be satisfied with contained, non-whitespace evidence or the stop BLOCKS quoting the gate's `declaration_text`; `_audit_spec_currency` — every in-flight teammate manifest's `spec_fingerprint` must match the active change dir's current fingerprint or carry a re-brief record (explicit `teammate` field; token-boundary attribution), with `_audit_manifest_id_hygiene` flagging malformed manifest ids; all three fail open when their artifacts are absent. **v3.55.0 (`_audit_frontend_e2e` — the frontend-E2E loop-exit arm):** aggregates `files_changed` across `.architect-team/reviews/*.json`, runs the EXISTING `frontend_impact.changed_files_touch_frontend`, and for a frontend-impacting run REQUIRES a genuine passing E2E verdict per slice at `.architect-team/frontend-e2e/<slice>-verdict.json` — a missing / non-passing verdict, or a slice that touched a real UI file but produced only a review-gate note, BLOCKS completion; genuineness enforced by CALLING `verify-frontend-e2e-loop-exit` (a populated-but-fake verdict is caught, not just a missing one); kill-switch `CT6_FRONTEND_E2E_GATE_DISABLED`; no-op for a no-frontend run / outside a run. **v3.56.0 (the turn-boundary COMPLETION LOCK):** a new `_completion_lock` evaluation in `main()` — the FIRST arm that is deliberately NOT gated behind `_is_real_run`, so it reaches plain Agent Teams sessions that never invoke `/architect-team` (the reported failure). It delegates to `hooks/open_work.py::evaluate_completion_lock` and BLOCKS the stop while the harness task list, the derived ask-ledger, or the one-line-of-state turn-output rule shows open work. **Placement is the requirement, not an implementation detail** — the lock is evaluated ABOVE the non-engaged early return, above the `CT6_MAX_NO_PROGRESS_STOPS` budget's `return 0`, above the escalation-marker return, and above `_in_progress_is_fresh`; each of those is a file the AGENT writes, so honouring any of them as a bypass would restore exactly the self-asserted exit the lock removes (pinned by tests that fail when the lock is moved). It COMPOSES the v3.30.0 continuation guard's block text (`CONTINUE`, the worklist, the post-compact skill-reload directive) and heartbeats the run marker, so an engaged run loses nothing, while deliberately NOT calling `note_continuation_block` — advancing that counter would burn the guard's auto-escalation budget on a run that was in fact progressing. Four per-source kill-switches (`CT6_COMPLETION_LOCK_DISABLED` master / `CT6_TASK_LIST_GATE_DISABLED` / `CT6_ASK_LEDGER_GATE_DISABLED` / `CT6_TURN_OUTPUT_GATE_DISABLED`); a missing `open_work.py` fails open but emits a LOUD stderr warning on every Stop (a gate that disappears silently is worse than no gate), and the import site catches `Exception`, not just `ImportError`, so a `SyntaxError` in the substrate cannot take the other audit arms down with it. Split failure semantics: the lock's own code raising fails OPEN; an unreadable SOURCE blocks and names the file. HONEST BOUNDARY — `TaskUpdate(status="deleted")` unlinks the task file, so the lock then reads a clean-empty directory and releases; the ask-ledger mitigates only partially (deleting lanes does not erase the user's asks). **v3.64.0 (inherited-task-debt):** the lock blocks on LIVE open tasks only — open tasks whose file mtime has aged past the abandonment window surface in an inherited-stale FYI section (store dir named; the substrate path still never printed, per F-A) instead of wedging sessions that inherited a terminated run's list; the kill-switch section states the launch-env fact and names both staleness windows; and the marker heartbeat on a lock block fires only for ENGAGED sessions, so an abandoned run's marker can actually cross `marker_is_stale`. **v3.61.0 (`_audit_release_backing`):** a release run (one that touched `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` and whose CHANGELOG top entry matches the plugin version) cannot close while `scripts/docs_tooling/changelog_check.py --require-measurements` fails — the published suite count must have a recorded bracket under `docs/measurements/`; silent on ordinary work and in the authoring window. **v3.66.0 (`_audit_diagnosis_falsified`):** an open diagnostic-research SR holds the run until the architect's `diagnostic-plan-<ts>.json` sidecar exists and validates under `verify-diagnosis-falsified` (resolved SRs never retro-gated; tool unimportable ⇒ fail-open; kill-switch `CT6_DIAGNOSIS_GATE_DISABLED`). **v4.0.0 (`_audit_required_reads` — the referencing mandate's hall monitor):** runs the 26th Layer-3 tool `verify-required-reads` over the active run's tool-call ledger and the per-skill INDEX declarations; a `required-read-skipped` is a MEASURED GAP printed with chapter + phase, blocking only under `CT6_REQUIRED_READS_BLOCKING=1` (the tool proves a file was opened, not understood — blocking on that would be the overclaim); kill-switch `CT6_REQUIRED_READS_GATE_DISABLED`; fail-open without a run id or when the tool is unimportable. **v4.0.0 (the task-tool floor):** the completion lock reads whichever harness task-dir layout exists (`<root>/<full session id>/` first, then `session-<first 8>/`) and treats a missing DEFAULT task root as an advisory `unavailable` source — the stop is then decided on the ask-ledger + the run marker and the block text says so — while a REDIRECTED `CT6_TASKS_ROOT` that is missing still blocks (the v3.60.0 F13 contract). **v4.1.0 (quiet holds — the channel changed, not the hold):** every hold is delivered as hook JSON on stdout with exit 0 through `_emit_hold` — `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` carries the agent's directive (the worklist, the CONTINUE text, the post-compact reload directive), Claude Code keeps the turn open through the same loop protections (the `stop_hook_active` input and the consecutive-continuation cap) and labels it `Stop hook feedback` in the transcript, so the user's screen stays clean; `CT6_STOP_HOLD_MODE=block` restores `decision: block` for a harness that does not honour `additionalContext` on Stop, `CT6_STOP_USER_NOTICE=1` adds a one-line `systemMessage`. `_audit_declared_gates` also holds on the `converge:<kind>` gates `scripts/converge/record.py` declares before a reviewer loop, and READS a cited `ct6-converge/v1` record rather than just finding it (`_converge_record_problem`): a `satisfied_at` whose record says `converged: false` can only be a hand-edited registry and is refused with the record's own reasons. A gate the USER waived through `/architect-team:unlock` (`waived_at` / `waived_by: user` / `evidence_path` → a release record under `.architect-team/releases/`) is accepted only when that record really exists. `tests/test_stop_hold_channel.py` + `tests/test_converge_gate.py`.
- **`hooks/pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py`** — the **PreToolUse** hook (fires on `Edit` / `Write` / `NotebookEdit`). Guards against a teammate unilaterally overriding a marker / protected file without the sanctioned override path. **v3.56.0 (ADV-3 / ADV-5 — completion-lock ground-truth immutability):** `_targets_completion_lock_ground_truth()` additionally refuses any agent `Edit` / `Write` / `NotebookEdit` whose target is the ask-ledger (`ask-ledger.json` under a `.architect-team/` parent — matched on name AND parent so an unrelated same-named file is not caught) or any path under the resolved harness task root (via `open_work.tasks_root()`, with a `~/.claude/tasks` fallback). It fires UNCONDITIONALLY, like the deploy-config rule and for the same reason: the reported failure happens in sessions with no CT6 run at all, so a run-gated check would miss exactly the case that matters. Closing a task through the `TaskUpdate` tool stays entirely legitimate — what is forbidden is reaching around the harness to rewrite the record, which would also bypass `review-gate-task.py` (a `PostToolUse(TaskUpdate)` hook that only ever sees the TOOL, never a filesystem write). The block message names which source was targeted and points the HUMAN at the four kill-switches. HONEST BOUNDARY: this raises the cost and makes a bypass deliberate and visible; an agent with unrestricted `Bash` can still write these files by other means, and no hook-tier guard can prevent that. Decodes stdin via `sys.stdin.buffer.read().decode("utf-8","replace")` (v3.9.3 A8). Exit 0 = allow / 2 = block. Exercised by `tests/test_pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py`. **v3.60.0 / v4.0.0 — more human-owned ground truth, each refused UNCONDITIONALLY like the v3.44.0 `.architect-team-deploy.json` rule:** `_targets_measurement_artifact` refuses any agent write under a `docs/measurements/` parent pair (the suite brackets and the v4.0.0 instruction-budget artifacts are the durable evidence a release count rests on; the backslash spelling was normalized in v3.64.0); **v4.0.0** adds `_targets_existing_publish_config` (an EXISTING `.architect-team-publish.json` — the page-publishing opt-in is written once by the setup question, by a human) and `_targets_project_principles` (the rendered `PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md` under a target project's `docs/` comes from `.architect-team/principles.json` through `scripts/principles/` and is never hand-edited). The block text names the owner of each file and the sanctioned path to change it. **v4.1.0:** `_targets_release_record` refuses agent writes under a `.architect-team/releases/` parent pair (keyed like the measurements arm, so a project's own `releases/` dir stays out of scope) — the release records only the user's `/architect-team:unlock` command writes.
- **`hooks/pretool_skill_gate.py`** — the **PreToolUse[*]** hook (v3.15.0, narrowed v3.15.1), fires before EVERY tool call. Real-time skill-invocation hard-gate: when the session transcript's most-recent GENUINE user prompt is an unsatisfied pipeline-command request (a `/architect-team:<pipeline-cmd>` slash invocation or prose), it BLOCKS (exit 2) the first BUILD/DISPATCH tool call (`_BLOCKED_TOOLS` = `Edit`/`Write`/`NotebookEdit`/`Agent`/`Task`/`TaskCreate`/`TaskUpdate`/`TaskStop`) until a pipeline skill is engaged — read-only investigation + the command wrapper's own `Bash` setup (banner/cleanup/worktree) are NOT blocked (the v3.15.1 narrowing, after the v3.15.0 all-non-`Skill` block over-fired on the wrapper) — converting the after-the-fact Layer-6 `skill_invocation_audit.py` from DETECTION to PREVENTION. Reuses `find_skill_requests` + `COMMAND_TO_SKILLS` from `skill_invocation_audit.py` (dual-form import); scoped to the 7 pipeline-driving commands (v3.65.0 adds `playwright-suite-builder`; v3.55.2 added `data-eng-pipeline` to `_PIPELINE_SKILLS`, real-time gating the data-eng lane the v3.50.0 lane addition had left covered only by the Stop-time auditor); anchor excludes injected/meta records (`isMeta` body-echoes, `promptSource:"system"`, `isSidechain`); satisfaction = engaging ANY pipeline skill (ts-ordered for user-precedence); the `Skill` tool is always allowed; fails open on any error; tail-capped transcript read for latency. Universal — no codebase-specific strings. **v3.30.0 (sticky run arm):** while an `active-run.json` marker is ACTIVE, a user-facing session that has not invoked a pipeline skill SINCE ITS LAST COMPACT BOUNDARY is blocked from build/dispatch tools (never `Read`/`Bash`) until it re-invokes the Skill; stands down for teammate sessions (recognized by the mandatory `[CT6-TEAMMATE <name> RUN <id>]` spawn-brief first line + a fail-open brief-shape fallback), sidechains, and `complete`/`stood-down` markers. The engagement itself is recorded at the NEW `PostToolUse(Skill)` wiring (a DENIED Skill call never engages). Exercised by `tests/test_pretool_skill_gate.py` (incl. an end-to-end subprocess run + cp1252 fail-open + the v3.30.0 sticky-arm/engagement cases). **v3.61.0 (Arm 3 — task deletion, `_task_delete_check`):** during an ACTIVE run a `TaskUpdate(status="deleted")` is refused, so deleting a task no longer releases the completion lock. **v3.67.0 (Arm 4 — commit identity, `_git_identity_check`):** a Bash command whose git subcommand authors a commit (`commit` / `merge` / `revert`; `--abort` / `--no-commit` / `--dry-run` author nothing) in an active, non-stale run is refused when the checkout has no `.architect-team/git-identity.json` record, carries no `-c user.name= / -c user.email=` pair (the `hooks/git_identity.py git --` wrapper counts as carrying it), or carries a DIFFERENT identity — with the exact wrapper command in the block; payload-only, marker-scoped, `escalation-pending.md` does not stand it down, kill-switch `CT6_GIT_IDENTITY_GATE_DISABLED`. **v4.1.0 (Arm 5 — the hold release is the USER's verb):** a Bash command running `hooks/completion_unlock.py release` is refused unless the LATEST genuine user prompt in the session's transcript carries the `/architect-team:unlock` command marker — an agent held by the lock cannot reach for it as a way out, and a worker / sidechain session can never release the Lead's hold; `inspect` and `status` are read-only and never gated; unlike arms 3 and 4 this arm DOES consult the transcript (the whole question is who asked) and fails CLOSED on a missing transcript, fail-open only on an internal error; kill-switch `CT6_UNLOCK_GATE_DISABLED` (launch environment).
- **`hooks/precompact-closeout.py`** — the **`PreCompact`** hook (v3.18.0 — the first `PreCompact` wiring). Before context is compacted it runs the deterministic staleness engine `closeout_check.py` against the working tree and, when docs look stale, injects a non-blocking, fail-open closeout reminder (CO-1). The review-and-update is the `closeout` skill + `closeout-agent`.
- **`hooks/closeout_check.py`** — NOT a hook; the **v3.18.0 deterministic closeout staleness engine** backing `precompact-closeout.py` + `/architect-team:closeout --check`. Mirrors the `documentation-currency` inventory (pinned against drift by a test); the `new-surface-undocumented` signal keys off the specific inventory-count docs (README / CLAUDE / CODEBASE_MAP) so a lone CHANGELOG touch can't mask a stale grid; parses `git status --porcelain` with rename/copy detection.
- **`hooks/hold_channel.py`** — NOT a hook; the **v4.1.1 hold-channel harness pin**: `HOLD_CHANNEL_VERIFIED_MIN = (2, 1, 319)` + `HOLD_CHANNEL_VERIFIED_LINE = (2, 1)` name the Claude Code range the quiet Stop-hold channel (`additionalContext` on Stop) was verified on; `detect_version` (`claude --version`, else the `version` stamp on transcript records), `hold_channel_verdict` (`verified` / `older-than-verified` / `newer-line-unverified` / `unknown`), `hold_channel_warning` (the one line; silent when unknown, under `CT6_HOLD_CHANNEL_PIN_DISABLED`, or when `CT6_STOP_HOLD_MODE=block`). Consumed by the SessionStart hook. Stdlib-only.
- **`hooks/sessionstart-run-continuity.py`** — the **`SessionStart`** hook (v3.30.0 — the first `SessionStart` wiring). **v4.1.1:** `maybe_warn_hold_channel` prints one line when the running Claude Code is outside the range the quiet Stop-hold channel was verified on (see `hooks/hold_channel.py` below). On startup / resume / clear / compact, an ACTIVE `active-run.json` marker injects a resume directive ("invoke `Skill(<skill>)` FIRST, then resume the run") so a resumed session re-loads the pipeline skill instead of solving by hand. Fails open; honors `CT6_RUN_CONTINUITY_DISABLED`. **(v3.39.0)** gains `maybe_heal_model_split()` — re-applies the role split to an INSTALLED plugin copy (under `~/.claude/plugins/` only; a dev checkout is never rewritten) whose agents drifted after a plugin update, from `gateway.json`'s recorded `model_policy`; fail-open, no new wiring. **(v3.40.0, ADV3-1)** the heal is heal-to-RECORDED-alias: it restores the alias the gateway state records as actually served (`secondary_alias`, else the legacy `codex_alias`) and never writes an alias the running config doesn't route; no recorded alias ⇒ no-op (never guess). **(v3.41.0)** the heal priority is `spawn_alias` > `secondary_alias` > legacy `codex_alias` — a pre-spawn-alias state still heals to the alias its config actually routes (with an upgrade note), and the serving-consistency check extends to the spawn generation. **(v3.41.1)** gains a SECOND, symmetric heal — `maybe_heal_activation()` — which re-applies the settings.json gateway env block when `gateway.json` records `activated` + `enabled` + `auth_mode: api-key` but settings.json has lost `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` (or has it with the token missing — half-drift). Guards in order: the installed-copy guard with an explicit-injection bypass (passing BOTH `gateway_state_path` and `settings_path` is programmatic consent to sandbox paths; the real `main()` never injects); the STATE guards; then a TCP liveness probe of `127.0.0.1:<port>` via an injectable `port_probe` seam (the recorded `enabled` flag is never trusted bare — re-pointing sessions at a dead gateway would convert silent drift into hard-broken sessions). A present-but-DIFFERENT `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` is never clobbered; a corrupt settings.json aborts fail-open; every failure path returns `""`. The state guards are read BEFORE the probe and that ordering is LOAD-BEARING and test-pinned: recorded consent is the single suppression seam, so a future credit-exhaustion failover that un-points Claude Code suppresses this heal by flipping recorded state with zero guard-logic change. Note the liveness probe is explicitly NOT an upstream-health or credit signal — a credit-dead gateway still binds its port. `main()` runs the activation heal FIRST, then the split heal. **(v4.0.0)** `maybe_warn_task_tools()` prints one warning line in a CT6 workspace (an `.architect-team/` dir) when `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` is not configured and the harness task root does not exist — a Claude 5 session ships without the Task tools the Phase 3 gate and the completion lock read; pure read, never raises; the fix is `setup.py` (which writes the flag with consent).
- **`hooks/open_work.py`** — **v3.56.0 (NEW)**: the stdlib-only substrate behind the turn-boundary COMPLETION LOCK — the module that lets a Stop hook decide "is work still open" from files the HARNESS writes instead of from what the agent claims. Not itself a wired hook (like `run_continuity.py`): `pipeline-completion-audit.py` imports it, and it also exposes an operator CLI. Surface: `read_harness_tasks` (loads the harness task list via `session_task_dir(root, session_id)` — `<root>/<full session id>/<taskId>.json`, the layout Claude Code writes today, else the older `<root>/session-<first 8>/`, whichever exists (v4.0.0); `tasks_root` honours `CT6_TASKS_ROOT`, and `tasks_root_is_default` carries the v4.0.0 floor rule — a missing DEFAULT root comes back as an advisory `unavailable` source the lock proceeds past and names in its block text, a missing REDIRECTED root blocks; `task_tools_opted_in` reads the `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS` opt-in from env or settings — partitioning into open / terminal / **`unreadable[]`** — read failures are returned as DATA, never raised, which is the property the block-vs-fail-open split rests on; sidecars are skipped BY NAME, never via a silent parse failure); `open_task_items` (only `completed` is terminal — a missing, empty, or unrecognized `status` counts as OPEN, the asymmetry failing safe); `ledger_path` / `read_ledger` / `derive_ledger_entries` / `accumulate_ledger` / `open_ledger_entries` / `resolve_ledger_entry` (the ask-ledger at `<root>/.architect-team/ask-ledger.json`, DERIVED from the harness-written transcript via `run_continuity`'s readers so an agent cannot decline to register an ask — accumulation is load-bearing because that reader is tail-capped, so re-derivation only ever ADDS and ambiguous stays open; the harness's `<command-name>` marker is stripped while the user's PROSE is kept, a bare invocation deriving nothing); `classify_turn_output` (the pure one-line-of-state predicate — Trips on a structural marker (heading, bullet — ASCII **or** Unicode, numbered item, `**Label:**` block, table row) at `>= 2` lines, on `>= 6` report-length lines, on `>= 12` lines of any length, or on `> 600` raw chars of unbroken prose. NEVER on a one-line turn whatever markers it carries, and it measures ALL assistant text since the last genuine user prompt — not the last block, which is what closes the summary-then-sign-off evasion. Kill-switches `CT6_COMPLETION_LOCK_DISABLED` (master) / `CT6_TASK_LIST_GATE_DISABLED` / `CT6_ASK_LEDGER_GATE_DISABLED` / `CT6_TURN_OUTPUT_GATE_DISABLED`; `CT6_TASKS_ROOT` is the test seam so no test can reach a developer's real task store. The `list` / `resolve --evidence` CLI is deliberately the HUMAN's release valve and is NOT advertised to the agent in the block message. Reuses `run_continuity` for transcript reading, the `CT6-TEAMMATE` token, and the atomic-write pattern; stdlib-only, no import-time I/O. Exercised by `tests/test_open_work.py` + `tests/test_completion_lock.py`.
- **`hooks/run_continuity.py`** — NOT a hook; the **v3.30.0 run-continuity substrate** shared by the continuation guard, the sticky run arm, and the SessionStart directive. Owns the `active-run.json` lifecycle marker (`active` / `complete` / `stood-down` — ENGAGED deterministically the moment a run-driving Skill fires, kept current via `--set phase=/slug=`, completed ONLY by `--mark-complete` (the documented last state action of Phase 8 / B8 / M8 / U9), stood down ONLY on the user's explicit `--stand-down`), the progress fingerprint (`.architect-team/**` stats + git HEAD/index + `git status --porcelain`), and compact-boundary-aware engagement/teammate transcript analysis. CLI: `--status` / `--engage <skill> [--session-id <id>]` / `--set` / `--mark-complete` / `--stand-down`. **v3.47.0:** engagement records the session id (precedence: the `CT6_SESSION_ID` operator override, then the primary `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`) so the review-gate's unmanifested-task arm can scope to the run's own session. **v3.55.4:** `RUN_DRIVING_SKILLS` gains `data-eng-pipeline` (4 → 5 members; `ENGAGEMENT_SKILLS` = the union with `proposal-refiner` → 6) — RESTORING `_PIPELINE_SKILLS == ENGAGEMENT_SKILLS` at 6 (both 7 since v3.65.0, when `playwright-suite-builder` joined; `RUN_DRIVING_SKILLS` stays 6), so a `/architect-team:data-eng` run engages the marker + counts as engaged (the v3.55.2 KNOWN FOLLOW-UP closed; the v3.50.0 omission). Kill-switch `CT6_RUN_CONTINUITY_DISABLED`; budget `CT6_MAX_NO_PROGRESS_STOPS`. Exercised by `tests/test_run_continuity.py`.
- **`hooks/override_markers.py`** — NOT a hook; the stdlib-only helper module backing the unilateral-override guard (the marker constants + detection helpers `pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py` and `vao_tools.py::verify-no-unilateral-override` both consume). Exercised by `tests/test_override_markers.py`.
- **`hooks/spec_fingerprint.py`** — NOT a hook; the **v3.47.0 spec-currency substrate**: `spec_files` (sorted posix relpaths under the active openspec change dir, reparse-point-safe) + `compute_spec_fingerprint` (SHA-256 over relpath + content) + `fingerprints_match`. Stamped into every teammate manifest at dispatch (per `team-spawning-and-review-gates`), audited at Stop by `_audit_spec_currency` — a mid-run spec amendment with no re-brief record is the failure it exists to catch. Exercised by `tests/test_spec_fingerprint.py`.
- **`hooks/deletion_guard.py`** — **v4.0.0 (NEW enforcement script)**: the **`PreToolUse[Bash]`** deletion-recoverability guard + the soft-delete verbs. **v4.1.3:** `strip_heredocs` removes here-document bodies before segmenting (a commit message is data, not a command) and a segment that cannot be tokenized asks only when `_DELETION_SHAPED_RE` says it is deletion-shaped. **v4.1.4:** `_record_assignments` / `_expand_known_vars` expand `NAME=value` assignments made earlier in the same command, so `git -C $W` and `$W/path` targets resolve. Before a deletion-shaped Bash command runs (`rm` / `rmdir` / `git clean` / `git checkout --` / `git reset --hard` / `find … -delete` / `xargs rm` / Python `os.remove`-style literals / the Windows spellings), every target path is classified by what git knows about it and RECOVERABILITY is the classifier: temp / cache / run-state scratch → allow; gitignored → allow; tracked AND clean → allow, naming the restore command; tracked WITH uncommitted changes → ask (those lines exist nowhere else); untracked and not ignored → ask (the only copy); outside any repo / unparsable → ask; a shared-state git operation (the teammate rules) → deny inside an ACTIVE run, ask outside. Most restrictive wins across a command's targets. Emits ONE `hookSpecificOutput` JSON (`permissionDecision: allow | ask | deny` + the reason + an `additionalContext` soft-delete hint) only when a decision is needed — NOTHING for a non-Bash tool or a non-deletion command, so ordinary commands are never slowed — and ALWAYS exits 0 (an internal exception emits an `ask` whose reason says the guard could not classify: fail-SAFE, never a crash, never a silent allow). CLI verbs `trash <path>` (moves into `<workspace>/.architect-team/trash/<run-id>/<rel>` — per-run scratch the guard itself classifies as deletable, so "delete" becomes "recoverable until prune"), `prune`, `classify`, `check`. Kill-switch `CT6_DELETION_GUARD_DISABLED`. Honest boundary: it reads command TEXT, not the scripts a command invokes. Rule text: `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/deletion-discipline.md`; also carried by `skills/ct6-agent-core`. `tests/test_deletion_guard.py`.
- **`hooks/timings_log.py`** — **v4.0.0 (NEW enforcement-slot script; observability, never a gate)**: library AND hook entry. Wired on `SubagentStart` / `SubagentStop` / `TaskCreated` / `TaskCompleted` / `Stop`; imported by `scripts/notify/notify.py` (phase / run intervals from the notifier's phase events) and `scripts/homepage/`. Appends one `timing/v1` line per closed interval to `<workspace>/.architect-team/run-history/timings.jsonl` (`run_id` / `lane` / `phase` / `task_id` / `agent` / `model` / `dispatch_mode` / `kind: agent | task | phase | run` / `started_at` / `ended_at` / `duration_s` / `outcome` / `size_bucket`), with the still-open intervals beside it in `timings-open.json`. Exits 0 on EVERY path (exceptions swallowed; stderr only under `CT6_TIMINGS_DEBUG=1`); agent / task intervals are recorded only while a CT6 run is ACTIVE in the workspace (the `run_continuity` marker) so an ad-hoc dispatch in an unrelated project never lands in the estimate basis; `Stop` closes the run interval ONLY once the marker is no longer active (the completion lock holds a run open across many Stops) and closes every other open interval as `outcome: "unknown"` — recorded as unknown, never guessed; every line is enriched best-effort from `intake-state.json` + the marker; `CT6_TIMINGS_USER_MIRROR=1` additionally mirrors closed lines to `~/.architect-team/timings.jsonl` (`CT6_TIMINGS_USER_MIRROR_PATH` overrides). Kill-switch `CT6_TIMINGS_DISABLED`. Read by `scripts/homepage/timings.py`. `tests/test_timings_log.py`.
- **`hooks/decisions_ledger.py`** — **v4.0.0 (NEW)**: NOT a hook; the single append-only record of what a run decided — `<workspace>/.architect-team/decisions.jsonl`, one `decision/v1` line per decision (`kind: triage | reuse | contract-approval | appearance | principles | sr-routing | option-choice | other`, `summary`, `chosen`, `rejected[{label, why}]`, `refs[]`; `run_id` / `phase` defaulted from `intake-state.json`). Triage verdicts, reuse decisions, contract approvals, appearance-mode bindings, principle verdicts, SR routing and option choices already happen in their own phases and artifacts — each deciding phase now ALSO appends one line here, and nothing is ever edited or removed (a reversed decision is a NEW line whose summary says so). CLI `add` / `list` (`--kind` / `--limit` / `--json`); an unknown kind is refused (exit 2), never coerced to `other`. Rendered as the homepage's Decisions panel. `tests/test_decisions_ledger.py`.
- **`hooks/completion_unlock.py`** — **v4.1.0 (NEW)**: NOT a wired hook; the engine behind `/architect-team:unlock`. `inspect` (read-only — what holds the turn right now: the session's open harness tasks, unresolved ask-ledger entries, declared gates incl. `converge:<kind>` with no satisfying record, the active run marker), `release` (closes every source the Stop hook reads under ONE durable record `.architect-team/releases/unlock-<ts>.json` carrying the user's words, the session, what held and what was done: the open tasks are RETIRED through `open_work.py`'s operator verb with `--force` so the store stays registered and every task carries the reason, the open asks are RESOLVED with the record as evidence, unsatisfied declared gates are WAIVED — `waived_at` / `waived_by: user` / `evidence_path` → the record, never faked as satisfied — the run marker is STOOD DOWN via `run_continuity.stand_down` (`pipeline-stand-down.md` with the user's words) with the guard state cleared, and one line goes to the decisions ledger), `status` (lists the release records). Not a kill-switch and not an exit an agent may reach for: the verb is sanctioned ONLY inside the user's own command turn (Arm 5 of `pretool_skill_gate.py`; the command is `disable-model-invocation: true`; no hold text names it). HONEST BOUNDARY: Bash can reach the module in forms the arm's command-text match does not see, and a hand-written release record is indistinguishable from a real one on disk — the same named residual every filesystem gate in this repo carries; the record, the ledger line and the stand-down artifact make a misuse a deliberate act on a named surface, visible after the fact. Rule text: `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/completion-unlock.md`. `tests/test_completion_unlock.py`.
- **`hooks/publish_config.py`** — **v4.0.0 (NEW)**: NOT a hook; the READ-ONLY reader for the page-publishing opt-in (the `deploy_config.py` shape). Merges the USER-level `~/.architect-team/publish.json` (machine-wide defaults — a `base_url` the user owns, adapter defaults, the GCP project) under the PROJECT-level `<project>/.architect-team-publish.json` (gitignored, local per checkout; the committed `.architect-team-publish.example.json` is the schema template), the project file winning key by key over the user file over the built-in defaults; schema `architect-team-publish/v1` (`mode: claude | private-url`, `adapter: local-port | gcp-signed-url | gcp-cloud-run | nominated-host`, `project_slug` (defaults to the repo directory name), `token_ttl_hours` 72, `bind` {host, port 8787, external_ip, tls_cert, tls_key}, `gcp` {project, bucket, service, region}, `nominated_host` {ssh, web_root, public_base_url}, `base_url`). Fail-safe by construction: an absent, unreadable, malformed or unknown-valued config resolves to `mode == "claude"` — a broken config can never open a port, upload a file or mint a URL. This module never writes (only `scripts/publish/setup_question.py` writes the file, once, with the human's recorded answer); the override guard refuses agent edits once it exists.
- **`hooks/git_identity.py`** — **v3.67.0**: NOT a hook; the per-checkout commit-identity substrate + CLI — `resolve` (recorded → a candidate from the repo's LOCAL git config → a candidate from the GLOBAL config → missing; exits 0 with the status in its output so the polyglot `||` form is safe), `set` (refuses a blank name, a newline, an email without `@` / with whitespace or angle brackets, an unknown `source`; atomic write), `status`, `args`, and the **`git -- <args>` wrapper** that injects `-c user.name= -c user.email=` from `<main-worktree>/.architect-team/git-identity.json` (`{name, email, source, recorded_at}`) and REFUSES (exit 2) when there is no record. Resolved via `git rev-parse --git-common-dir` so every run worktree of a checkout shares one record; gitignored with the rest of `.architect-team/` (identity is per PERSON, never committed); git config is read only to PROPOSE a candidate and is never written; console ASCII-only. Read by `pretool_skill_gate.py` Arm 4 and by `scripts/setup/worktree_lifecycle.py::merge_branch_to_main_and_prune` (`_commit_identity_args`). Rule text: `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/git-commit-identity.md`. `tests/test_git_identity.py`.
- **`hooks/deploy_config.py`** — **v3.44.0**: NOT a hook; the fail-safe READ-ONLY reader for `.architect-team-deploy.json` (human-authored, gitignored, local per checkout; the committed `.architect-team-deploy.example.json` is the template): `prod_deploy.enabled` gates the dev → test-on-dev → prod discipline, `always_merge_to_prod_on_complete`, `prod_branch` (default `main`), `deploy_command`; **v4.0.0** adds the human test-selection pins `tests.always_run` (test ids or fnmatch globs — "always run the billing suite") and `tests.full_suite_at` (phases; default `["phase-8"]`, the one scheduled full-suite moment before the merge — a project may add Phase 5), read by `scripts/test_index/` through `tests_always_run` / `tests_full_suite_at`; no agent may lower either. An absent / malformed config resolves to "prod deploy NOT enabled"; once the file exists the override guard blocks any agent edit.
- **`hooks/frontend_impact.py`** — **v3.44.0**: NOT a hook; the canonical frontend-file extensions + `detect_frontend_impact` / `changed_files_touch_frontend` (either-signal: frontend files changed OR a changed backend endpoint consumed by a frontend route). Feeds the review gate's conditionally-required `frontend_impact_e2e_review`, the `_audit_frontend_e2e` Stop arm, the playwright-suite builder's map-freshness check and (v4.0.0) the test-index selector's impacted-flows join.

**v3.10.0 — the Layer 3 verification surface is now a package behind a facade (R2):**

- **`hooks/vao/`** — NOT a hook; the **`hooks/vao/` package (v3.10.0 R2)** holding the 26 deterministic Layer 3 verification tools, split by discipline family into **19 modules** (disk-counted at v4.0.0, `__init__.py` excluded): `core.py` (verdict-write + the shared `{tool, valid, gaps, verdict_at}` output helpers + `_is_test_path` / `_looks_like_test_path`; 185 lines), `oracle.py` (4 tools), `fake_data.py` (2), `live_verification.py` (2 — incl. `verify-target-element-measured` with the v3.66.0 `stuck-without-verification-plan` gap), `persona.py` (2), `scope.py` (2), `prod_safety.py` (1), `registry_inflight.py` (2), `deploy_pipeline.py` (2) + its `deploy_pipeline_b.py` split (1), the claims-citation family `deferral.py` (2) / `deferral_b.py` (v3.47.0) / `mention_context.py` (v3.47.0 — the per-occurrence mention-vs-use guard), the v3.47.0 `check_integrity.py` (`verify-check-can-fail`), the v3.55.0 `frontend_e2e.py` (`verify-frontend-e2e-loop-exit` — the run-level loop-exit genuineness check the `_audit_frontend_e2e` arm calls), the v3.59.0 `claim_binding.py` (`verify-claim-instrument-binding` — a claim must name the instrument that measured it), the v3.66.0 `diagnosis.py` (`verify-diagnosis-falsified` — the executed-falsification check over the architect's `diagnostic-plan-<ts>.json` sidecar that `_audit_diagnosis_falsified` calls; six `diagnosis-*` severities), and the two **v4.0.0** modules: `principles.py` (`verify-principles-respected` — reads the `.architect-team/principles.json` register + a `principles-verdicts/v1` sidecar and returns `{valid, gaps[], warnings[]}`; hard gaps `principle-without-verdict` ("not considered" is not a verdict) / `violated-without-cause` / `principle-violated` (no `user_override` quoting the user) / `chosen-option-violates` (auto mode, a fitting option existed) / `chosen-option-missing` / `all-options-violate` (the hard stop — the run writes `escalation-pending.md` and asks which principle to relax) / the sidecar-shape gaps; warnings `principles-cap-exceeded` / `no-confirmed-principles` / `verdict-for-inactive-principle` / `principle-overridden-by-user`) and `required_reads.py` (`verify-required-reads` — parses every `INDEX.md` under the plugin root (the Required-reads table — `Before` = phases or `always`, `Read` = backticked paths — or the frontmatter shape; the walk skips `.git` / `.architect-team` / `tests` / `openspec` / …), reads the run's tool-call ledger, and reports `required-read-skipped` / `index-path-missing` when a phase started without its declared Reads; it proves the file was OPENED, not understood — the Stop arm reports, it does not block by default). Each module uses the dual-form `try: from hooks.vao.X / except ImportError: from vao.X` import so it runs both as a package and as a bare-module script.
- **`hooks/vao_tools.py`** — NOT a hook; the **facade (v3.10.0 R2; 472 lines at v4.0.0)** over the `hooks/vao/` package. Re-exports every test-referenced name (190 names in `_REEXPORT_MAP` at v4.0.0 — the 26 `verify_*` functions + the underscore helpers + `detect_deploy_mandate_in_prompt`) so `vao_tools.<name>` / `from hooks.vao_tools import <name>` resolve unchanged, and keeps the `if __name__ == "__main__":` argparse dispatch stable — the **26 `verify-*` subcommands**: `verify-oracle-match`, `verify-baseline-clean`, `verify-no-fake-data`, `verify-every-element`, `verify-rendered-parity`, `verify-interactions-honored`, `verify-live-verification-claim`, `verify-live-data-wiring`, `verify-no-standing-red`, `verify-no-end-of-run-deferral`, `verify-per-persona-path-coverage`, `verify-affordance-coverage`, `verify-no-implementation-scope-cut`, `verify-test-prod-safety-classification`, `verify-discipline-registry-current`, `verify-inflight-clarifications-processed`, `verify-deploy-mandate-satisfied`, `verify-target-element-measured`, `verify-no-pipeline-bypass`, `verify-no-unilateral-override`, `verify-check-can-fail` (v3.47.0), `verify-frontend-e2e-loop-exit` (v3.55.0), `verify-claim-instrument-binding` (v3.59.0), `verify-diagnosis-falsified` (v3.66.0), `verify-principles-respected` (v4.0.0), `verify-required-reads` (v4.0.0) — exit codes (0 valid / 2 invalid) and verdict-file writes unchanged. Every VAO fixture + the real-subprocess `tests/test_vao_glue_execution.py` stay green; `tests/test_vao_facade_reexports.py` is the mechanical safety net (identity-checks every re-export).
- **`hooks/shared_util.py`** — NOT a hook; the **v3.10.0 (R1a) shared-function home**: `load_json(path, *, missing_ok)` (the unified `_load_json` — `missing_ok=True` returns the default for the fail-open call sites, `missing_ok=False` raises for the fail-closed ones), `_utc_now_iso`, and the unified JSONL reader. Eliminates the prior 3–4 duplicate definitions across `hooks/vao/*` / `inflight_inbox.py` / `discipline_registry.py` / `skill_invocation_audit.py` / `pipeline-completion-audit.py`, each now importing dual-form. (`scripts/phenotypes/phenotypes.py` keeps its own helper by design — independent subsystem, not a hook gate.)

**v3.8.0 lineage modules (NOT hooks — stdlib-only support modules under `hooks/`):**

- **`hooks/lineage_graph.py`** — **the deterministic Code & Data Lineage Graph (CDLG) core (lineage roadmap P1).** Stdlib-only (`hashlib` + `re`), NO import-time side effects, every function pure given its inputs. The *machine half* of the `endpoint-trace-mapping` skill: the agent does the live polyglot extraction at runtime; this module owns the testable pieces. Provides: the graph schema + validator (`schema_version` 1; nodes of `kind` ∈ {function, endpoint, data_asset}; edges of `kind` ∈ {calls, reads, writes, modifies, serves, originates, serves_route}; `serves_route` FE→BE seam edges MUST carry a `match_basis` + confidence); the `func://` / `asset://` ID nomenclature (rename-stable via a content fingerprint — the load-bearing join key for MemPalace dedup + graph diffing); **runtime-witness reconciliation** (`reconcile_with_witness` — edge recall / hallucination metrics + the `witness_gate` trust gate that *consumes* the existing `code-path-witness.json` from v0.9.31/0.9.32 as ground truth, never re-implementing execution capture); transitive freshness (`transitive_stale_nodes` — O(V+E) single reverse-BFS after the v3.8.0 adversarial-review rewrite); and the cost-ceiling / truncation. Source of truth: `docs/LINEAGE_UPGRADE_REQUIREMENTS.md` §4 + REQ-DOC-01/04/06/08 + REQ-MEM-02. Exercised in isolation by `tests/test_lineage_graph.py`.
- **`hooks/run_metrics.py`** — **per-run metric instrumentation (lineage P0.5; REQ-CDL-02 / REQ-SAFE-02).** Stdlib-only, no import side effects. Writes a per-run metrics JSON at `<workspace>/.architect-team/run-metrics/<run_id>.json` recording the §6 success metrics so a bug-fix run's before/after is measurable on the frozen bug benchmark: `dev_loop_iterations`, `first_pass_fix`, `oscillation_count`, `bug_still_present_count`, `fix_regression_count`, `fe_api_verdict` (the REQ-DIAG-02 discriminant: frontend-bug / api-bug / inconclusive), `layer_fixed`, the derived `wrong_layer`, `cdlg_edge_recall`, `cdlg_hallucination_rate`. Exposes `METRIC_KEYS` / `record_run_metrics` (merge semantics — partial subsets allowed) / `read_run_metrics` / `compute_wrong_layer`. Mirrored to MemPalace run-history per `common-pipeline-conventions ## Run metrics + success measurement (v3.8.0)`. **v3.10.0 (R6c)** adds `heartbeat_snapshot(workspace, run_id)` — returns `{run-id, phase, elapsed-since-start, qa-cycle-count, agents-dispatched}` from the existing metrics + intake-state for the unbounded-run heartbeat (the `heartbeat` notify event; see `## Unbounded solving discipline (v3.8.0)` `### Heartbeat discipline (v3.10.0)`).

**Other stdlib-only support modules under `hooks/` (NOT hooks — no wiring, no import side effects):**

- **`hooks/skill_invocation_audit.py`** — the **Layer-6 Stop-hook skill-invocation auditor** (after-the-fact DETECTION that the right pipeline skill was actually engaged for a pipeline-command request). Owns `find_skill_requests` + the `COMMAND_TO_SKILLS` map (pipeline-command → required-skill table) — the same detection surface the v3.15.0 real-time `pretool_skill_gate.py` reuses (dual-form import) to convert audit into PREVENTION, and the evidence `verify-no-pipeline-bypass` reads. Stdlib-only.
- **`hooks/discipline_registry.py`** — the **v2.18.0 codebase discipline registry.** The 4-entry `DISCIPLINE_CATALOG` (prod-safe-test-classification (auto-apply-safe) / live-data-wiring / multi-persona-path-coverage / affordance-coverage (SR-route)), `freshness_check(workspace, catalog)` + `read_registry` / `write_registry` / `record_application`, `REGISTRY_RELATIVE_PATH = .architect-team/discipline-registry.json`, `SCHEMA_VERSION = "1.0"`. Drives Phase 0.1, `/architect-team:discipline-status`, and `verify-discipline-registry-current`. (v3.10.0 R7 added prod-safe + multi-persona applicability guards; v3.13.0 added `.architect-team` to the frontend-marker scan skip-set.)
- **`hooks/inflight_inbox.py`** — the **v2.19.0 in-flight clarification inbox.** The per-run inbox JSONL at `.architect-team/inbox/<run-id>.jsonl` + `read_inbox` / `append_clarification` / `mark_processed` / `unprocessed_messages` / `current_run_id` / `inbox_path_for`; the 4 classifications `scope-amendment` / `clarification` / `out-of-scope` / `parallel-problem` (the last opens a v3.16.0 concurrent in-run lane). Drives `/architect-team:inject` and `verify-inflight-clarifications-processed`. (v3.26.0 hardened `_inbox_lock` against a Windows `PermissionError` on `os.open(O_CREAT|O_EXCL)`.)
- **`hooks/shared_rule_constants.py`** — the **v3.1.0 single CODE source of truth** for `FORBIDDEN_GIT_OPERATIONS` / `TEST_FAILURE_ORIGINS` / `PARITY_VERBS` / `ACTION_KIND_VALUES`, imported by `hooks/vao_tools.py` + `hooks/pipeline-completion-audit.py` so the constants can't drift between the gate and the auditor. Stdlib-only.

### Setup & support scripts (15) + the v3.42.0 instruction-surface / memory / eval engines + the v4.0.0 engines (instruction budget, test index, principles, publishing, homepage, sampling)

- **`scripts/setup/setup.py`** — checks Python ≥ 3.10 / Node ≥ 20.19; installs openspec CLI, pytest+pytest-asyncio+httpx, Playwright+chromium. **v3.9.0 → v3.67.0 (hard prerequisites, SUPERSEDED in v4.0.0):** a missing REQUIRED plugin (`REQUIRED_PLUGINS` was superpowers@claude-plugins-official, cartographer@cartographer-marketplace, ralph-loop@claude-plugins-official) was an **exit-1 HARD block**; **since v4.0.0 `REQUIRED_PLUGINS` is EMPTY** — `ralph-loop` is retired (its job is `workflows/ct6-converge.js`) and `superpowers` / `cartographer` joined `bauplan` in `CONDITIONAL_PLUGINS` (reported + remediated through the same marketplace-source lines, never gating; the lanes record `methodology: native` and follow `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/native-methodology.md` when one is absent); NEW `ensure_openspec_propose_skill()` is a 4th HARD-gated prerequisite that verifies the vendored `.claude/skills/openspec-propose/SKILL.md` (or an opsx/openspec plugin id) resolves — "missing" → exit 1. **v1.0.0** extends with `claude --version` ≥ 2.1.32 check + `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1` check (env OR `~/.claude/settings.json`); offers to add the flag to `~/.claude/settings.json` with user consent; honors `--check-only` (report-only, never modifies user files, exits non-zero on unsatisfied checks) and `--no-prompt` (skip the settings.json write even when missing). **Subsequent levers** (each tracked in the frontmatter ledger): **v3.32.0** first-install hardening (cartographer marketplace provenance `kingbootoshi/cartographer`, the npm EACCES `--prefix ~/.local` retry, the PEP-668 `uv` → `pip --user` → `--break-system-packages` install ladder + `tiktoken`, `--yes`/`-y` + `CT6_SETUP_ASSUME_YES` non-interactive consent, the unconditional fable-fallback note); **v3.35.0** `--codex`/`--no-codex` + `CT6_CODEX_56_AVAILABLE` (the model-policy deploy — never gates); **v3.36.0** `--external-llm`/`--no-external-llm` + the `CT6_EXTERNAL_LLM` tri-state (the gateway install — never gates; v3.37.1 fixed the loader's missing `sys.modules` registration); **v3.38.0** the `--external-llm` interactivity pass-through — an interactive setup run (NOT `--yes`/`CT6_SETUP_ASSUME_YES`, NOT `--check-only`, NOT `--no-prompt`, stdin a real TTY) lets the gateway installer prompt for missing keys (warn-degrade preserved); **v3.40.0** the `--secondary <provider>` / `CT6_SECONDARY_PROVIDER` pass-through selecting the gateway's secondary API (provider names derived from the lever's `SECONDARY_PROVIDERS` registry, never a hardcoded list); **v3.63.0 (the CONDITIONAL tier)** NEW `CONDITIONAL_PLUGINS` — a second dependency tier beside `REQUIRED_PLUGINS`, holding `bauplan@bauplan-skills`. The two sets are **disjoint by invariant** (unit-pinned) and the conditional tier is routed nowhere near the `if missing: return 1` branch, so a missing conditional member is REPORTED and REMEDIATED but can never change setup's exit code — the point being that a domain plugin most users never touch must not gate every install. Its remediation reuses `_PLUGIN_MARKETPLACE_SOURCES` (which gains `"bauplan@bauplan-skills": "BauplanLabs/bauplan-skills"`) and `plugin_remediation_lines()` verbatim, so the third-party-marketplace path built for cartographer is shared rather than duplicated. **v4.0.0 (the Claude 5 task-tool floor + the per-project steps):** `check_task_tools` writes `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` into `~/.claude/settings.json` `env` with the same consent flow as the Agent Teams flag (`--check-only` reports, `--no-prompt` skips the write, `--yes` consents) — a Claude 5 session ships without `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate` otherwise, which left the Phase 3 evidence gate and the completion lock silent on exactly the models the plugin runs on; `--project-root <path>` runs the per-project steps `check_publish_config` (the one page-publishing question via `scripts/publish/setup_question.py` — ecosystem detection of gcloud / a public address / ssh hosts with injectable probes, the `AskUserQuestion` payload with the security notes said out loud, `write_config` once; an existing project config is never overwritten) and `build_first_homepage` (`scripts/homepage/build.py` → `.architect-team/homepage/index.html`).
- **`scripts/setup/set_default_model.py`** — **(v3.32.0)** the stdlib-only uniform agent-model lever (`--model fable|opus|sonnet|haiku` / `--check`) rewriting ONLY the frontmatter `model:` line across `agents/*.md` (idempotent, line-ending-preserving) — today the IMPLEMENTED Opus 5 fallback for a harness predating the `fable` alias. **(v3.35.0)** grows the availability-gated Codex 5.6 role-split policy: `AGENT_ROLES` classifies all 39 stems (18 architecture/control/design ⇒ `fable`; 21 development/code-checking/testing ⇒ `codex-5.6-sol`), `apply_policy` / `--split codex` / the tri-state `--auto` on `CT6_CODEX_56_AVAILABLE` (truthy=split, set-falsy=fable, absent=no-op) / policy-state `--check` / `--codex-model <id>`; unclassified stems fail safe to fable; the uniform lever refuses the codex id. Wired into `setup.py --codex|--no-codex`; availability is an INPUT, never probed. **(v3.39.0)** the runtime agents-dir resolver: `installed_plugin_agents_dir()` / `runtime_agents_dir()` resolve the INSTALLED plugin copy's `agents/` from Claude Code's `installed_plugins.json` (`$CT6_PLUGIN_REGISTRY` override, repo fallback) so install-time policy lands where the runtime actually reads it. **(v3.40.0)** the `SECONDARY_PROVIDERS` registry (per-entry `model` / `key_env` / `route_model` / `api_base` / `label`: `openai` → `gpt-5.6-sol`, `zai` → `glm-5.2` via `https://api.z.ai/api/paas/v4`; one dict entry per future provider, extensibility test-pinned with a fake provider) + the provider-neutral alias — the split writes `ct6-secondary` under the policy string `secondary-split` (exported constants `POLICY_SECONDARY_SPLIT` / `POLICY_UNIFORM_FABLE` / `LEGACY_POLICY_CODEX_SPLIT`); readers recognize BOTH alias generations (whitespace-trimmed), writers emit only the new, the uniform lever refuses both aliases, `--split secondary` is canonical (`--split codex` deprecated), and `--secondary-model` supersedes `--codex-model` (kept as a compatibility synonym). **(v3.41.0)** registry entries REQUIRE `route_dialect` (`openai` → `openai`; `zai` → `hosted_vllm`) with the gateway route DERIVED `<route_dialect>/<model>` and `route_model` REMOVED (an entry structurally cannot carry a route inconsistent with its dialect; the exact key-set is test-pinned); the split's written frontmatter id becomes the exported `SPAWN_ALIAS_MODEL_ID` `claude-haiku-4-5` (a real harness-accepted Claude id the gateway impersonates to the chosen secondary — Claude Code's spawn path rejects unknown ids client-side; test-pinned so changing it is a deliberate act); `policy_state` recognizes all THREE alias generations, `migrate_legacy_split` moves BOTH superseded frontmatter values (`codex-5.6-sol` AND the spawn-dead raw `ct6-secondary`) to the spawn alias on every config-regenerating install, and the uniform lever refuses the spawn alias too. **(v4.0.0 — the ROLE split, the shipped state)** `--split role` applies `ROLE_SPLIT_MODELS` (policy `role-split`; `role_split_model` / `role_split_targets` / `apply_role_split`; an unclassified stem fails safe to `fable`): `fable` on the 15 judgment seats, `opus` on the 9 delivery / execution seats, `sonnet` on the 14 mechanical verification / extraction / observation / documentation seats, `haiku` on `bug-classifier`; `policy_state` reports `role-split`; `--split delivery` still reproduces the v3.43.0 state and `--split secondary` the gateway split (`--split` choices: `secondary` | `codex` (deprecated) | `delivery` | `role`).
- **`scripts/setup/teams_mode.py`** — **v1.0.0 mode-detection helper.** Exposes `is_teams_mode_available(env=None, settings_path=None, claude_cmd="claude", flag_no_teams=False) -> bool` and `detect_no_teams_flag(argv) -> bool`. The pipeline calls it once at startup to decide between teams mode and subagents mode; records the decision in `intake-state.json`. Falsy / malformed / missing inputs degrade gracefully to subagents mode. Imported by the six lane skills via the orchestrator's Bash dispatch (the polyglot `python3 X.py || python X.py` pattern from v0.9.30 applies).
- **`scripts/setup/worktree_paths.py`** — **v1.1.0 worktree-aware state-resolution helper.** Three stdlib-only functions: `shared_state_dir() -> Path` returns the MAIN worktree's `.architect-team/` path (used for `locks/`, `.mempalace/`, `run-history/` — anything two concurrent sessions need to coordinate on); `run_state_dir() -> Path` returns the CURRENT worktree's `.architect-team/` (used for `reviews/`, `teammates/`, `handoffs/`, this-run's `openspec/changes/<slug>/`, this-run's findings + refined-prompts — anything per-run); `is_worktree() -> bool` returns True iff the cwd is inside a `git worktree add`-created worktree (uses `git rev-parse --git-dir` vs `--git-common-dir`). All three are best-effort — any git subprocess failure falls back to `Path.cwd() / '.architect-team'`, never raises. Consumed by `hooks/locks.py`'s default `locks_dir` resolution (lazy import inside `_default_locks_dir()` via `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`, so the lock layer remains usable even if the helper is unreachable) and documented as the palace-path resolver in `skills/mempalace-integration/SKILL.md`. The 3-layer model the helper enables (filesystem isolation = worktrees / architectural coordination = locks / context sharing = MemPalace) is documented in `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/SKILL.md` `## Running in parallel sessions`.
- **`scripts/setup/worktree_lifecycle.py`** — **v1.2.0 worktree-lifecycle helper, extended in v1.3.0 with auto-cleanup.** Stdlib-only; the v1.2.0 / v1.3.0 functions below plus the v3.6.0 / v3.7.0 additions `finalize_run_worktree`, `list_run_branches`, `merge_branch_to_main_and_prune`, `recommend_worktree`. v1.2.0: `create_run_worktree(slug, base_branch="main", parent_dir=None) -> Path` creates `<parent>/<repo-name>-<slug>/` on a fresh branch `architect-team/<slug>` via `git worktree add -b`, with collision handling that appends `-2`, `-3`, ... when either path or branch is taken (bounded at 999 attempts), and raises `RuntimeError` with an actionable message on parent-dir-not-writable / base-branch-missing / `git worktree add` failure; `cleanup_run_worktree(worktree_path, remove_branch=False) -> None` runs `git worktree remove <path>` (idempotent on already-gone worktrees — catches the `is not a working tree` / `is not a registered` markers) with optional `git branch -d architect-team/<slug>`, falling back to `--force` removal once before raising on unexpected failures; `current_worktree_is_run() -> bool` is True iff `git rev-parse --abbrev-ref HEAD` starts with `architect-team/` — used by the slash commands' re-entry detection to skip nested worktree creation; `current_run_slug() -> str | None` extracts the slug from `architect-team/<slug>` or returns None on non-run branches. v1.3.0: `list_merged_architect_team_worktrees(against="origin/main", exclude_current=True) -> list[Path]` walks `git worktree list --porcelain` to get (path, branch) pairs, then for each `architect-team/*` branch runs `git merge-base --is-ancestor <branch> <against>` — exit 0 = merged → include; default excludes the current worktree (safety: don't auto-remove the cwd even if its branch is merged); non-architect-team branches are NEVER considered; `cleanup_merged_worktrees(against="origin/main", dry_run=False) -> list[Path]` calls the list helper and (when not `dry_run`) invokes `cleanup_run_worktree(path, remove_branch=True)` on each; idempotent on a worktree that disappears between list and remove.
