Consumed by the six lane commands (`commands/architect-team.md`, `commands/bug-fix.md`, `commands/mini.md`) via the polyglot `python3 -c '...' || python -c '...'` invocation pattern in each command's `## Auto-worktree creation (v1.2.0)` AND `## Auto-cleanup of merged worktrees (v1.3.0) — runs first` sections, plus the new explicit `commands/cleanup-worktrees.md` command, plus mini Phase M7's post-merge cleanup. The split from v1.1.0's `worktree_paths.py` is intentional — paths is read-only resolution; lifecycle is side-effecting subprocess work. Full rules in `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/SKILL.md` `## Auto-worktree lifecycle` (the `### Auto-cleanup (v1.3.0)` sub-section is the canonical home of the auto-cleanup rule including the two trigger points, `exclude_current` safeguard, merged-branch detection mechanism, squash-merge limitation, `--dry-run` capability, and best-effort discipline). **Subsequent additions** (tracked in the frontmatter ledger): v3.6.0 added `finalize_run_worktree()` (end-of-run merge check — removes the worktree + branch when merged into `origin/main`, LEAVES it with a persistence warning when unmerged; unmerged work is NEVER auto-deleted) + the hidden per-project container layout `<parent>/.<repo>-worktrees/<slug>/` (dual-layout sweep keeps recognizing old flat worktrees); v3.7.0 added `list_run_branches()` + `merge_branch_to_main_and_prune()` (the auto-merge-to-main + prune default; never `--force`; branch protection wins). **v3.8.0 (lineage P6)** adds opt-in squash-merge detection — an `include_squash_merged` flag on `list_merged_architect_team_worktrees` / `cleanup_merged_worktrees` / `list_run_branches` backed by the conservative `_branch_is_squash_merged` / `_branch_is_merged_or_squash_merged` predicates (a branch counts as squash-merged only when its net diff is already fully present on `<against>`; default `False` keeps the safe false-negative, never a false-positive auto-delete) — plus a task-aware worktree heuristic, and unifies `list_run_branches`'s default to `against='origin/main'` so its "already-merged?" judgment agrees with the v1.3.0 sweep (catching a branch landed on `origin/main` via a GitHub PR while local `main` is stale).
- **`scripts/setup/agent_resume.py`** — **v1.8.0 agent dispatch result-wrapping + resume helper.** Three stdlib-only functions: `is_truncated(result) -> bool` detects empty / sub-50-char / rate-limit-marker / missing-report-format-marker dispatch results (case-insensitive for both marker sets); `wrap_agent_result(result, agent_id, send_message=None, max_attempts=2, resume_prompt=DEFAULT_RESUME_PROMPT) -> dict` wraps every background Agent dispatch result; on truncation invokes the dependency-injected `send_message(to=agent_id, prompt=resume_prompt)` to ask the SAME agent for its final verdict from already-loaded context, merges the resumed output with the original via a `[resumed via wrap_agent_result]` seam-marker, caps retries at `max_attempts`, surfaces `resumed_failed=True` + `resume_error` on cap-exhaustion without raising so the orchestrator can route on-disk artifacts to the user; `read_checkpoint(agent_id, checkpoints_dir=None) -> dict | None` reads `.architect-team/agent-checkpoints/<agent_id>.json` with default-dir resolved via the v1.1.0 `shared_state_dir()` lazy-import pattern (so checkpoints live in the main worktree, visible across worktrees during the same run); returns None for absent / unreadable / malformed; never raises. `DEFAULT_RESUME_PROMPT` is the canonical follow-up: explains the stream-timeout context, asks for the standard `Status:` / `DONE` / `BLOCKED` / `NEEDS_CONTEXT` markers, directs the resumed agent to read its own checkpoint FIRST and skip already-completed steps. Consumed by every lane orchestrator at every background Agent dispatch (architect-team-pipeline Phases −2 / −1 / 2 / 3 / 3b / 4 / 5 / 7 / 8; bug-fix-pipeline Phases B−1 / B1 / B2 / B3 / B4 / B5 / B6 / B6b / B8; mini-architect-team-pipeline Phases M2 / M3 / M4 / M5 / M7) per `common-pipeline-conventions ## Background-agent resume discipline`. Stdlib-only matching the convention used by `scripts/setup/teams_mode.py` and `scripts/setup/worktree_paths.py`. The dependency-injected `send_message` keeps the helper testable without harness coupling — the orchestrator binds the harness's real `SendMessage` tool at call time; `tests/test_agent_resume_discipline.py` passes a mock callable.
- **`scripts/setup/agent_boilerplate_blocks.py`** — **(v3.1.0; v4.0.0 shape)** the single CODE source of truth for the FIVE canonical agent boilerplate blocks — `operating-context` (the v1.0.0 operating-context text), `forbidden-git-operations`, `checkpoint-discipline`, `principles` (the compact operating-principles block whose source of truth is `docs/ETHOS.md`) and `reporting` (reporting and escalation): canonical texts, the `BLOCKS` registry with per-block `standard_agents` / `variant_agents` partitions, and the `extract_block` / `classify_agents` helpers. Since v4.0.0 the blocks are rendered ONCE into `skills/ct6-agent-core/SKILL.md` (by `compile_skills.py`) rather than into 39 agent files. Stdlib-only, no import side effects.
- **`scripts/setup/sync_agent_boilerplate.py`** — **(v3.1.0; INVERTED in v4.0.0)** until v3.67.0 it compiled the five canonical blocks into every `agents/*.md` and kept the 39 copies byte-identical to `agent_boilerplate_blocks.py`; v4.0.0 (proposal change 4) moved the one canonical copy into `skills/ct6-agent-core/SKILL.md`, so the tool now holds the INVERSE contract — no agent carries a canonical block heading in its own body (the three operating-context VARIANT agents keep their role-specific operating-context section, which was never canonical text), every agent's frontmatter `skills:` list names `ct6-agent-core`, and `skills/ct6-agent-core/SKILL.md` is in sync with the canonical strings (`compile_skills.py --check` owns that half; this tool reports it). `--check` exits non-zero on drift and names it; without `--check` it performs the conversion (strips a stray canonical block, adds the preload). `tests/test_agent_boilerplate_sync.py` is the drift guard; each file's line-ending style is preserved.
- **`scripts/setup/install_mempalace.py`** — uv-first (pip fallback) MemPalace install; prints the `claude mcp add` + `mempalace init` commands; never auto-runs them.
- **`scripts/setup/install_librarian.py`** — **(v3.29.0)** the stdlib-only full-lifecycle installer + CLI behind `/architect-team:librarian-install` (install / status / add-topic / list-topics / remove-topic / run-once / uninstall; `--enable` / `--check-only` / `--json` / `--purge`). Provisions per-user state under `~/.architect-team/librarian/` (config.json, topics.json, index.sqlite, bodies/, metadata/, librarian.log.jsonl), wires the real Anthropic LLM adapter when `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` resolves (else `FakeLLMClient` in an honest provisioned-but-disabled state with an `--enable` remediation), then generates the per-OS boot descriptor via `bg_runtime.install_descriptor` (launchd / systemd / schtasks) and PRINTS the register hint (never auto-loads). Reuses the `services/librarian` + `services/common` substrate — no new service logic. **(v3.38.0)** key-prompt parity with the gateway installer: the hidden-getpass `_prompt_for_key` seam (interactive real-TTY only; blank-to-skip; interrupt → skip), the per-key `key-declines.json` decline record with auto-reset on key resolution, the `decline [--clear]` subcommand, `--interactive-prompts` / `--re-ask-keys`, `status` decline honesty, `uninstall --purge` symmetry.
- **`scripts/setup/install_knowledge_server.py`** — **(v3.49.0)** the stdlib-only knowledge-server installer + CLI behind `/architect-team:knowledge-install`, built on the `install_librarian.py` template (install / status / print-registration / confirm-serving / uninstall; `--check-only` / `--json` / `--purge` parity). Provisions per-user state under `~/.architect-team/knowledge/`, GENERATES + PRINTS the portable MCP registration `python <repo>/services/knowledge_server/server.py --repo-root <repo>` (never auto-edits the user's MCP config; repo-root resolves `--repo-root` flag > recorded config > the git root of the install context), and adopts `install_gateway.py::confirm_gateway_serving`'s live-round-trip bar: the word "serving" is printed ONLY after a real `tools/call` round trip against the actually-started server — described "serving on this machine", NEVER "deployed". The probe argv is DERIVED from the registration argv in ONE function (`_probe_argv == _server_argv + --max-messages`), so what the installer probes is byte-identical to what it prints — the launch-dead-server class is structurally refused (the paired review caught the original `--base-dir` registration that would have launched a DEAD server; the in-test fake that ignored unknown args was closed by `test_fake_server_rejects_unknown_args`). A bare `install` in a repo without generated sidecars yields a DORMANT (not false) registration — `confirm-serving` honestly reports NOT-serving there. `tests/test_install_knowledge_server.py`.
- **`scripts/setup/install_gateway.py`** — **(v3.36.0)** the stdlib-only external-LLM gateway installer behind `setup.py --external-llm` (mirrors `install_librarian.py`): installs the MIT-licensed `litellm[proxy]` via the PEP-668 ladder (`--only-binary litellm`), provisions `~/.architect-team/gateway/` (`config.yaml` with `os.environ/...` secret references; `gateway.env` the ONLY raw-key location, chmod 0600 best-effort; per-OS launcher; boot descriptor), routes `codex-5.6-sol` → `openai/gpt-5.6-sol` (`--openai-model` override) behind a generated master key; TWO auth modes resolved from key presence, never probed — api-key (catch-all Anthropic route; `--activate` writes `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` + `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` into settings.json merge-preserving AND applies the codex split) vs subscription (no Anthropic key: fable keeps Claude sign-in auth, the split stays OFF, the gateway serves OpenAI to direct callers); install / status / uninstall + `--activate` + `--check-only`, symmetric uninstall. **(v3.37.0)** auto-registration: `register_gateway` / `unregister_gateway` / `is_gateway_registered` EXECUTE the user-level boot registration + start-now (windows `schtasks onlogon` + a Startup-folder-shim fallback + a detached spawner; linux `systemctl --user` with a `default.target` unit; darwin LaunchAgents), never sudo; `--no-register` opt-out; `status` `registered=`. **(v3.38.0)** ask-then-apply key prompting: the hidden-getpass `_prompt_for_key` stdin seam (interactive real-TTY only; blank-to-skip; interrupt → skip), the per-key `key-declines.json` decline record (via wrapper / prompt-skip, auto-reset on key resolution), the `decline <anthropic|openai> [--clear]` subcommand, `--interactive-prompts` / `--re-ask-keys`, `status` `declined=`, `uninstall --purge` removes the record. **(v3.39.0)** one-call confirmation: activate/status/uninstall target the RUNTIME agents dir (`set_default_model.runtime_agents_dir()`); a registered install live-probes `/v1/models` via `confirm_gateway_serving` with ONE user-level `restart_gateway` repair cycle (never sudo) and reports "CONFIRMED live — CT6 runs the split"; `status --live`; `gateway.json` records `model_policy` for the SessionStart self-heal; uninstall flips it back. **(v3.40.0)** the selectable secondary provider: resolution `--secondary <openai|zai>` > `CT6_SECONDARY_PROVIDER` > the choice RECORDED in `gateway.json` > the v3.39-era grandfather (an `openai_model`-only state counts as openai — a pre-upgrade install never sees a popup) > an interactive-TTY provider prompt; `--re-ask-provider` mirrors `--re-ask-keys`; `--zai-key` joins the key machinery with FULL decline parity (`decline <anthropic|openai|zai>`); `--secondary-model` with `--openai-model` a deprecated openai-scoped synonym; routes the provider-neutral `ct6-secondary` to the chosen provider (`openai/gpt-5.6-sol`, or `openai/glm-5.2` + `api_base https://api.z.ai/api/paas/v4`); legacy migration fires on EVERY config-regenerating install with prior-state carry-forward (ADV3B-1); a provider switch retains the other provider's stored key (ADV3-2); `status --live` probes the STATE-RECORDED alias; subscription mode serves the CHOSEN provider's model to direct callers. **(v3.41.0)** the GLM secondary made real + the install-honesty ladder: routes are registry-DERIVED `<route_dialect>/<model>` (zero hard-coded prefixes; `zai` → `hosted_vllm/glm-5.2` — api.z.ai implements only chat-completions, the prior `openai/` route 404'd every `/v1/messages` call); the generated config emits the DISCLOSED spawn-alias impersonation route (`claude-haiku-4-5` → `<dialect>/<model>`) in BOTH auth modes AHEAD of the anthropic catch-all (no real-Anthropic route for that one id; `gateway.json` records `spawn_alias`/`spawn_alias_maps_to`; `status` prints the mapping); `confirm_gateway_serving` requires a spawn-alias-MANDATORY bounded `/v1/messages` completion — the serving deployment resolved via `/model/info` must equal the registry-derived route (never a constant) — plus an auth-enforcement rung (a deliberately-invalid, never-echoed key must be DENIED; the prisma no-DB crash-500 is enforcement-with-a-named-warning; only a 200 fails), all through injectable prober seams (keyless pytest hermetic). Deploy honesty across seven live-verified iterations: a config-REgenerating install restarts a running gateway; staleness is judged against the SERVED state by two-armed route-contradiction (wildcard-advertised expansions are ROUTED, not stale; explicit routes take precedence; a missing explicitly-routed group is stale); the verified restart stops the actual port holder when the tracked stop leaves the port held (netstat/lsof pid resolution, image-agnostic, honest pid+image naming; refusal ⇒ no start) and reports restarted only after a bind-verify against the generated config; install keeps exactly ONE owned instance — owned = the validated process TREE (recycled-pid links severed) whose cmdline carries this state dir's config path; the accumulation sweep NEVER touches the port-holder's tree while deliberate port-freeing stops take the whole tree root-first; deploys are never-dark verify-then-swap — a STAGING instance of the identical config must pass the full ladder (bind + served-state, staging-bind ownership, real-key completion with upstream identity, auth enforcement) before ANY live stop, the staged instance is stopped on ALL exit paths, and cutover-retry exhaustion embeds the recovery procedure; the generated launchers REFUSE an already-listening port (naming the holder pid) and log masked master-key presence (first 8 chars or MISSING); the master key NEVER rotates on a re-install. `status --live` derives the same expectations from recorded state; a legacy no-spawn-alias install keeps the fallback confirm.
- **`scripts/notify/notify.py`** — the **best-effort per-project email notifier** (v0.9.18; informative-run-notifications overhaul v3.34.0). A path-addressed CLI (invoked exactly like `setup.py`) that the orchestrator runs at TEN event types — the run-level bookends `run_start` (fires once when the architecture + solution plan first exists and EMBEDS the plan artifacts via the repeatable `--plan-file` flag: per-file `PLAN_FILE_MAX_CHARS = 20_000` cap with a truncation marker, `MAX_PLAN_FILES = 8` with an omission note, missing files degrade to a note via the never-raising `_read_plan_file`) and `run_complete` (the run's final email — elapsed + final commit, optional lines omitted when absent); the classic `phase_start` / `phase_complete`; the v3.34.0 dispatch-wait pair `waiting_on_agents` / `agents_complete` (the `--agents` roster: missions when dispatched, outcomes when returned); `issue_discovered` / `git_commit` / `deploy`; and the v3.10.0 tick-driven `heartbeat`. Every event additionally renders the universal informative blocks when provided — `--details` / `--progress` / `--next-step` (`_context_trailer`) — per the CPC "Informative, not just status" content contract. Single stdlib-only module: config loader, two interchangeable providers (`GmailProvider` over `smtp.gmail.com:587` STARTTLS via `smtplib`; `SendGridProvider` POSTing to `https://api.sendgrid.com/v3/mail/send` via `urllib.request`), per-recipient event filtering, an `argparse` CLI. Opt-in (a `.architect-team-notify.json` at the target project root) and best-effort by construction — every failure path (missing config, missing secret, unreadable plan file, provider/network error, bad arguments) exits 0, so a notification can never block, fail, or alter a run. Provider secrets are read only from the env var named in config, never committed, never logged. Wired into all four pipelines (main / bug-fix / mini / ux-test) per CPC `## Notifications wiring convention`.
- **`scripts/setup/compile_skills.py`** — **(v3.42.0)** the stdlib-only skills-tier boilerplate compiler — the counterpart to `sync_agent_boilerplate.py` for skills. A marker-fence compiler that rewrites shared boilerplate (including the `docs/ETHOS.md` principles block) into the 7 pipeline-driving skills (`architect-team-pipeline`, `bug-fix-pipeline`, `mini-architect-team-pipeline`, `ux-test-builder`, `data-eng-pipeline`, `playwright-suite-builder` (v3.65.0), `common-pipeline-conventions`) from ONE canonical source; byte-stable output; `--check` freshness mode suite-gated (`tests/test_compile_skills.py`). The same run extracted the rarely-fired `## Auto-worktree lifecycle` block from `common-pipeline-conventions` to `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/auto-worktree-lifecycle.md` behind a STOP-read pointer + a reference index (−10,633 B off the always-loaded body; `tests/test_skill_references.py`); the two next-largest pipeline skills recorded a `no-safe-extraction` verdict (every block is a live gate/discipline) and that adjudication was accepted. **(v4.0.0)** `BLOCKS` carries the five canonical ids — `operating-context`, `forbidden-git-operations`, `checkpoint-discipline`, `principles`, `reporting` — and renders them between `<!-- ct6:block:<id>:begin/end -->` fences into `skills/ct6-agent-core/SKILL.md` (the skill every agent preloads) from the very strings `agent_boilerplate_blocks.py` holds (the skill-side principles render IS the agent-side canonical string — the same object); the `principles` block is also compiled into the 7 pipeline-driving skills (8 fenced skill files in all); `--check` stays suite-gated.
- **`scripts/setup/guidance_blocks.py`** — **(v3.42.0)** the shared installer-guidance helper: `upsert_block` / `remove_block` over `<!-- ct6:<capability>:begin/end -->` fences, wired into `install_mempalace.py` / `install_librarian.py` / `install_gateway.py` behind an OPT-IN `--claude-md` flag (adds a capability-gated guidance block to the target project's `CLAUDE.md` on a verified install; self-removes on a failed capability check or uninstall; idempotent, never touches text outside the fences; NO flag ⇒ no side effect). `tests/test_installer_guidance_blocks.py`.
- **`scripts/memory/recall_hygiene.py`** — **(v3.42.0)** the deterministic recall-hygiene engine: the do-not-interpret data envelope (`<recalled-data source="mempalace" instructions="false">…</recalled-data>` + a one-line preface) applied to every MemPalace-rendered block at BOTH render boundaries (the `mempalace-integration` contract render paths + the injected-recall path in `hooks/sessionstart-run-continuity.py`), an optional room/wing recall allowlist (default permissive), and a TTL digest cache with a per-entity byte cap + a per-injection budget, invalidation-on-`mine`, and a fail-open stale-degraded fallback (a visible `degraded: stale-cache` marker). Stdlib-only, no import-time side effects; the `mempalace-integration` skill is its contract. `tests/test_recall_hygiene.py` + `tests/test_sessionstart_recall_envelope.py`.
- **`scripts/docs_tooling/`** — **(v3.42.0)** the deterministic instruction-surface tooling: `capability_index.py` generates `docs/CAPABILITY_INDEX.md` (every skill / command / agent one line from its `name` + `description`; a regenerate-and-diff freshness test `tests/test_capability_index.py`; pointed to from `CLAUDE.md`) + `changelog_check.py` mechanizes the two machine-checkable invariants of `docs/CHANGELOG_RUBRIC.md` (top-entry version == `plugin.json`; a suite-total line present in the top entry) as a suite gate (`tests/test_changelog_rubric.py`). Stdlib-only; the qualitative voice guidance stays LLM-judgment, explicitly not linted.
- **`scripts/evals/`** — **(v3.42.0)** the opt-in behavioral eval tier (stdlib-only core): `runner.py` (shells to the `claude` CLI — stream-json parse, `--max-turns` bounds), `judge.py` (a bounded second `claude` call scoring a result vs ground truth; thresholds live in the fixture, never the judge), `collector.py` (a per-run verdict + cost JSON + prior-run comparison), `budget.py` (a warn-first >2× tool/turn budget-regression gate, failing mode behind `CT6_EVALS_BUDGET_STRICT`). Gated behind `CT6_EVALS=1` and EXCLUDED from the default suite (`tests/evals/` holds the routing + planted-defect outcome evals); the engine logic is verified offline in `tests/test_evals_offline.py`, so the default suite stays key-free and deterministic. Model resolution is explicit (`CT6_EVALS_MODEL` → `services/common/service_config.FALLBACK_MODEL`, child-env scrub). The live `claude` CLI is the adapter boundary — a single 4/4-pass smoke (3 routing + 1 outcome) ran this release at recorded real cost.
- **`scripts/measure/suite_measurement.py`** — **(v3.60.0)** the bracketed suite-measurement engine: a quiescence check, a before/after tree-state diff hash, and a durable `suite-measurement/v1` artifact under `docs/measurements/` (`counts`, `environment`, `result_tail`, `verdict`, the `machine_bound` note); refuses a release label on a dirty tree / an open bracket / a contradicted count; `scripts/docs_tooling/changelog_check.py --require-measurements` is the release gate and the `_audit_release_backing` Stop arm its backstop. The v4.0.0 artifact is `docs/measurements/2026-11-09-v4.0.0-suite.json` (8874 passed / 2 skipped / 0 failed; linux; python 3.13.18; `measured-clean`).
- **`scripts/measure/instruction_budget.py`** — **(v4.0.0 — proposal change 8)** the deterministic instruction-budget measurement (the engine shape of `suite_measurement.py` and `instruction_compliance.py`): `tokens = ceil(utf8_bytes / 4)` — the `bytes/4` yardstick, named in the artifact, NOT a tokenizer — over every `commands/*.md`, `skills/*/SKILL.md`, `skills/*/references/*.md` (class `chapter`; an INDEX flagged) and `agents/*.md`; the per-lane START load (command + lane skill + constitution + the chapters its INDEX says are read before Phase −2), the always-loaded descriptions, and the per-agent DISPATCH load (agent file + preloaded skills); `--check` lists budget breaches, `--write` records `docs/measurements/<date>-<label>-instruction-budget.json` (tree state + digest like the suite bracket). The budgets are the proposal's targets and several are still over (`chapter_max_tokens` 5000 is breached by the lane `SKILL.md` spines and the constitution) — the engine RECORDS, it does not yet gate. The v4.0.0 before/after: architect-team 140,542 → 45,196 tokens, bug-fix 96,650 → 30,658, mini 82,940 → 25,748, data-eng 78,068 → 26,456, ux-test 82,574 → 27,107, playwright-suite 74,366 → 24,644; agents 199,604 → 150,508 in total; always-loaded 20,701 → 21,853. `tests/test_instruction_budget.py`.
- **`scripts/test_index/`** — **(v4.0.0 — proposal change 12)** the deterministic test index + impact-based selector under `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/test-selection.md`. `engine.py` builds `<workspace>/.architect-team/test-index.json` (`test-index/v1`: an import graph + per test `files` / `funcs` (`func://` ids joined from `lineage-graph.json`) / `endpoints` / `elements` (from `suite-manifest.json`) / `kind` unit | integration | e2e / `layer` / `always_run` / `trust: static | runtime-witnessed | unknown` — a STATIC mapping is a hypothesis, a WITNESSED one is evidence, an UNKNOWN test is always selected) and selects from a change set (`--baseline <sha>` or `--changed-files`) plus changed endpoints / elements, writing `test-selection-<ts>.json` with per-test reasons; `cli.py` is the verb set (`build` / `select --phase N` / `stats` / `pins`; exit 0 / 2 usage / 3 internal, so a crash is never mistaken for an empty selection); `pytest_witness.py` is a small pytest plugin (`-p scripts.test_index.pytest_witness`) that records the repository files each test executed via `sys.setprofile` into `test-witness.json` (merged, never erased; roughly 2–3× slower — a bootstrap / refresh run, not the default way to run tests). The human pins come from `deploy_config.tests_always_run` / `tests_full_suite_at`. Phase 3 runs the selected set per slice, Phase 5 the selected integration tests + impacted flows + pins, Phase 8 the full suite once before the merge (REVERSES the v3.65.0 ENTIRE-dev-suite-at-Phase-5 rule — the full run moved, it did not disappear). Fixtures under `tests/fixtures/test_index/` are stored as `.tmpl` so pytest never collects them. `tests/test_test_index.py`.
- **`scripts/principles/`** — **(v4.0.0 — proposal change 10)** `principles.py`, the pure engine over the `project-principles/v1` register (`<workspace>/.architect-team/principles.json`; verbs `propose` / `confirm` / `add` / `retire` / `default_set` / `show` / `binding_brief` / `extract_candidates` / `check_verdicts`; a document or extracted source needs its file AND verbatim quote — only `user-stated` may omit them; soft cap 15 with `cap_warning`; `render_markdown` writes the HUMAN twin `<workspace>/docs/PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md` deterministically, byte-stable and CRLF-preserving — never hand-edited) + `cli.py` (the same verbs for `/architect-team:principles`; every workspace verb writes BOTH twins; exit 0 clean / 1 a refused verb or blocking `check` findings / 2 usage / 3 internal). `check` is the CLI face of `hooks/vao/principles.py`. `tests/test_project_principles.py`.
- **`scripts/publish/`** — **(v4.0.0 — proposal change 14)** `publish.py` (`publish <file>` / `revoke <token>` / `list` / `serve-status` / `serve-stop` / `check <file>`; `claude` mode copies the page to `.architect-team/publish/outbox/` and returns Artifact-tool instructions, `private-url` mode mints a `secrets.token_urlsafe(24)` token and records the link + expiry in `~/.architect-team/publish/registry.json`; `check_page` enforces the page contract — one self-contained HTML file under 16 MB, pinned cdnjs / jsdelivr / unpkg libraries only, no secret-shaped strings — and `--force` overrides the contract findings but never the secret finding; every private link is printed with `boundary_lines`), `setup_question.py` (`detect_ecosystem` with injectable probes / `question` — the exact `AskUserQuestion` payload with the security notes / `write_config` — the ONE writer of `.architect-team-publish.json`; gcloud + ssh are reported present or absent, never gating), and the four adapters under `adapters/`: `local_port.py` (a SELF-CONTAINED stdlib token-checking server — serves ONLY live-token paths, 404 otherwise, redacted access log, TLS when `bind.tls_cert` + `bind.tls_key` are set, runs DETACHED with a pidfile; the hourly expired-page prune rides `services/common/bg_runtime.Scheduler` when present, a plain timer thread otherwise — the Cloud Run image copies this one file as `server.py`), `gcp_signed_url.py` (`gcloud storage cp` + a time-limited signed URL through ONE `run(cmd)` seam; a missing gcloud returns a structured error with remediation), `gcp_cloud_run.py` (GENERATES `server.py` + `Dockerfile` + `deploy.sh` + a README under `.architect-team/publish/cloud-run/` and deploys NOTHING — the URL is a PATTERN until a human has run `deploy.sh`), `nominated_host.py` (scp into the user's own web root under their public base URL; expiry advisory until revoke). `tests/test_publish.py`.
- **`scripts/homepage/`** — **(v4.0.0 — proposal change 15)** `build.py` builds the one-page project homepage from the files that already exist, each source best-effort with an EXPLICIT missing state (`discover_sources` → `source_hashes` → `build`; `is_stale` compares the recorded hashes; a key whose name contains `token` / `secret` / `key` / `password` / `api` / `auth` is never rendered, and no other config value is either except the publish `mode` / `adapter` / `slug`; map freshness via a LAZY in-function import of `services/knowledge_server/freshness.py` — `unknowable` with the reason on import failure, never a fabricated `current`); `timings.py` answers "how long will it take?" from the ledger `hooks/timings_log.py` writes — `stats` (per lane / phase / agent: n, median, p90, mean by nearest rank, a trend of the last 5 runs vs the earlier ones — `insufficient`, never a fabricated direction; heartbeat lines excluded) and `estimate` (refuses below `min_n` 5 samples with "no basis yet"; every answer carries "history, not a promise"); `--also-user-level` merges the user mirror de-duplicated by content. `tests/test_homepage.py`.
- **`scripts/data_dictionary/sampling.py`** — **(v4.0.0 — proposal change 13)** the sample-first iteration engine under `skills/data-eng-pipeline/references/sample-first.md`: `plan` (a `sample-plan/v1` from a `data-dictionary/v1` entry — strata by partition / low-cardinality predicate columns else hash bucket, edge rows (NULLs, boundaries, duplicates, the exact rows a bug report named), group-preserving keys for aggregate-level changes, the smallest covering N (capped), the seed, the skip threshold, the sandbox location), `render_sql` for postgres | bigquery | snowflake | sqlite (deterministic hash-modulo stratified sampling + a UNION ALL of the edge selects + a group-preserving variant that samples DISTINCT group keys first), `diff_report` (per-stratum row counts + sorted-key sha256 checksums + numeric aggregate deltas), `should_sample`. Only the sqlite dialect is executed in tests. `--full-data` on `/architect-team:bug-fix` / `/architect-team:data-eng` opts out. `tests/test_sampling.py`.
- **`scripts/compliance/instruction_compliance.py`** — **(v3.31.0; v4.0.0 checks)** the deterministic instruction-surface lint (contract `docs/INSTRUCTION_COMPLIANCE_RUBRIC.md`; 132 in-scope files at v4.1.0 — every `SKILL.md`, agent, command, the two canonical `docs/` maps; zero findings suite-gated by `tests/test_instruction_compliance.py`). **v4.0.0** adds the referencing-mandate checks: `dead-pointer` (a backticked repo-relative `.md` / `.py` pointer that does not resolve on disk), `dead-heading` (a backticked heading literal cited for a pointer in the same sentence that is not EXACTLY a heading of that file — suffixes are not stripped), and `chapter-over-budget` (a `skills/*/references/*.md` chapter over the 5,000-token `bytes/4` re-attach budget); `scripts/compliance/pointer_allowlist.txt` is the ONLY suppression (each pre-existing dead cite classified with its reason; hits are reported as `pointer_allowlist_hits`, so a stale entry is visible). This map is in its scope.
- **`scripts/converge/`** — **(v4.1.0)** `record.py`, the deterministic half of the `ct6-converge` reviewer-convergence loop (`workflows/ct6-converge.js` is the orchestration half): `declare` appends gate `converge:<kind>` to `<workspace>/.architect-team/declared-gates.json` BEFORE the loop runs; the workflow's result carries a `ct6-converge/v1` `record` block; `record` writes it to `<workspace>/.architect-team/converge/<kind>-<ts>.json` and satisfies the gate ONLY when `converged` is true, every reviewer returned in the final round and every one of them said `ok` (anything less exits 1 and leaves the gate open, so the Lead runs the loop again — a human `maxRounds` can pause the loop, never satisfy the gate); `status` lists the converge gates and whether each is satisfied. The Stop arm `_audit_declared_gates` holds the run until the record exists — the hold `ralph-loop`'s Stop hook provided, enforced from disk rather than from a typed phrase. Stdlib-only; `tests/test_converge_gate.py`.
- **Other deterministic engines** (each named by its skill contract in `docs/CAPABILITY_INDEX.md` and rowed one line each in the directory tree above): `scripts/data_dictionary/` (`data_dictionary.py` / `annotations.py` / `jsonld_export.py`), `scripts/sql_mining/sql_mining.py`, `scripts/intuition_lifecycle/` (`engine.py` + `cli.py`), `scripts/playwright_suite/manifest.py`, `scripts/bauplan/` (`arming.py` + `dispatch_record.py`), `scripts/claude_md/claude_md_efficiency.py`, `scripts/mcp_design/output_contract.py`, `scripts/helpdesk/logit.py`, `scripts/token_compression/caveman.py`, `scripts/claude_design/claude_design_import.py`, `scripts/delivery/delivery_manifest.py`, `scripts/contract/interface_contract.py`, `scripts/phenotypes/phenotypes.py` — all stdlib-only, each with its own test file under `tests/`.

### Services — the CT6-6 server tier + the v3.49.0 knowledge server (26 modules)

> The CT6-6 service / server tier (v3.23.0-v3.29.0) + the v3.49.0 `knowledge_server/`, written separable per REPO-3 — a stdlib-only DESIGN + a runnable core + tests, **NOT live-deployed** (the live servers, ChromaDB, the Anthropic API, web scraping, Postgres, and the SEC-4 attestation / SMP-4 billing are adapters / operator-provided). `services/separation.py::check_separation()` (REPO-4) asserts every one of these 26 modules is import-clean (stdlib + in-repo only at module load). This is the SERVER counterpart to the in-repo CT6-6 skill tier (v3.17.0-v3.22.0); the CT6-6 program is COMPLETE as of v3.28.0. The v3.29.0 Librarian install is opt-in + a self-managed local daemon.

**`services/common/` — the shared substrate (v3.23.0):**

- **`services/common/ed25519.py`** — a pure-Python, stdlib-only RFC-8032 Ed25519 (keygen / sign / verify; no 3rd-party dep — cross-validated byte-for-byte vs libsodium + the RFC vectors). The signature primitive under the SEC triage handshake.
- **`services/common/handshake.py`** — signed submission envelopes binding every field (payload / nonce / ts / alg / attestation) + freshness + nonce-replay protection + a pluggable SEC-4 attestation hook (HMAC stub). SEC-1/2/3/5.
- **`services/common/bg_runtime.py`** — the BG-1…4 always-on runtime: a cron-like `Scheduler`, self-check, the per-OS boot/restart `install_descriptor` (systemd / launchd / Task-Scheduler, injection-guarded), and the log-ship interface.
- **`services/common/service_config.py`** — the same-Anthropic-key config model + an `LLMClient` adapter (the real Anthropic call is a documented boundary; `FakeLLMClient` for tests).

**`services/librarian/` — topic-research curation (v3.24.0; installable v3.29.0):**

- **`services/librarian/library_index.py`** — a stdlib `sqlite3` keyword/summary/concept-cloud reference index + the LIB-10 conceptual search (weighted concept×3 / keyword×2 / text×1 overlap over unicode-folded tokens — overlap, not semantic).
- **`services/librarian/extract.py`** — the LLM read→confirm-relevant→title/summary/strong-keywords/concept-cloud extraction with a string-aware JSON parse (`raw_decode`).
- **`services/librarian/librarian.py`** — the fetch→extract→index→metadata orchestration on the shared `bg_runtime` + the LIB-8 file-folder body store (path-safe filename).
- **`services/librarian/daemon.py`** — **(v3.29.0)** the daemon entry point (builds `Librarian` + `Scheduler` + `run_forever`) + `UrlSource(Source)`, a stdlib `urllib` topic→URL fetcher that skips a failing fetch gracefully. Installed via `scripts/setup/install_librarian.py`.

**`services/triage/` — the Evaluator / triage service (v3.25.0; EVAL-1…17 + SEC):**

- **`services/triage/issue.py`** — the normalized issue record + dedup fingerprint, REUSING the helpdesk `logit` privacy engine (off by default per EVAL-17).
- **`services/triage/evaluator.py`** — the EVAL-1 senior-architect log review + root-cause categorization (string-aware parse) + the EVAL-3 ~hourly `bg_runtime` task.
- **`services/triage/tally_queue.py`** — EVAL-4/10 dedup tally by fingerprint + backlog promotion of recurring issues.
- **`services/triage/triage.py`** — the EVAL-11/12 quarantine rule (an issue first seen on an old version may already be fixed by an intermediate release → hold + verify from the fixed version onward) + EVAL-6 resolution log + EVAL-7/13 recurrence + EVAL-5 two-stage review.
- **`services/triage/sink.py`** — the EVAL-2 GitHub-issue sink adapter (payload built; the real POST injected).
- **`services/triage/server.py`** — the SEC submission server verifying a signed Ed25519 envelope via `services/common/handshake.py`, then re-applying privacy before storing.

**`services/session_review/` — session-level review (v3.26.0; SR-1…3):**

- **`services/session_review/session_review.py`** — a session-level review agent (of Librarian design): reviews a full session via the shared LLM (SR-1; string-aware parse + robust boolean coercion), pushes a session summary (SR-2), and files ONLY the issues the agents could not solve on the first attempt (SR-3) as REUSED triage `issue` records through the triage `sink`. EVAL-17 default-off — under `off` it transmits nothing.

**`services/seeded_mempalace/` — authenticated seeded-MemPalace download (v3.27.0; SMP-1…5):**

- **`services/seeded_mempalace/bundle.py`** — the seeded-MemPalace bundle schema + a merge that refreshes the seeded sections while PRESERVING every user top-level key (SMP-3/5).
- **`services/seeded_mempalace/catalog.py`** — the SMP-4 phenotype catalog, REUSING the existing phenotype store; `gate_catalog` ships full records only for entitled phenotypes (the future purchase model).
- **`services/seeded_mempalace/client.py`** — the SMP-1/2 authenticated download (signs the request via the Ed25519 SEC handshake) + merge.
- **`services/seeded_mempalace/server.py`** — the SMP-2 server skeleton: verifies the handshake, resolves entitlements by the VERIFIED public key, gates the catalog.

**`services/knowledge_server/` — the standing repo-knowledge MCP server (v3.49.0; installable):**

- **`services/knowledge_server/server.py`** — the JSON-RPC 2.0-over-stdio loop (`initialize` / `tools/list` / `tools/call` hand-implemented — NO `mcp` SDK, no LLM anywhere) + the tool registry + `discover_sidecars` `--repo-root` auto-discovery of the conventional CT6 sidecars (`docs/data-dictionary.json`, `lineage-graph.json`, `docs/*_MAP.md`, `docs/data-annotations/`; explicit `--dictionary` / `--graph` / `--map` override/augment; at least one resolvable source REQUIRED else a clean nonzero exit — no silent empty serve).
- **`services/knowledge_server/adapter_base.py`** — the pluggable READ-ONLY `SourceAdapter` seam + `Tool`; a 6-tool sweep mutates no source sidecar (sha256 before == after).
- **`services/knowledge_server/freshness.py`** — `{verdict: current / stale / unknowable, basis: []}` on EVERY tool response (not just the status tools): map staleness via `hooks/lineage_graph.py::transitive_stale_nodes` + the `last_mapped` / `last_traced` stamps + `map_invalidated`; dictionary staleness via reference-file git-drift since `built_at`; DB-side currency honestly `unknowable` without a live connection — never a fabricated `current`, never a bare wall clock. **(v4.0.0)** gains a second consumer outside the server: `scripts/homepage/build.py` loads it lazily in-function (the same form `freshness.py` itself uses for `hooks/lineage_graph.py`) to stamp each map's freshness verdict on the project homepage — an import failure renders `unknowable` with the reason, never a fabricated `current`.
- **`services/knowledge_server/contracts.py`** — 8 closed per-tool output contracts via `scripts/mcp_design/output_contract.py` (CT6 eats its own dog food — a malformed response FAILS validation through the real dispatch).
- **`services/knowledge_server/dictionary_source.py`** — `DictionarySource` over `docs/data-dictionary.json` (+ `docs/data-annotations/`): `search_dictionary` / `get_table_details` / `find_relations` / `get_dictionary_status`; ranked search COMPOSES the Librarian's `LibraryIndex` via the daemon's sibling-import bootstrap (verified by module identity — NOT a fork); re-index rides a `bg_runtime.Scheduler` tick when the sidecar hash changes (bytes + mtime_ns).
- **`services/knowledge_server/map_source.py`** — `MapSource` over `lineage-graph.json` + the `*_MAP.md` frontmatter: `search_map` / `get_route_details` / `find_call_paths` / `get_map_status` — `find_call_paths` generalizes deng's `find_join_paths` over the lineage edge vocabulary (`calls` / `reads` / `writes` / `modifies` / `serves` / `originates` / `serves_route`), walking ONLY edges present in the graph (a poisoned or invented edge is refused). Installed via `scripts/setup/install_knowledge_server.py` + `/architect-team:knowledge-install`; "serving on this machine" only after a real `tools/call` — never "deployed".

**`services/` root — the separation manifest (v3.28.0; REPO-1…4 — completes the CT6-6 program):**

- **`services/separation.py`** — `SEPARATION_MANIFEST` (the open-core-vs-paid-repo plan + the adapter SEAMS the closed/paid pieces plug into — SEC-4 attestation, SMP-4 entitlement/billing) + `check_separation()` (the REPO-4 invariant: parses every `services/**/*.py` and asserts each is import-clean — stdlib + in-repo only at module load, recursing through try/if/with/class bodies but NOT function bodies).
- **`services/SEPARATION_MANIFEST.md`** — the human-readable separation plan + the seam table + the "how to separate out" procedure (the doc half of `separation.py`).
- **`services/README.md`** — the entry-point overview of the `services/` tier: what the CT6-6 server tier is (Librarian / Session-Review / Evaluator-Triage / seeded-MemPalace + the shared substrate), its counterpart-to-the-plugin-tier framing, and the explicit HONEST BOUNDARY (design + a stdlib-only runnable core + tests, NOT live-deployed — the cross-machine deployment / live triage server / ChromaDB / Anthropic API / scraping / Postgres are adapter boundaries the operator provides; nothing is "deployed / running in production").

### Config files

- **`.architect-team-notify.json`** (in a *target* project's repo root — NOT in this plugin) — the opt-in per-project email-notification config (v0.9.18) consumed by `scripts/notify/notify.py`: `provider` (`gmail`/`sendgrid`), `from_address`, optional `from_name`, the provider-settings object naming the secret env var, and a non-empty `recipients[]` (each with `email` + an `events[]` subscription list, or the `"all"` shorthand). Absent config => the notifier is a silent no-op.
- **`.architect-team-notify.example.json`** (this repo's root) — the documented, schema-valid template a project copies to enable notifications.
- **`.architect-team-deploy.json`** (target project root; gitignored) — the v3.44.0 human-authored dev → test-on-dev → prod opt-in read by `hooks/deploy_config.py` (`prod_deploy.enabled` / `always_merge_to_prod_on_complete` / `prod_branch` / `deploy_command`), extended in v4.0.0 with the test-selection pins `tests.always_run` / `tests.full_suite_at`; immutable to agents once it exists. **`.architect-team-deploy.example.json`** (this repo's root) is the schema template.
- **`.architect-team-publish.json`** (target project root; gitignored since v4.0.0) + the USER-level `~/.architect-team/publish.json` — the page-publishing opt-in read by `hooks/publish_config.py` (`mode` / `adapter` / `project_slug` / `token_ttl_hours` / `bind` / `gcp` / `nominated_host` / `base_url`; project wins key by key); written ONCE by the setup question, immutable to agents; a missing or broken file means `claude` mode. **`.architect-team-publish.example.json`** (this repo's root) is the documented template, security notes included.
- **`hooks/hooks.json`** — the plugin's hook wiring (10 events → 9 scripts; see the Hooks section). Global by design — not skill frontmatter.
- **`workflows/ct6-converge.js`** — **(v4.0.0)** the Workflow-tool script behind every reviewer-convergence gate: `args` = `kind` / `promise` / `artifacts` / `reviewerAgent` / `reviewerPrompt` / `fixerAgent` / `fixerPrompt` / `reviewers` (tier 3 ⇒ 3, tier 2 ⇒ 1) / `maxRounds` (HUMAN-set only; absent = no cap — the no-self-imposed-limits rule). Each round runs the reviewers in parallel, blind to each other, against a typed `REVIEW_SCHEMA` (`status: ok | deficient` + `deficiencies[{where, what, evidence}]`), briefs ONE fixer with every deficiency (`FIX_SCHEMA`), and loops until every reviewer returns `ok`; silence is never read as ok. Per-gate presets in `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/converge-workflows.md`. Replaces the `ralph-loop` plugin (81 mentions retired). **(v4.1.0)** the result additionally carries a `record` block (`schema: ct6-converge/v1`, `kind`, `promise`, `reviewers`, `converged`, `rounds`, `history`) that is handed to `scripts/converge/record.py`; the gate is declared BEFORE the workflow runs and the Stop audit holds the run until the record exists.
- **`scripts/compliance/pointer_allowlist.txt`** — **(v4.0.0)** the ONLY suppression for the lint's `dead-pointer` / `dead-heading` checks: one resolved repo-relative path per line (or `<path>#<heading literal>`), each pre-dating the checks and classified beside it (13 entries at v4.1.0: two per-codebase OUTPUT artifacts cited as bare `docs/` paths, one chapter path, and ten `<path>### <heading>` literals); the lint reports which entries were hit so a stale entry is visible.
- **`.gitignore`** — ignores the runtime state `.architect-team/`, the MemPalace store `.mempalace/` + `mempalace.yaml`, and the per-checkout human opt-ins `.architect-team-notify.json` / `.architect-team-deploy.json` / `.architect-team-publish.json` (their committed `*.example.json` templates are tracked).

### Tests (v4.1.0: 8932 PASS + 2 SKIPPED, 0 FAILED on linux / py3.13 across 277 test files; the v4.0.0 bracket was 8874 + 2 across 274)

> The per-version test breakdown below is the v2.2.0-era snapshot. **Live total as of v4.1.0 (quiet-holds-and-the-users-release): 8932 passing + 2 skipped, 0 failed across 277 top-level `tests/test_*.py` files** — the CHANGELOG top entry's count, bracketed under `docs/measurements/` as the release's closing act (`python scripts/measure/suite_measurement.py --label v4.1.0`); v4.1.0 adds `tests/test_completion_unlock.py`, `tests/test_converge_gate.py` and `tests/test_stop_hold_channel.py`. The v4.0.0 bracket was **8874 passing + 2 skipped, 0 failed across 274 files**, measured on linux / Python 3.13.18 and recorded at `docs/measurements/2026-11-09-v4.0.0-suite.json` (verdict `measured-clean`; the artifact's `machine_bound` note says five committed tests hard-require gitignored `.architect-team/` fixtures and one skips without the local `.architect-team-deploy.json`, so a fresh clone does not reproduce this count — the figure describes THIS checkout). **Two v4.0.0 conventions every test author must know:** (1) structural wording pins read the SURFACE an agent receives, never the raw file — `tests/helpers/surfaces.py` exposes `skill_surface(name)` (the `SKILL.md` plus every `references/**/*.md` chapter spliced in), `agent_surface(name)` (the agent file plus the `SKILL.md` of every skill its `skills:` frontmatter preloads — `ct6-agent-core` for all 39) and `command_surface(name)` (commands carry no chapters), every helper pure and rooted so a test that copies the plugin into a tmp dir passes `root=`; moving text into a chapter therefore never breaks a pin while a dead pointer fails the compliance lint; (2) fixture trees that contain test-shaped files (`tests/fixtures/test_index/`, `tests/fixtures/homepage/`) store them as `.tmpl` so pytest never collects them. New v4.0.0 test files: `tests/test_task_tools_floor.py`, `test_required_reads.py`, `test_deletion_guard.py`, `test_project_principles.py`, `test_test_index.py`, `test_sampling.py`, `test_publish.py`, `test_homepage.py`, `test_timings_log.py`, `test_decisions_ledger.py`, `test_instruction_budget.py` (the per-release itemization is in `CHANGELOG.md`). Prior bracket — **v3.65.0 (playwright-suite-intuition-lifecycle): 8205 passing + 6 skipped, 0 failed across 259 top-level files**, measured on Windows/win32 (PyYAML present) — the first win32-recorded bracket since the v3.60.0 counting doctrine landed; recorded under `docs/measurements/` as that release's closing act (the prior v3.64.0 darwin basis — 7829 + 16 — is recorded at `docs/measurements/2026-08-24-v3.64.0-suite.json`). The single failure the v3.63.0 artifact honestly recorded — `test_pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py::test_backslash_spelling_is_still_refused`, a cross-platform pin the measurement-immutability arm failed on POSIX — was CLOSED in v3.64.0 (D0: backslash spelling normalized in `_targets_measurement_artifact`), returning the darwin basis to zero failures. The 16 skips are 14 PyYAML-gated + 2 archived-coverage-map; a PyYAML-present host reports fewer. **Read the skip and failure counts as platform-dependent and the artifact's `environment` block as their qualifier** — the prior v3.56.0 figure below (7166 + 6, 241 files, Windows) went stale for seven releases precisely because it was carried as an unqualified number. Historic detail as of **v3.56.0 (turn-boundary-completion-lock — +142 tests across TWO new files, `tests/test_open_work.py` (the substrate) + `tests/test_completion_lock.py` (the wired arm, incl. the placement pins that fail if the lock is moved below any agent-written release path), plus additions to `tests/test_pipeline_completion_audit_continuation.py` + `tests/test_pretool_unilateral_override_guard.py`; the v3.55.4 +2 live in `tests/test_run_continuity.py` + `tests/test_ux_test_builder_skill.py`) is 7166 passing + 6 skipped, 0 failed** (241 test files by the top-level `tests/test_*.py` disk-count basis, `git ls-files`-anchored — the v3.53.0–v3.55.0 release notes carried "235", an undercount of this basis; pytest additionally collects 3 files from the two `tests/bug-fix-*/` replication dirs. The per-release file additions since v3.49.0 — the data-eng lane's `test_data_eng_entry_surfaces` + `test_data_eng_pipeline_skill` (v3.50.0), `test_sql_mining` (v3.51.0), `test_data_annotations` (v3.52.0), `test_usage_review` (v3.53.0), `test_jsonld_export` + `test_data_dictionary_usage` (v3.54.0), and `test_frontend_e2e_loop_exit` (v3.55.0) — are itemized per release in `CHANGELOG.md`), measured on **Windows** (PyYAML present) under BOTH the default cp1252 environment and `PYTHONUTF8=1` — zero-failure since v3.47.0 closed the two v3.42.x-era environmental failures (the `guidance_blocks.py` CRLF product bug + the LF-normalized skill-references byte pin). The macOS-without-PyYAML basis (5966 + 16 at v3.46.0, where the 16 skips were 14 PyYAML-gated + 2 archived-coverage-map cases) has not been re-measured since. The opt-in behavioral eval tier under `tests/evals/` is excluded from this default collection (gated behind `CT6_EVALS=1`). This is the current environment claim.
