the `exclude_current` flag flips inclusion of the cwd's worktree; non-architect-team branches are NEVER included regardless of merge state; `cleanup_merged_worktrees` removes the merged worktree's filesystem entry; `cleanup_merged_worktrees(dry_run=True)` returns the candidate list but leaves the filesystem untouched; end-to-end — create 2 worktrees, merge only one, call cleanup, assert only the merged one is gone). All 14 v1.2.0+v1.3.0 worktree-lifecycle cases exercise real `git init` + `git worktree add` subprocesses with no git mocks (same discipline as the v1.1.0 state-resolution tests). **v1.4.0 adds `tests/test_scope_discipline.py`** (35 tests parametrized across the 6 parity-implying verbs and the 3 pipeline bodies) — audits the canonical `## Scope discipline` section in `common-pipeline-conventions/SKILL.md` (exists exactly once, names the anti-pattern, contrasts with v0.9.36, lists each of the 6 verbs, documents visual + structural + behavioral parity, references the `AskUserQuestion` surfacing pattern, calls scope-narrowing a domain gate, forbids the documented deferral patterns); each of the 3 pipeline bodies references the canonical section and stamps with v1.4.0; `prompt-refiner` documents the `scope-fidelity` axis + grade-schema + domain-gate language; `proposal-refiner` Phase R2 documents the 6th axis + new weight; `bug-classifier` has the `## Action-verb interpretation (v1.4.0)` section + lists each verb + documents `unclear` routing; `system-architect` Master Review Audit references the scope-discipline check + has `scope_fidelity_finding` in the verdict schema + the Phase 2 brief documents the `Scope check`. Total now **1744 passing + 1 skipped** across **76 test files**. **v1.5.0 adds `tests/test_dispatch_banner.py`** (20 tests covering banner shapes / fallback reasons / pipeline-command structural assertions / status-command frontmatter + body / version pinning). **v1.6.0 adds `tests/test_teammate_git_discipline.py`** (265 parametrized + singleton tests across 6 forbidden ops × canonical section + 3 pipelines × 2 assertions + 27 agents × 4 per-agent assertions + 27 agents × 5 outside-section op audits). **v1.7.0 adds `tests/test_frontend_missing_api_discipline.py`** (26 parametrized + singleton tests across 4 anti-patterns × frontend agent body + canonical section + singletons for each layer's section-exists-once + SR origin-kind verbatim + routing + cross-layer consistency). **v1.8.0 adds `tests/test_agent_resume_discipline.py`** (42 tests covering: 10 `is_truncated` cases — positive + negative + parametrized rate-limit markers + case-insensitive; 10 `wrap_agent_result` cases — passthrough on well-formed, no-send-message detection, resume-invocation, merge-with-marker, max-attempts cap, max-attempts=1, early-stop on success, send-message-exception tolerance, extra-keys preservation, None-input tolerance; 5 `read_checkpoint` cases — absent / parsed / malformed / non-dict-payload / default-dir-resolution; 4 canonical-section structural tests; 3 per-agent fan-out tests; 3 parametrized pipeline-reference tests; 2 helper-surface tests — exports + stdlib-only audit). Total now **2098 passing + 1 skipped** across **80 test files**.

## 5. Data Flow (abridged)

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant Orch as architect-team-pipeline (orchestrator)
    participant Team as backend/frontend teammate
    participant Gate as review-gate-task.py
    participant Verif as verifier agents/teams
    participant Stop as pipeline-completion-audit.py

    User->>Orch: /architect-team <folder or plain-language requirement>
    Orch->>Orch: Phase -1 (map) -> Phase 1 (100% coverage gate)
    Orch->>Team: Phase 2 spawn (non-overlapping scope; each teammate preloads ct6-agent-core)
    Note over Team: builds against the SELECTED tests (scripts/test_index select --phase 3); deletion_guard.py classifies every rm / git clean; timings_log.py times the dispatch
    Team->>Gate: TaskUpdate(completed) + reviews/<id>.json
    Gate-->>Team: exit 0 allow / exit 2 block (v7 schema — 17 required self-review + VAO fields + 6 optional + independent_review; a tier-2 gate: producer + ONE paired adversary)
    Orch->>Verif: Phase 3/5 — task-reviewer, test-completeness, editability, visual-verification
    Verif-->>Orch: verdict JSON (pass / fail -> SR or re-engage)
    Note over Orch,Verif: tier-3 gates (map convergence, architecture approval, master review) run as workflows/ct6-converge.js, declared before and recorded after via scripts/converge/record.py
    Note over Orch: Phase 3b — open SRs auto-spawn fix teams
    Orch->>Orch: Phase 4-7 (reconcile, integrate, master review)
    Orch->>Stop: Phase 8 — the full suite ONCE, --check, commit as the recorded identity, then session Stop
    Stop-->>Orch: exit 0 + nothing (clean) / exit 0 + hook JSON hold (v4.1.0 Stop hook feedback: the worklist, the completion lock's open harness tasks / unresolved asks / turn-output rule; CT6_STOP_HOLD_MODE=block restores decision: block)
    User->>Stop: /architect-team:unlock <why> — only the USER releases a hold (completion_unlock.py release, Arm 5-gated to this turn)
    Orch->>User: final report + auto-commit/push
```

## 6. Conventions

**Skill frontmatter:** required `name` (matches dir) + `description` (≥ 20 chars). Quote the `description` (or avoid `: `) — an unquoted colon-space breaks the YAML parser. **v4.0.0:** `user-invocable: false` on the 57 internal skills (the `/` menu keeps `readme-styling` / `token-compression` / `claude-md-efficiency`; the description is still loaded for the model, so this does not shrink the always-loaded figure); `disable-model-invocation` is deliberately NOT set on the lane skills (the lane commands invoke them through the Skill tool). A heavy skill is a short `SKILL.md` + chapters under `references/` behind STOP pointers (heading + pointer + first paragraph) + an INDEX under `references/` whose Required-reads table says which chapters to Read before each phase; a chapter stays under the 5,000-token re-attach budget and never names another chapter as a prerequisite; `scripts/compliance/instruction_compliance.py` fails on a dead pointer or a mis-cited heading.

**Agent frontmatter:** 5 required keys — `name`, `description`, `tools` (from the valid tool set — `LS` / `NotebookRead` / `Task` retired v3.10.0; `TodoWrite` left every list in v4.0.0 in favour of `TaskList, TaskUpdate`), `model` (`fable`/`opus`/`sonnet`/`haiku`/`inherit`, per `tests/test_agents.py` `VALID_MODELS`), `color` — plus, since v4.0.0, a `skills:` list that names `ct6-agent-core` (the preload that carries the five canonical blocks; `scripts/setup/sync_agent_boilerplate.py --check` fails on a missing preload or a stray compiled block in the body). Model pattern: the SHIPPED state (v4.0.0) is the ROLE split — `fable` on the 15 judgment seats, `opus` on the 9 delivery / execution seats, `sonnet` on the 14 mechanical seats, `haiku` on `bug-classifier` (`scripts/setup/set_default_model.py --split role`, policy `role-split`; the full seat list is in the Agents section's Model note). Evolution: v3.32.0 pinned all 39 to uniform `fable`, eliminating the earlier opus-for-synthesis + sonnet-for-mechanical split; v3.43.0 put the 12 delivery + adversarial agents on `opus` (`--split delivery` still reproduces it); the uniform lever `--model <name>` remains the Opus fallback for a harness that predates the `fable` alias; v3.35.0 adds the availability-gated secondary role split — `--split codex` / `--auto` put the 21 development/code-checking/testing agents on `codex-5.6-sol` (the id joined `VALID_MODELS` in v3.35.0 so the sanctioned split state is VALID frontmatter). v3.40.0 renames the split's written id to the provider-neutral `ct6-secondary` (`VALID_MODELS` gains it alongside the legacy id; `--split secondary` canonical; the uniform lever refuses both aliases); v3.41.0 makes the split's written frontmatter id the spawn alias `claude-haiku-4-5`.

**Command frontmatter:** required `description`; optional `argument-hint`, `allowed-tools`.

**Map artifacts:** `<codebase>/docs/CODEBASE_MAP.md` (`last_mapped`), `ROUTE_MAP.md` (`last_routed`), `DESIGN_MAP.md` (`last_designed` + `design_baseline`), `<workspace>/docs/INTEGRATION_MAP.md` (`last_synthesized`).

**Runtime state (gitignored under `.architect-team/`):** `intake-state.json` (re-entry + `dev_loop_iterations` + `map_invalidated`), `reviews/<task-id>.json` (evidence), `teammates/<name>.json` (manifests), `handoffs/`, `solution-requirements/SR-*.json`, `diagnostic-research/`, `editability/`, `master-review/`, `documentation-currency/`, `failure-pathway/`, `test-completeness/`, `visual-fidelity/` (`capture/` + `analysis/` + `verification-verdict-*.json`), `runs/`, `escalation-pending.md` (the Stop-hook stand-down marker), `active-run.json` (the v3.30.0 run marker), `ask-ledger.json` + `declared-gates.json` (completion-lock + declared-gates ground truth), `git-identity.json` (main worktree; v3.67.0), `discipline-registry.json`, `inbox/<run-id>.jsonl`, `run-metrics/`, `frontend-e2e/`, `diagnostic-research/`, and the v4.0.0 additions `principles.json` (the register behind the rendered `PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md`), `decisions.jsonl`, `run-history/timings.jsonl` + `timings-open.json`, `test-index.json` + `test-witness.json` + `test-selection-*.json`, `trash/<run-id>/` (the deletion guard's soft-delete store), `publish/` (outbox, pages, the generated cloud-run bundle) and `homepage/index.html` (+ its build state), and the v4.1.0 `converge/<kind>-<ts>.json` (the recorded reviewer verdicts a `converge:<kind>` gate cites) + `releases/unlock-<ts>.json` (the user's hold-release records; agent-immutable) + `pipeline-stand-down.md`. MemPalace store at `<workspace>/.mempalace/palace`; the user-level `~/.architect-team/` holds the Librarian / knowledge-server / gateway installs, the publish registry + user-level publish config, and the optional timings mirror.

**Review-gate evidence schema (v7 — defined once in `hooks/review_evidence_schema.py`):** 17 required fields — the 12 base teammate self-review fields `task_id`, `spec_review`, `quality_review`, `real_not_stubbed`, `tests`, `demo_artifact`, `files_changed`, `reuse_compliance`, `visual_fidelity_review`, `test_completeness_review`, `integration_testing_review`, `ui_interaction_review` (v0.9.19) PLUS the 5 v2.0.0 Verified Agent Output fields `oracle_match_review` / `baseline_clean_review` / `no_fake_data_review` / `adversarial_review` / `skill_invocation_audit` — PLUS the 6 OPTIONAL fields in `OPTIONAL_VAO_FIELDS` (`interactions_honored_review` (v2.1.0) / `live_verification_review` (v2.2.0) / `appearance_scope_review` (v3.14.0) / `check_integrity_review` (v3.47.0) / `claim_instrument_binding_review` (v3.59.0) / `principles_review` (v4.0.0) — a passing `check_integrity_review` or `principles_review` MUST cite the Layer-3 verdict it derived from) — PLUS the required `independent_review` block (v0.9.13). Each `*_review` field takes `pass`/`n/a`/`fail` — `fail` blocks; `n/a` needs a `_note`. `ui_interaction_review` gates the genuineness of a shipped UI — every interactive element genuinely user-flow-tested, every page live not a placeholder, every displayed value correctly static or dynamically bound, or a confirmed stub. The `independent_review` block is the verdict of an independent `task-reviewer` agent: it carries `reviewer` / `verdict` / `spec_review` / `quality_review` / `real_not_stubbed` / `reuse_compliance` / `reviewed_at`, and `validate_evidence()` rejects it when `reviewer == teammate` — the producer cannot be its own checker.

## 7. Gotchas (cross-cutting)

- **Hook exit codes:** 2 blocks, 0 allows; never return 1 for an intentional block. A malformed hook *payload* fails open (exit 0); a malformed *evidence file* blocks. Two exceptions speak through hook JSON instead: `deletion_guard.py`, a permission-decision hook, ALWAYS exits 0 and decides through `hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision` (`allow` / `ask` / `deny`; its internal failure is an `ask`, never a crash and never a silent allow); and since v4.1.0 the Stop hook's HOLD is exit 0 + `hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext` (transcript `Stop hook feedback`, never a hook error on the user's screen) — `decision: block` only under `CT6_STOP_HOLD_MODE=block`. The evidence hooks (`review-gate-task.py`, `teammate-idle-check.py`) still block with exit 2.
- **The two evidence hooks must not drift.** They import one shared `review_evidence_schema.py`; before v0.9.9 they carried separate copies and `teammate-idle-check.py` had drifted to an 8-field schema. `test_cross_consistency.py` guards this.
- **Teams mode vs. subagents mode is decided ONCE at startup.** The pipeline calls `scripts/setup/teams_mode.py::is_teams_mode_available()` at intake and records the decision in `intake-state.json`. Every subsequent dispatch uses the recorded mode. The decision is NOT re-checked mid-run — a mid-run env-var change does not switch modes. Re-running the pipeline picks up the new state.
- **Lock-file hashes are scope-glob-derived; not run-id-derived.** Two leads claiming the same scope glob write to the same lock-file path; the `holder` field distinguishes them and the existing-lock's TTL governs queueing. A lead releases by lock id, not by scope.
- **Hook trigger split is internal.** `hooks.json` registers the new TaskCompleted / TeammateIdle triggers alongside the existing PostToolUse / SubagentStop ones; the hook scripts branch on payload shape via `_detect_trigger_mode` in the shared schema module. Adding a new hook script that handles both modes must use the same helper — not its own ad-hoc detection.
- **The `Stop` hook can block a session.** `pipeline-completion-audit.py` is deliberately conservative — it acts only on a real architect-team run (or an explicit ACTIVE `active-run.json`), stands down on a `.architect-team/escalation-pending.md` marker or a fresh `in-progress.md`, and fails open on any error. Since v3.30.0, `stop_hook_active` stands the hook down only for NON-engaged sessions; a session that engaged a pipeline skill gets the bounded continuation guard instead (progress => keep blocking, `CT6_MAX_NO_PROGRESS_STOPS` consecutive no-progress blocks => auto-escalate + allow). To finish an intentionally-parked run, write the escalation marker, run `run_continuity.py --mark-complete` (done) / `--stand-down` (explicit user opt-out), set `CT6_RUN_CONTINUITY_DISABLED=1`, or remove `.architect-team/`. Since v4.1.0 the hold is quiet — hook JSON on stdout, shown only as transcript feedback — and only the USER releases one: `/architect-team:unlock <why>` runs `hooks/completion_unlock.py release` under a durable record in `.architect-team/releases/`; Arm 5 of the skill gate refuses that verb outside the user's own command turn, so the agent finishes or reports a held turn and never releases it.
- **The orchestrator cannot be hooked mid-run.** Every phase discipline is trust-based Markdown; the `Stop` hook + the Phase 8 `--check` gate enforce only the *terminal* state.
- **A classification is not a verdict.** "Unchanged" / "untouched" from an intake recon answers *what changed*, not *what is design-compliant* — and during a design-baseline migration "unchanged" inverts to "drifted." Verification re-checks against the Oracle / the live app, never skips on a classification.
- **Visual fidelity is verified against the LIVE app.** The `visual-verification-team` renders the running app itself; a self-reported reconciliation does not gate the run.
- **No arbitrary wakeups.** The pipeline runs synchronously; `ScheduleWakeup` / `CronCreate` / timer tools are forbidden inside a pipeline phase (v0.9.2).
- **`$ARGUMENTS` does not propagate** from a command into the skill it invokes — `commands/architect-team.md` binds `$REQ_DIR` explicitly.
- **Scaffold-agent does not update `EXPECTED_AGENTS`** — `test_cross_consistency.py::test_no_unregistered_agents` catches an unregistered agent.
- **Plugin-cache vs. source-on-disk lag (operational reality, v0.9.28 note).** The Claude Code plugin cache lives at `~/.claude/plugins/cache/architect-team-marketplace/architect-team/<version>/`. When `/architect-team` or `/architect-team:bug-fix` is invoked, the harness loads the cached version's skill bodies — NOT the source-on-disk in the development repo. After a `git pull` (or after a dogfood `git push` to main), consumers must run `/plugin marketplace update` → `/plugin update architect-team` → `/reload-plugins` to pick up the new skills/agents/commands. The repo never validates "the cached skill matches the source" — it can't, by design. For dogfood runs: the orchestrator reads source-on-disk via the `Read` tool (so the LATEST skill bodies are honored during the build) but the harness's Skill-tool dispatch uses the cached version's body for the orchestrator's own behavior. This explains why some dogfood-shipped features only take effect on the NEXT consumer run after their release commit lands. Closes cohesion-review issue #10.
- **One-time v0.9.23 dogfood asymmetry (historical, v0.9.28 note).** v0.9.23 shipped the `doc-updater` agent, but the agent didn't yet exist in the cached plugin when its own Phase 8 doc-currency gate ran — the orchestrator typed the v0.9.23 doc updates manually as a transition step. From v0.9.24 onward, every Phase 8 / Phase B8 dispatches the agent automatically. Closes cohesion-review issue #6.
- **Two harness task-dir layouts exist (v4.0.0).** Claude Code writes `~/.claude/tasks/<full session id>/<n>.json` today; older builds wrote `session-<first 8>/`. `open_work.session_task_dir` resolves both (full id first) and `review-gate-task.py` uses it — never spell either layout by hand in a new consumer. And a Claude 5 session has NO Task tools unless `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` is set: setup writes it, the SessionStart hook warns, and a missing DEFAULT task root makes the completion lock advisory on that one source (it says so in the block text), while a missing REDIRECTED `CT6_TASKS_ROOT` still blocks.
- **A pointer is a contract.** Rule text lives in `references/` chapters behind STOP pointers; tests pin the SURFACE (`tests/helpers/surfaces.py`), so moving words into a chapter never breaks a pin — but a dead pointer or a mis-cited heading fails `scripts/compliance/instruction_compliance.py` (`dead-pointer` / `dead-heading`: cite headings EXACTLY as written, suffixes are not stripped; a bare chapter path in backticks resolves against the CITING file's skill dir, so from `docs/` write the full `skills/<name>/references/<chapter>.md`), and a chapter over 5,000 tokens is `chapter-over-budget`. `verify-required-reads` proves a chapter was OPENED before its phase, not that it was understood; the Stop arm reports a skipped read as a measured gap and blocks only under `CT6_REQUIRED_READS_BLOCKING=1`.
- **`user-invocable: false` hides a skill from the `/` menu — nothing more.** Its description is still loaded for the model, so the always-loaded figure did not fall in v4.0.0 (20,701 → 21,853 tokens with four new skills and three new commands); shrinking it means shortening descriptions. `disable-model-invocation` must NOT be set on a lane skill — the lane commands reach them through the Skill tool and the flag would break every lane. The hooks stay in `hooks/hooks.json` (global), not in skill frontmatter, because the completion lock must survive a session boundary where no skill has been invoked yet.
- **The reviewer count is the gate's, not the agent's.** Three independent reviewers + synthesis is the tier-3 shape for architecture approval, the Phase 7 master review and map convergence; every Phase 3 task gate is tier 2 (the producer + ONE paired adversary, a third verdict only on disagreement); classification and shape checks are tier 1 (a tool or a `prompt` hook). One editability / interaction reviewer at a Phase 3 / 5 slice gate, three at the Phase 7 sweep; one flow-explorer / flow-executor / visual-analyzer / reference-tracer per slice by default. Decide the tier from `skills/team-spawning-and-review-gates/references/review-tiers.md`, record it in the dispatch brief, declare the gate with `scripts/converge/record.py` BEFORE the loop, run the loop as `workflows/ct6-converge.js`, and record its verdicts after — the Stop audit holds the run until the record says every reviewer returned `ok`; never a prose "loop until converged", never a self-imposed round cap (`maxRounds` is the human's, and it can pause the loop but never satisfy the gate).
- **The full test suite runs ONCE, at Phase 8.** Phase 3 runs the selected set per slice (`python scripts/test_index/cli.py select --workspace . --phase 3 --baseline <sha>`), Phase 5 the selected integration tests + impacted flows + the human pins; a static test→file mapping is a hypothesis until the pytest witness upgrades it to `runtime-witnessed`, and an unmapped test is always selected. `tests.always_run` / `tests.full_suite_at` in `.architect-team-deploy.json` are human pins no agent may lower. (This reverses the v3.65.0 "ENTIRE dev suite at Phase 5" rule on purpose — the full run moved, it did not disappear.)
- **Human-owned files are immutable to agents.** `.architect-team-deploy.json`, `.architect-team-publish.json`, the rendered `PROJECT_PRINCIPLES.md` (change it through `scripts/principles/cli.py`), anything under `docs/measurements/`, the user's release records under `.architect-team/releases/` (v4.1.0), the ask-ledger and the harness task store: the override guard refuses every agent `Edit` / `Write` / `NotebookEdit`, unconditionally — and the deletion guard denies the six shared-state git operations inside an active run. A publish config that is missing or broken means `claude` mode (nothing listens, nothing is uploaded); `gcp-cloud-run` generates a deployable and deploys nothing; a private link's token sits in the URL and is said so at publish time.
- **Conditional is not optional-by-accident.** `superpowers` and `cartographer` (like `bauplan`) are reported and remediated by setup but never gate it, and a lane that finds one missing records `methodology: native` and follows `skills/common-pipeline-conventions/references/native-methodology.md` — it never aborts. `REQUIRED_PLUGINS` is empty by design; moving a plugin into it is a spec change, not a convenience edit.

## 8. Navigation Guide

- **Add a skill:** `skills/<name>/SKILL.md` (quote the description; `user-invocable: false` unless it belongs in the `/` menu) → add to `EXPECTED_SKILLS` in `tests/test_skills.py` → if it carries procedure longer than a stub, put each discipline WHOLE in `skills/<name>/references/<slug>.md` behind a STOP pointer + its first paragraph and declare the per-phase reads in `skills/<name>/references/INDEX.md` (under 5,000 tokens a chapter; depth constitution → index → chapter) → pin wording through `tests/helpers/surfaces.py` (`skill_surface`), never the raw file → regenerate `docs/CAPABILITY_INDEX.md` (`python scripts/docs_tooling/capability_index.py --write`) → `python scripts/compliance/instruction_compliance.py` must stay at zero findings. A pipeline-driving skill additionally registers in `_PIPELINE_SKILLS` / `RUN_DRIVING_SKILLS` and receives the compiled `principles` block from `scripts/setup/compile_skills.py`.
- **Add an agent:** `agents/<name>.md` (the 5 frontmatter keys + `skills:` naming `ct6-agent-core`; `tools:` with `TaskList, TaskUpdate`, never `TodoWrite`; NO compiled boilerplate block in the body) → classify its stem in `ROLE_SPLIT_MODELS` (`scripts/setup/set_default_model.py`; unclassified falls back to `fable`) and set `model:` to that tier → add to `EXPECTED_AGENTS` in `tests/test_agents.py` → `python scripts/setup/sync_agent_boilerplate.py --check` → regenerate the capability index. `scaffold-agent` produces the file but does not update `EXPECTED_AGENTS` (`test_cross_consistency.py::test_no_unregistered_agents` catches it).
- **Add a command:** `commands/<name>.md` → add to `EXPECTED_COMMANDS` in `tests/test_commands.py` → regenerate the capability index. A new LANE command also resolves the commit identity at its front door (v3.67.0), fires `cleanup_merged_worktrees()` first, and registers in `COMMAND_TO_SKILLS` (`hooks/skill_invocation_audit.py`).
- **Add a hook:** write `hooks/<name>.py` (read stdin JSON, exit 0/2 — or, for a permission-decision hook like `deletion_guard.py`, always exit 0 and emit `hookSpecificOutput`; fail open on its own errors unless the file it reads is harness-written ground truth, in which case block and NAME the file) → register in `hooks/hooks.json` (global — not skill frontmatter) → add `tests/test_<name>.py` + a wiring assertion in `tests/test_hooks_structure.py`. Its kill-switch is a `CT6_*_DISABLED` env var only the HUMAN sets.
- **Add a Layer-3 tool:** a `verify_<x>` function in a `hooks/vao/<family>.py` module (dual-form import; `{tool, valid, gaps, verdict_at}` output through `core.py`) → a `verify-<x>` subparser + dispatch arm in `hooks/vao_tools.py` and its name in `_REEXPORT_MAP` (`tests/test_vao_facade_reexports.py` identity-checks it) → cite it in the Layer-3 gate-invocation chapter and, if the Stop hook should enforce it, add an `_audit_<x>` arm in `hooks/pipeline-completion-audit.py` with its own kill-switch and an honest blocking / measured-gap posture.
- **Add a reference chapter:** `skills/<name>/references/<slug>.md`, whole, with the STOP-pointer stub (heading + pointer + first paragraph) left in `SKILL.md` → a row in that skill's INDEX naming the phase that must Read it → under 5,000 tokens (`python scripts/measure/instruction_budget.py --check`) → cite it from another file by its full `skills/<name>/references/<slug>.md` path (the lint resolves a bare chapter name against the citing file).
- **Touch the evidence schema:** edit `hooks/review_evidence_schema.py` ONLY — both evidence hooks import it. An OPTIONAL field joins `OPTIONAL_VAO_FIELDS` (never `REQUIRED_EVIDENCE_FIELDS`, which stays at 17) so older evidence files keep validating. Update `tests/test_review_gate_task.py` + `tests/test_teammate_idle_check.py` helpers in lockstep.
- **Run the tests:** the selected set while building (`python scripts/test_index/cli.py select --workspace . --phase 3 --baseline <sha>`), the whole suite once before a merge (`python -m pytest -q`; green under both Windows cp1252 and `PYTHONUTF8=1`); the expected pass / skip count is the CHANGELOG top entry's, backed by its `docs/measurements/` artifact.
- **Bump version & release (the order is a constraint, not a preference — v3.60.0, sharpened v3.61.0):** update `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` + `marketplace.json` (moving `tests/test_dispatch_banner.py`'s version pin in the same edit) → add the `## [x.y.z]` CHANGELOG entry (the file keeps the ten most recent releases; older entries move verbatim to `docs/CHANGELOG_ARCHIVE.md`) → refresh `README.md` per `skills/readme-styling` (one NEW-IN spotlight, never two) and append the release to `docs/RELEASE_HISTORY.md` (pinned by `tests/test_release_history.py`) → archive the OpenSpec change SECOND (the `git mv` changes the tree, so archiving after measuring stales the artifact) → commit until the tree is clean as the checkout's RECORDED identity (`python hooks/git_identity.py git -- commit …`, never ambient git config and never a named author) → `python scripts/measure/suite_measurement.py --label v<version>` as the LAST act, then commit the artifact → `python scripts/measure/instruction_budget.py --write` records the weight → close with `python scripts/docs_tooling/changelog_check.py --require-measurements` (exit 0 or the release is not backed; this repo has no CI and no pre-commit hook, so the releaser runs it — checklist-strength, backed at Stop by `_audit_release_backing`). Consumers update via `/plugin marketplace update` → `/plugin update` → `/reload-plugins`.

## 9. What's intentionally NOT here

Scope boundaries the project has deliberately chosen. Each is a decision, not an
oversight — recorded here so a future contributor extends the boundary on purpose
rather than by accident. Every entry carries the rationale and the condition that
would justify revisiting it.

- **A single harness — Claude Code native, not a cross-runtime abstraction.** The plugin targets Claude Code directly: its hook wiring, plugin manifest, slash-command surface, and Agent-Teams dispatch are expressed in that harness's own primitives rather than an intermediate portability layer. One harness lets every discipline be enforced by a real hook exit code instead of a lowest-common-denominator shim, and keeps the surface small enough to reason about end-to-end. *Revisit trigger:* a second first-class harness with an equivalent hook + agent-dispatch model becomes a supported target, or a maintained portability layer covers the exact enforcement points the hooks rely on.
- **No usage telemetry — local-first, phones home to nothing.** A run leaves its trace only in the gitignored local state tree and the user's own git history; there is no analytics beacon, no run counter, no remote event stream emitted from a pipeline run. A coding pipeline operating on a user's source has no business emitting usage data off the machine, and the absence removes an entire class of privacy and consent questions before they arise. *Revisit trigger:* an opt-in, consent-gated diagnostic channel is genuinely needed — the manual `logit` / server-tier triage path is the sanctioned, explicitly-consented alternative and already exists for exactly this.
- **A stdlib-only core — heavier runtimes stay behind adapter seams.** The Python surface (setup, hooks, engines, and the service-tier core) runs on the standard library alone; anything heavier — a vector store, an external model API, a network fetcher — is reached only through an injected adapter with an in-repo fallback, and the separability invariant is machine-checked. This keeps the core installable and testable on a bare interpreter, keeps the suite hermetic, and keeps every heavy dependency optional. *Revisit trigger:* a capability genuinely cannot be expressed against an adapter seam and must load a heavier dependency at import time in the CORE — as opposed to the separable service tier, which is permitted its own dependencies.
- **No embedded browser runtime — QA drives the real browser through the user-flow layer.** The plugin does not bundle or embed a browser engine of its own; visual and interaction verification drive an actual browser through the Playwright-based user-flow discipline against the live running app. Verifying against the app the user will actually run — rather than an in-process render the plugin controls — is the whole point of the visual-verification and interaction-completeness gates. *Revisit trigger:* a verification need arises that a live user-flow-driven browser provably cannot express, or the QA layer moves to a different live-app driver.
